Proved end to end against the fake panel, whose new per-action counter (`apiCalls`, reported by `/admin/faults`) shows **one `get_vod_info` and one `get_series_info` for two opens**, and **both episode shapes** (`episodesAsMap` on and off) read the same seasons and episodes.
- **`WatchProgress`** (`DbWatchProgress`): a save marks watched at ≥ 95 % of the player's duration and clears a dismissal; a length the player never learned never completes by position; Mark as unwatched forgets the row. Continue watching is worked out again on every change to the history, the catalogue, its details or the favorites: movies between a minute and 95 %, and per series its newest episode that counts (past a minute, or finished) — the one in progress, or the one after it, from its start when it had only a few seconds; a series whose last watched episode was its last has none; dismissing a series marks every episode's row, or an older one would take the newest's place.
- **Found: the short EPG's first answer never arrived** (a Phase 3 bug). `ShortEpgGuide` coalesced requests with `.whenComplete(() => _inFlight.remove(key))`, and `remove` returns the removed future — the very one `whenComplete` made — so it waited for itself forever. The answer still reached the screen late, because the fetch filled the cache before its future hung, and the next minute's refresh read the cache. Fixed with `removeWhere`, and the new repositories, which had copied the pattern, with it; `short_epg_guide_test.dart` fails on the old line (checked) and passes on the new.

### Images (step 3)
- **Our own cache** (decision 6; `lib/data/images/`): `ArtworkCache` keeps files in the platform cache folder (`AppPaths.artwork`, `~/.cache/<app id>/artwork` on Linux) named by two 64-bit FNV-1a hashes of the URL (no new dependency); a file is written as `.part` and renamed once whole; a sweep in an isolate deletes the least recently used (a read moves a file's modified time) down to 90 % of 500 MB, and `.part` files older than ten minutes, at launch and after every 50 MB written. `CachedArtwork` is the `ImageProvider` over it, keyed by URL; `ResizeImage` decodes at the width drawn times the pixel density; Flutter's `ImageCache` is capped at 150 MB (`capDecodedImages`, from `bootstrap()`). extended_image is gone from `pubspec.yaml` (ADR-002 amended), with three packages it brought.
- **Fetching:** six at a time, the newest request first; past 96 waiting, the oldest are dropped, so posters a grid scrolled away from never start (Flutter's `Image` already defers loading during a fling). A 404, a web page served as 200 (the first bytes must be a JPEG, PNG, GIF, WebP or BMP), an answer over 10 MB or 30 s is no picture and isn't asked for again for ten minutes; a cached file that no longer decodes is deleted and fetched again. Failures never quote the URL (`ArtworkUnavailable` carries a reason only; dart:io's errors can name the address).
  - **Departure:** the plan said a dropped request is one "for posters scrolled away"; which widgets still want a picture isn't visible to an `ImageProvider` (Flutter's `ImageCache` listens to every pending load itself), so the queue's length stands in for it. The grid's measurement (step 8) says whether that is enough.
- **Screens ask `artworkFor(context, url, width:)`**, which reads `ArtworkScope` (an inherited widget the app root fills from `artworkImagesProvider`); a widget test without it gets plain network images decoded at size. The six `NetworkImage`s (Live TV's rows and preview, the player's OSD, banner and channel panel, the Guide's rows, Settings → Guide and its picker) moved onto it.
- **`ArtworkImage`** (design component) draws the stand-in — the monogram, the poster's title tile — while a picture loads or when it fails, and cross-fades to the picture in `motion.fast`, the stand-in fading out so a transparent logo never shows the monogram through it. `ChannelLogo`, `PosterCard` and `LandscapeCard` use it.
- **Found:** every Flutter error became a toast, including the *silent* ones Flutter raises for a picture that failed with no widget left waiting for it — which a grid of 30,000 posters would do constantly. `ErrorReporter` now logs silent errors as warnings and toasts only the rest.

### The Movies and Series grids (step 4)
- **One screen for both** (`lib/features/vod/presentation/catalogue_screen.dart`, the canvas's `Movies`): "All movies · 27 movies" (18/700 and 12, as drawn), a 220 px filter and the Recently added / Name / Rating control (remembered per kind for the session); the chips — All, Favorites, the visible categories in the user's order, Uncategorized — in one row that scrolls sideways, one Tab stop, ↓ into the grid, and **More ▾** pinned at the right listing them all; the grid `TitleGrid`: columns from the width at 160 px and more (6 at 1280, 7 at 1440, 10 at 1920), 2:3 posters with the title and year · ★ rating under them, NEW for a week, a ★ mark top-right on a favorite (where the canvas puts Downloaded), the progress line on a movie in progress; the focused card's line adds the runtime once its details were fetched ("2024 · 1 h 46 min"; `MovieItem.runtime` joined from `movie_details`).
- **Windowed like Live TV:** a count, then pages of 120 read as they show, skeleton cards until they come. One Tab stop; the arrows move by card (a row beyond the screen is built so ↓ always has one), PageUp/PageDown by a screen with the focused row kept where it was, Home/End, F toggles the favorite, the menu key has Open and the favorite, Enter opens the title's page and Esc comes back to the card it left.
  - **Departure:** no typing into the filter from the grid, which the plan offered: F, and D later, are the grid's own keys. The filter is one Shift+Tab away.
- **States:** no source, skeletons, "Getting your movies…" during a sync, "Your provider doesn't offer movies", an empty category (with Show all movies), no favorites yet, no match (with Clear filter), an error with Retry.
- **The details routes** (`/movies/<source>/<key>`, `/series/<source>/<key>`) arrive with the grid, inside their branch; the shell draws no top bar there (`DesktopShell.immersive`, the canvas draws none). The page shows what the row knows — the back chip, the artwork, the poster, the title, year · ★ rating — and step 5 fills in the rest. `AppDetailsTokens` holds the canvas's measures, `movieTitle` (52/58) and `seriesTitle` (44/50) its titles.
- **Found: a list never showed a new star** (Live TV since Phase 3, and the Guide). Lists re-read their rows when the count stream fired, and a favorite, a rename or a watch mark leaves the count as it was — Riverpod passes no equal value on. They now listen to a revision that moves on every change (`channelRevisionProvider`; the grid's `TitleCount.revision`).
- **Found with it: a refresh tore the focused row down.** Lists refreshed by dropping every page, so the rows were skeletons for a frame and the row with the keyboard's focus was disposed — with the fix above, every F would have lost the focus (a second F went nowhere). Live TV, the Guide and the grid now read their pages again in place, the old rows staying until the new ones come, and the grid keys a card by its title's identity, not its data. Live TV's F reads the channel afresh before toggling, so the selection's stale copy can't add it twice.
- **Found: a focus request after a frame waited for a frame nobody asked for.** Home on a grid that didn't need to scroll scheduled its focus for "after the next frame", and none came until the mouse moved. The grid asks for the frame itself.

### Movie details and Series details (step 5)
- **Movie details** as the canvas draws it: the backdrop (or the poster blurred when there is none), the 240 × 360 poster, "MOVIE  /  DRAMA", the 52 px title, year · runtime · ★ · genres with FHD / 4K and 5.1 badges from the panel's probe and "✓ Watched" once watched, the plot (or "No description from your provider."), Director and Cast, then **Play** — or **Resume from 1:12:40** and **Start over** between a minute and 95 % (decision 3: this page is the resume prompt) — and the favorite; the progress line with "46 min left" under them. What the grid row knew shows at once; skeleton lines stand where the fetched parts go; a failed first fetch says why beside Retry.
- **Series details:** the left column (the poster, the 44 px title, year · N seasons · ★ · genre, the plot, **Play S1 · E1**, **Continue S2 · E4** or **Play again**, and the favorite) and on the right the season tabs with the episode count and the episodes: the 176 × 99 still with its progress line, E4 and the title, ✓ once watched, the runtime or the time left, the description on the focused row. Enter plays an episode from where it was left; its menu has Resume or Play, Start over, and Mark as watched / unwatched. The page opens on the season of the episode to continue, and the list's first Tab stop is that episode (`FocusPaneController.remember`, new). States: skeleton rows, no episodes yet (Retry), a failed first fetch (the reason, Retry).
- **`nextUpFor`** (pure, `next_up.dart`) picks the primary action by Continue watching's rule: the newest episode that counts (past a minute, or finished) is resumed when in progress, or its next played; nothing watched, the first; all watched, the first again.
- The page's data: `movieDetailsProvider` / `seriesDetailsProvider` keyed by source and key (a favorite toggled on the page doesn't start the fetch over), `watchMarkProvider` and `seriesMarksProvider` live from the history. F toggles the favorite, Esc goes back.
- **Play is wired to `VodLauncher`** (`lib/features/vod/domain/vod_launcher.dart`), which step 6 implements with the player; until then its provider does nothing, so **Play and Resume start nothing in the step 5 commit**. The widget tests check what each action asks the launcher for.
- `AppButtonSize.xl` (52 px) for the details pages' actions; the series page uses it too rather than the canvas's 50.
- **Found (tests only):** a widget test that writes to the database from `runAsync` while a page watches a drift stream deadlocks: the write waits on the page's stream, which only moves when the test pumps. The tests write before the page opens, or from the test's own zone.

### VOD in the full-screen player (step 6)
- **The engine:** `PlayerEngine.seek`, `PlayRequest.start` and the `PlayerDuration` / `PlayerPaused` events. media_kit's `Media(start:)` sets mpv's `start` in its load hook, so a resume is one open that begins there: 532 ms to the first frame 30 s into the fake panel's MP4. Pause and seek go straight to mpv (`pause`, `seek … absolute`) without waiting for initialization, like the engine's other calls: media_kit's own `play()`, `pause()` and `seek()` wait for the video controller's first texture. media_kit runs mpv with `keep-open`, so a file's end arrives as `eof-reached`, not as idle.
- **One coordinator (decision 1).** States carry a sealed `Playable` — `PlayableChannel`, `PlayableMovie`, `PlayableEpisode` (docs/01's `PlayableSource`; Phase 8 adds a file). `state.channel` is the live channel or null, so Live TV and the Guide read what they did. The guide scheduler's "busy" now reads the item, so any stream of the source being set up counts, a movie's too. New: `playVod(item, from:)`, `seek`, `setPaused`, `timelines` (`VodTimeline`: the position, the player's length else the provider's, what is buffered ahead, paused) and `PlaybackEnded`. The prober takes a source id.
- **A file's watchdog:**
  - The end of the file is finished, unless it came 10 s or more short of the player's length: then it was a drop, and the file reconnects where it was, with the URL built again. The rule uses the player's length only: the provider's is often wrong (the fake panel's is made up), and it would turn every end into a reconnect.
  - A stall or a failure also reconnects at the last position.
  - Paused is never a stall, and neither is buffering while paused.
  - The first frame gets at least 30 s, whatever the buffer preset: a resume on a panel without Range reads its way to the position.
- **Saving where it was left:** `WatchProgress.save` runs
  - every 10 s of playing (paused time doesn't count), on pause and after a seek;
  - on leaving — a stop, or another item starting;
  - on a failure;
  - at the end, at the file's length, so it counts as watched.
  Nothing is saved for a file that never showed a picture. The provider's length stands in for the save's duration while the player hasn't reported one.
- **The launcher:** `PlayerVodLauncher` starts the file on the coordinator, then pushes `/player` unless the player is already up (a next episode plays in place). `vodLauncherProvider` moved from `details_state.dart` to `lib/features/playback/presentation/vod_launch.dart`.
- **The player stops a file.** Leaving the player any way at all disposes it: Esc, a destination shortcut, the end. A file still playing then stops and saves. Live TV's route listener still stops only a live channel (`current`), so it can't cut a movie short; the Guide's does the same.
- **The player's VOD face** (the approved sketch; `vod_osd.dart`):
  - **Top:** the title and "S2 · E4 · Undertow"; FHD / 4K and 5.1 badges from the picture and the audio track playing (`pictureBadge` / `soundBadge`, moved to `vod_text.dart` and shared with Movie details); the clock.
  - **Bottom:** the seek bar between the elapsed and remaining time: `AppSlider` with its buffered range and bubble, plus a new `showBubble` for the keys. Under it Play/Pause, −10 and +10, then the live player's controls.
  - **The OSD stays up** while a file opens ("Preparing…" — the plan's answer to a slow resume), while paused or seeking, and while the card shows.
  - **Departure:** the seek bar is not a Tab stop (`ExcludeFocus`). ←/→ seek from anywhere in the player, and a focused Material slider would take the arrows itself, in 5 % steps, with no end to run one seek on. The mouse drags it.
  - **Departure:** −10 and +10 are text buttons; the icon set has no rewind or forward icon.
- **Keys:**
  - Space plays and pauses.
  - ←/→ seek 10 s and Shift+←/→ 60 s: the bar and the bubble move at once, and one seek runs once the keys rest 300 ms (held keys repeat).
  - Home goes to the start; M, A, S, I and F work as for live.
  - Esc saves, stops and goes back.
  - ↑/↓, PageUp/PageDown, Backspace and the digits do nothing.
- **`VodPlayerController`** (presentation) holds what the player does around a file, apart from the widgets: the pending seek, "Resumed from 24:10 · Home starts over" for 5 s, the next episode, and the card and end card. It is tested under fake time.
- **The next episode (decision 4):**
  - It is looked up when an episode starts (`SeriesRepository.episodeAfter`), with its own resume point.
  - At 20 s left the card counts down from 10, then plays it from that point. A pause holds the count; seeking back out of the last 20 s takes the card away.
  - Play now has the focus, and a bar under it runs down with the count. **Departure:** a bar, not the button's own fill, which `AppButton` doesn't have.
  - Esc cancels: the episode plays to its end, and the card comes back as Play next episode / Back to series.
  - After the last episode, the player goes to the series page. An episode that ends before its card was answered goes straight on.
  - **Edge:** the card plays the next episode at about 10 s left. For an episode under about 3 minutes, that is short of 95 %, so it isn't marked watched (CI's samples are 2 minutes; real episodes run 20 minutes and more). Not changed: marking it watched on Play now would race the coordinator's own save of the position.
- **The failure card:** Retry, Next episode (for an episode with one after it) and Details. The words say movie or episode; a 404 is "No longer available" and "This movie is no longer available from your provider." (docs/05).
- **Found: the full-screen player took Ctrl+2 for a channel number** (live since Phase 3, and now VOD), so the destination shortcuts didn't work from it. Keys with Ctrl, Alt or Meta now pass through to the app's shortcuts.
- **Found: starting the next episode, or leaving the player, from inside the coordinator's end-of-file event threw** ("Cannot fire new event": the state stream is synchronous). The controller acts on the end in a microtask.
- **Found (by a widget test):** the card's Play now column stretched in a row that gave it no width (a layout exception); it is sized to the button now (`IntrinsicWidth`).
- **A drop, on the real player:** the fake panel's `drop_after_bytes`, half-way through the file, cuts the connection. mpv and FFmpeg reconnect at that byte by themselves and the movie carries on, as the soak saw for live. The coordinator's reconnect, back where it was, is the fallback, proved with the fake engine.
- **CI:** the samples step also makes `vod_h264_aac_10min` and `vod_h264_ac3_10min`, at `VOD_SECONDS=120`.
- **Tests:**
  - Unit: the coordinator's file rules (20, on the fake engine under fake time), `VodPlayerController` (14), and the resolver's movie and episode URLs.
  - Widget: the player's VOD face (14) — Play; Esc saving, with Resume then offered on the page; Resume and Start over; the keys; the end; leaving another way; a 404; the card, its count, cancel and the end card; a failed episode's Next episode; Preparing.
  - Integration: `player_engine_vod_test.dart`, the real engine on a file (a start 30 s in, the length, pause, seek, the end); and `vod_player_test.dart`, the whole app against the fake panel in 72 s. It runs Play → Shift+→ → Esc → Resume from → "Resumed from" → Start over → a drop → the end, watched; then an episode's card counts into the next, Esc cancels it on that one, the end card shows, and Esc returns to the series page.
  - The fault suite, the Live TV walk and the Guide's Watch test still pass. **1,782 app tests** under `TZ=UTC`.

### Home (step 7)
- **The rows (decision 5)**, in docs/05's order, each only when it has something: Continue watching (every source), Favorite channels, Recently watched channels, Recently added movies and Recently added series (the browsed source). `HomeRepository` (`lib/features/home/`) streams each from the catalogue's own repositories, read again whenever a table behind it changes. So after the player, Continue watching has already moved. Recently watched channels leaves out hidden channels and ones the provider dropped.
- **The canvas's measures:** Continue watching 4 across with a 24 px gap (16:9 `LandscapeCard`s, "Movie · 46 min left", "S2 · E4 · 31 min left", "S2 · E5 · Up next"); channel tiles 5 across with 16 (`ChannelTile`, a new design component: 84 px, 44 px logo, the programme on now, a 3 px bar — the accent on the focused tile, grey on the others, as the canvas draws one of each); posters 8 across with 20 (the grid's `MovieCard` / `SeriesCard`, moved to `title_cards.dart` and shared). Each row is a rail that scrolls sideways.
  - **Canvas over the Phase 1 component:** `SectionHeader`'s title is the canvas's 20/28 bold (new token `sectionTitle`), and See all is the accent link without a chevron.
- **The first-run hero** (the approved sketch) shows until anything was watched on any source (a channel counts): WELCOME, Start watching, "3 channels, 15 movies and 1 series from Northwind TV." (a zero left out), **Open Live TV** first, then Browse movies (or Browse series, or nothing).
  - **Departure:** the app's gradient (`AppBackdrop`), not the newest movie's backdrop. That picture is only known once its details were fetched, and Home would have to fetch them itself to show it.
- **Keyboard:**
  - Each row is one Tab stop.
  - ←/→ move by card and stop at the row's right end; ← on the first card goes on to the nav rail.
  - ↑/↓ go to the nearest card, by position, in the row above or below — the "same column" when rows hold 4, 5 and 8 cards. ↓ from the hero goes to the first row.
  - Enter on a Continue card resumes at once (decision 3). On a channel it plays full screen, and back on Home the stream stops, as Live TV and the Guide stop theirs. On a poster it opens the title's page.
  - F toggles the favorite; the menu key has Remove from Continue watching, Go to series, and Add to / Remove from favorites.
- **See all** only where another screen lists the same thing: Favorite channels opens Live TV on Favorites; the two Recently added rows open Movies or Series, all titles, Recently added first.
  - **Decided:** a poster opens its page in its own branch (Movies or Series), so Esc there goes back to that grid, as the page's "‹ Movies" chip says. Ctrl+1 returns to Home as it was.
- **States:** no source (Add a source); skeleton rows; a first sync still under way ("Getting your catalogue…" over what has arrived); an error with Retry; and nothing to show after something was watched elsewhere (Open Live TV).
- **Found: Riverpod 3 pauses a hidden screen's providers,** so a screen coming back into view shows its old data for a frame. On Home, the shell's Ctrl+1 put the focus on the stale hero's button, and the fresh data then removed it: the focus went nowhere. Fixed on Home: the sections are keyed (a row appearing above another keeps its cards and their focus), and a hero leaving with the focus hands it to the first card. The widget test couldn't show this; the integration test did.
- **Tests:** the repository (5), Home's widget tests (18: the hero and its counts, Browse series, a first sync, the rows, Enter on each kind of card, F, the arrows, the menu, an episode's line, See all ×2, no source, loading, error and Retry, nothing to show, Ctrl+1 landing on the first card), goldens at first run and with history (1280×800, 1920×1080), and `home_continue_test.dart` on the real player: a movie left at 75 s is on Home with its progress, Ctrl+1 lands on its card, and Enter resumes it there. **1,812 app tests** under `TZ=UTC`.

### The phase exit (step 8)
- **The walk, keys only** (`integration_test/vod_walk_test.dart`, 45 s with the 10-minute samples): Home → Ctrl+4 → the movie's name typed into the grid's filter → Tab into the grid, → to its poster, Enter → Play → Shift+→ and → past a minute → Ctrl+1 straight from the player: Home, the file stopped, the movie in Continue watching with the focus on its card → Ctrl+5 → the series through the filter → Tab to its episodes (one Tab stop, on S1 · E1), ↓, Enter on S1 · E2 → Shift+→ and → to its last 25 s → the card counts → S1 · E3 plays → Esc to the series page → Ctrl+1: Home with the series first ("S1 · E3 · Up next") and the movie after it, the focus on the series → Enter plays S1 · E3 → Esc: Home, nothing playing, the panel's slot free. The test reads state only to check it and to count the seek keys to an episode's end. The three file-playing integration tests now share `support/panel_app.dart`; `Keys.typeIn` types into a field with no label of its own.
- **Found by the walk: Ctrl+1 put the focus on "See all Recently added movies",** not on the first Continue card, once Continue watching had appeared above rows that were built before it. The shell's "first control of the screen" was the first node in the focus tree, and the focus tree keeps nodes in the order they were attached, not where they are drawn. The first Ctrl+1 of the walk only worked through step 7's hand-off from the hero.
  - **Fixed:** `FocusPaneController.focusFirst()` takes the first control in reading order — top to bottom, then left to right, the order Tab uses — leaving out route scopes (a route's scope is in the list, as big as its screen) and anything not laid out yet. Positions are read once per control, not in every comparison.
  - **Found by the suite, from that fix: a jump to a details page landed on its "‹ Movies" chip** (Enter on a Home poster): the page's top-left control, and its only one while the title is read. Play's `autofocus`, arriving with the title, is then ignored: its scope already has a focused child. The old order had only worked because it picked a control of the grid under the page, in another scope. Fixed twice over:
    - the chip takes no focus while the page is loading;
    - after a jump the shell waits for the focus manager's next update (a microtask queued while the new screen was built) and leaves the focus where it is when the new screen took it itself — autofocus wins; otherwise the first control in reading order.
  - **Tests:** Home's widget tests gain Ctrl+1 onto a Continue watching row that appeared above the others, after the focus was left further down (it failed before: "See all Favorite channels"), and Enter on a poster opening its page on Play (it failed before: "Back to Movies"). The shell's own test of a destination shortcut still passes.
- **Measured: the cold start** (`test/app/cold_start_benchmark_test.dart`, `--tags benchmark --run-skipped`, after `flutter build linux --profile`). The app's own profile build is launched five times on one data folder holding the `large` catalogue synced (50,000 channels, 30,000 movies, 3,000 series), three movies in Continue watching, favorite and recently watched channels, and Home's pictures cached by an uncounted first launch. Each launch is timed from `Process.start` to the line Home now logs on its first frame with its rows (`LaunchMark`: `[startup] Home is on screen, N ms after main()`), by the line's own timestamp. **Median 575 ms, worst 622 ms** (budget 2.0 s); about 236 ms of it before Dart's `main()`, and Home on screen about 340 ms after `main()`. Real display, Wayland.
  - The launched app gets a D-Bus session address that leads nowhere. It can't reach the keyring: a sync there would prune the real app's secrets as orphans of the benchmark's database (`pruneOrphanedSecrets`).
- **Measured: the poster grid** (`integration_test/poster_grid_scroll_test.dart`, `flutter drive --profile` on the real display, three runs): the `large` catalogue's 30,000 movies, every poster through the app's own picture cache from the fake panel.
  - **Flings, the wheel, ↓, PageDown, End and Home: no frame over 16 ms** (the worst 7–15 ms; the UI isolate's longest pause 18–35 ms).
  - **Top to bottom by fast flings** (1.43 million pixels in 200 s, about 11,900 frames): **13–17 frames over 16 ms, the worst 20–25 ms**, the longest pause 35–39 ms. Not the budget's "no frame over 16 ms"; recorded in Known issues, with the Guide's PageDown.
  - **Memory after it: decoded pictures 150.0 MB** — the cap, 907 posters at 169 KB each, decoded at the size drawn (a 600 × 900 poster decoded whole is 2.1 MB) — and 26 in use on screen. The picture folder 450–492 MB after the fix below; the process grew 154–166 MB.
  - In debug mode (CI) the test runs the same kinds of scroll without the top-to-bottom one: 48 s under xvfb.
- **Found by the measurement: the mouse took the keyboard's focus out of the grid.** The grid builds only the cards near the screen, so a card scrolled away by the wheel or a fling is disposed, and its `Focus` lets go of the focus: the arrows, PageDown, Home and End then did nothing until a Tab back in. (Its PageDown frames counted 7 in 3.6 s.) Fixed (**decided**: the keyboard carries on from what is on screen):
  - while the focused card is gone, the grid holds the focus itself (a node that isn't a Tab stop);
  - when the view comes to rest, the focus goes to the card in the same column of the first row wholly on screen, and an arrow or Enter pressed before that lands there first;
  - PageUp, PageDown, Home and End count from the view when the focused card is off screen.
  - A card notices in `deactivate`, before its `Focus` is torn down.
- **Found: End on a large catalogue left the focus nowhere** (since step 4). It jumps to a page not read yet, so the last card isn't built when the focus is placed. The grid now holds the focus and hands it to that card when it is built.
- **Found: the picture folder ran to 524 MB** of its 500 MB cap. The sweep deletes down to 90 % once the folder is over the cap, but only ran after each 50 MB written. It now also runs as soon as the size the last sweep left, plus what was written since, passes the cap.
- **Tests:** the grid's widget tests gain a wheel scroll past the focused card (the focus on a card of the view in its column; ↓ and PageDown from there) and End onto a page not read yet; the cache's, a sweep started by the cap alone; `LaunchMark` logs once, and not at all unless `bootstrap()` made it.
- **Your panel's own VOD, run 2026-09-29 with your go-ahead** (`integration_test/real_provider_vod_test.dart`, behind the same `"play": true` switch as the live test, turned on for the run and back off; the masked report is `build/real_provider_run/vod_report.md`). The newest movie and the first episode of the newest series:
  - **Both Matroska (`.mkv`), H.264 1280 × 720 with AAC stereo**, decoded on VA-API; 1 h 28 min and 2 h 49 min long.
  - **Range is honoured:** 206 with `Content-Range`, at the start and in the middle, typed `video/x-matroska`. Its `Accept-Ranges` isn't the standard `bytes` but the file's span (`0-737001786`); mpv doesn't read it.
  - **The first picture after 901 ms** (the movie) **and 1,398 ms** (the episode).
  - **A seek's new request isn't refused on the one connection:** seeks to the middle and back to 10 % of each played on, with no reconnect and no failure in the 8 s after each. This answers the plan's open question as far as the player can tell. How long a seek took to play again wasn't measured: the check read the coordinator's position, which moves at once. The test now waits for playback 2 s past the point.
  - **The account read 1 of 1 connections in use before the run, during it, and after it** — still 1 some minutes later, with nothing of the app running. In Phase 3 it read 0 before and after. Something else held a connection, or was counted, and our streams were let in all the same.
  - What this settles: no fallback is needed for a panel that ignores Range, and "Preparing…" stays only as the safety net for a slow resume.
  - **Answered 2026-09-29:** another of the user's devices was connected during the run. It explains the count.


## ADR-013 · 2026-09-29 · Accepted 2026-09-29 (plan approved 2026-09-29; accepted at the phase exit) — Phase 6 search, favorites, names and hiding choices
**Decision:** the Phase 6 choices below, organized by step, as docs/plans/phase-6-search-and-polish.md proposed and the user approved on 2026-09-29 ("continue the work") — **the recommendation in all eight decisions and the three sketches**, with the departures recorded where they happen.

The eight, in short: **1** cleaned names stored (`channels.clean_name`, `quality`), filled by sync; a screen shows the rename, else the clean name, else the provider's. **2** what cleanup removes and keeps (below, with one departure). **3** search covers every source, the browsed one first, in the canvas's four groups, up to 5 rows each, prefix matches first. **4** Enter plays a channel or a programme on now, opens the Guide on an upcoming one, and a title's page for a movie or a series. **5** "Remind me" is not in this phase; **the user put it in Phase 9** (2026-09-29). **6** a `favorite_groups` table, per source. **7** the favorites' order and groups carry into Live TV (groups under Favorites in the categories pane) and Home. **8** a channel the user hid is gone everywhere; one in a hidden category stays only where it is a favorite; Settings → Categories gets a Hidden channels tab.

### Channel name cleanup (step 1)
- **`cleanChannelName`** (`lib/features/live_tv/domain/channel_names.dart`, pure) returns the name to show and a `ChannelQuality` (SD, HD, FHD, 4K). Its tag tables and scanners moved out of the guide's matcher into `channel_name_tags.dart`, which both use: the matcher's 144-line corpus did not move. The scanners now read either case (the matcher reads a lower-cased name, the cleaner the provider's capitals), and a leading tag may be a quality word (`4K |`, `(4K)`, `[HEVC]`), which the matcher then drops as it always did.
- **The rules:** entities decoded however often escaped; odd whitespace, control characters and U+FFFD read as spaces, runs collapsed; leading country and language tags dropped where a separator or brackets mark them, as the matcher reads them (`TV 5 Monde`, `ABC News` keep their first word); resolution tags at the end — plain, superscript (`ᴴᴰ`), bracketed (`(HD)`), two-word (`Full HD`) or as lines (`1080p` → FHD, `720p` → HD, `576i` → SD, `2160p` → 4K) — become the badge, the highest winning; `+1`, numbers, the provider's capitals and punctuation that is the name's (`Canal+`, `Nick Jr.`, `E!`) are kept; a name with nothing left but tags stays as the provider wrote it, with no badge. Cleaning a cleaned name changes nothing (a fuzz test checks 5,000 random names).
- **Departure from decision 2: technical tags stay in the name.** The plan removed codecs (`HEVC`, `H.265`), frame rates (`50fps`) and `Backup` with no badge. Real panels list one channel several times — `ESPN`, `ESPN HEVC`, `ESPN (Backup)` — and with those words gone the rows would read the same, with nothing to choose between them. So only what becomes the badge goes: `UK: Sky Sports F1 FHD HEVC` shows as **Sky Sports F1 HEVC** with FHD; `History (Backup)` stays as it is; a leading `[HEVC]` moves after the name. The matcher still drops them all. (Decided under "decide UX"; easy to reverse in `technicalWords`.)
- **Schema v7:** `channels.clean_name` and `quality` (provider-owned: in the channels DAO's `DoUpdate` list, so a re-sync rewrites them with the name; `display_name` stays the user's); `favorite_groups` (source, name, order, collapsed; removing the source removes them); `favorites.group_id` in place of the never-written `group_name` (a deleted group leaves its channels favorites, in no group). `favorites` is copied to change its column (`TableMigration`).
- **Search indexes the name shown:** `channels_fts` is now `(clean_name, display_name)`, rebuilt in the upgrade, so "uk" or "hd" never matches a channel by its tags. Before the fill reaches a row (below), only its rename is searchable.
- **Sync** cleans every channel in its isolate (`withCleanName`, Xtream and M3U alike); 50,000 names clean in about 175 ms (debug JIT).
- **The fill for catalogues synced before v7** (`ChannelNameFill`, `runChannelNameWork`): after launch, before the launch syncs, one query asks whether any channel lacks a cleaned name; if so, a guarded job names them 5,000 at a time in row id order, each batch one transaction, and never overwrites a row a sync wrote meanwhile (`WHERE name = ? AND clean_name IS NULL`). A stop between batches leaves whole batches only, and the next launch carries on. Rows show the provider's name until it reaches them.
- **Repositories:** `ChannelItem` gains `quality`; its `name`, the name sort and the text filter use the rename, else the cleaned name, else the provider's name (`COALESCE(display_name, clean_name, name)`), in Live TV, the Guide, Home and the player's panel, which all read `DbChannelRepository`, and in Settings → Guide.
- **Tests:** the corpus (`test_fixtures/channel_names/names.tsv`, 111 names: every docs/05 pattern, the plan's additions, and names that must keep their words), odd names (empty, blank, tags only, entities escaped twice, U+FFFD, lone surrogates, emoji, Arabic, Cyrillic, Greek, 500 characters, unbalanced brackets) and the fuzz; the migration from a populated v6 database and the index after it; the DAO (a re-sync rewrites the name and badge and keeps the rename); both kinds of sync; the fill (batches, names already there, odd names, a stop between batches three times over, the service's single run and its stop); the repository (the name order, the sort ignoring tags, the filter on the name shown).
- **Measured:** the fill names 50,000 channels in **1.2 s** (10 batches of about 120 ms, each holding the database while it commits; `--tags benchmark --run-skipped test/data/sync/channel_name_work_test.dart`, file database, debug JIT).

### Clean names and badges on screen (step 2)
- **The badge as the canvas draws it,** not the plan's outlined `AppBadge`: a small grey tag after the name (`ChannelBadge`, `surface3`, 11 px bold `textSecondary`), stepping up to the border colour on a raised row (hovered, focused, pressed, selected) so it doesn't vanish into the row. `ChannelRow` takes it as `quality`; Live TV's rows and the player's channel panel pass the channel's. Only the canvas's Live TV and Favorites rows have one: the preview, the OSD, the Guide's column, Home's tiles and Settings → Guide show the cleaned name without a badge.
- **Everywhere else the name appears** already showed the cleaned name from step 1: each reads `DbChannelRepository` (or, for Settings → Guide, the same `COALESCE`).
- **Rename…** opens with the name shown; under the field, "Provider's name: UK: Arena Sports 1 FHD" whenever the provider wrote something else; **Use provider's name** only once the channel is renamed, and it goes back to the cleaned name with its badge. `ChannelItem.providerName` is now the provider's own name whenever it differs from the name shown (not only after a rename), with `isRenamed` beside it.
  - **Decided:** saving the name unchanged on a channel not renamed stores nothing. Storing it would pin today's cleaned name as a rename, and the provider's later names would stop showing.
- **Tests:** the component (the badge after the name, raised on the selected row); Live TV's rows (the canvas's names and badges from panel-shaped names, no tag anywhere on screen); the dialog (the provider's name under the field, Save unchanged pins nothing, a rename keeps the badge, Use provider's name). The Live TV fixture's names are now panel-shaped (`UK: Arena Sports 1 FHD`, `|UK| Arena Sports 2 HD`, `Velocity Motors ᶠᴴᴰ`) and cleaned as sync cleans them. Goldens re-recorded and looked at: Live TV at both sizes, the component sheet's channel rows. The Guide and Home goldens didn't move (their fixtures have untagged names). **1,963 app tests** under `TZ=UTC`.

### Search: the query (step 3)
- **`SearchRepository`** (`lib/features/search/domain/search.dart`; `DbSearchRepository` in `data/`, `searchRepositoryProvider`): `search(text, now:, preferredSourceId:)` gives the four groups — `ChannelHit`, `ProgrammeHit`, `MovieHit`, `SeriesHit`, each with its source's name and its category's — up to 5 each with `hasMore`, the number of hidden channels that match (counted only when no visible one does), and how many sources there are; and the recent searches (the last 8, newest first, once each whatever the case, in the settings table under `search.recent`; anything stored that isn't a list of text reads as none).
- **The words** (`searchWords`, pure): runs of letters, marks and digits in any script, at most 8 of 64 code points; each becomes a quoted prefix term, all required. Quotes, `*`, `-`, parentheses, `NEAR(`, emoji and lone surrogates are only separators, so no text reaches FTS as syntax (a fuzz test sends 300 mixes).
- **The order, as decision 3:** in every group the browsed source first. Channels: favorites, then names that start with the text, then a word that does, then the index's rank, then the name. Movies and series the same without the favorites step. Programmes: by start, which puts what is on now first.
  - **Decided:** among channels, favorites come before a closer name: the plan lists both rules without saying which wins, and a favorite is the more specific wish.
  - Two names equally close go by the index's rank (BM25), which puts the shorter name first ("Arena Starred" before "Arena of Kings" for "arena").
- **Visible** is decision 8's rule in SQL, the same as Live TV's: a channel hidden itself never shows; one in a hidden category only as a favorite; movies and series follow their category the same way. A programme shows only on a visible channel, and an HD/SD pair on one guide id is one hit, on the favorite of the two, else the one the provider listed first.
- **Measured, and changed:** the first version sorted every programme that matched, and a short word matches much of a guide — "t" 276,712 of 611,328 programmes, "the" 203,379. **One to three characters took 0.7–5.2 s**, all in the programme query (channels 8 ms, movies 22 ms at worst). The database isolate would have been blocked that long for every other query. Now:
  - **The guide's swap inserts programmes by start time** (`ORDER BY start_utc, epg_channel_id`; about 0.7 s more for 600,000 rows in a 22 s import), so a source's row ids follow its schedule — ids only grow (`AUTOINCREMENT`).
  - **Each source's programmes are read in row id order** from the first programme on now (the lowest id of each matched guide channel's latest start; about 6 ms, remembered for five minutes — an older answer is still a valid lower bound, only a little further back) **and the read stops at 6.** Programmes on now all started before anything upcoming, so row id order is on-now-first, then by start.
  - **One-letter words don't reach the programme index:** they only narrow what the longer words found (a word in the title or subtitle starting with that letter), and a text with no longer word finds no programme. Channels, movies and series answer from the first letter.
  - **The result** (`--tags benchmark --run-skipped test/features/search/search_benchmark_test.dart`: the `large` catalogue synced from the fake panel in its own process, 611,328 programmes over 8 days, a file database, 55 texts of 1–20 characters, three rounds): **median 6.6 ms, p95 24 ms, worst 31 ms** ("t"), against the proposed budget of **50 ms per query**; the UI isolate's longest gap 4 ms. By length: one character 26–31 ms, two 15–24, three 12–21, four and more under 14.
  - **Transition:** a guide imported before this build keeps the provider's order until its next import (at most a day, the scheduler's policy). Until then, programme hits are still all not finished and visible, but not necessarily the soonest.
- **Tests:** 22 — every group, each ordering rule, the hiding rules, two sources, the HD/SD pair and its favorite, title and subtitle but not description, one-letter words, a programme 20 hours long still on now, text with no word, the fuzz, LIKE characters, recent searches; the swap's start order; the words (scripts, syntax as separators, the limits, a cut never splitting a surrogate pair). **1,985 app tests** under `TZ=UTC`.

### Search: the overlay (step 4)
- **As the canvas draws it** (`search_overlay.dart`): the 64 px query bar with "6 results" and the Esc keycap; the headings CHANNELS, ON TV NOW & UPCOMING, MOVIES, SERIES; 52 px rows with a 56 px picture slot (a 36 px logo, a 30 × 44 poster); the typed words in bold where they start a word of the name, found with the index's folding (case, Latin accents, superscripts; `highlightRanges`); the cursor's row raised, ringed, with an Enter keycap; LIVE on a programme on now; the footer of keys and "Recent: …". The panel grows to the window's height, then its results scroll. Its measures are tokens now (`AppSearchTokens`, `text.searchQuery`, `text.searchHeading`): the Phase 1 frame had them as literals (hard rule 9).
- **The lines:** a channel "118 · Evening Bulletin, until 8:30 PM" (the guide's now, warmed for the rows shown), adding its category when two channels in the group share a name (decision 2) and its source when there are several; a programme "Courtside · ends 10:55 PM" or "Blue Water · Tomorrow, 9:30 PM", its episode's title after a colon; a movie "2024 · Drama · Resume at 1:12:40" (the category until its genre is known); a series "Series · 3 seasons · Crime".
- **The keyboard stays in the field** (`SearchSession` holds the text, the last results — kept while a newer search runs, so rows don't blink — and the recent searches; a search runs 150 ms after the last key): ↑/↓, PageUp/PageDown, Tab and Shift+Tab by group, Enter opens (the field's submit, as the platform sends it), the Menu key or Shift+F10 opens the row's menu, Esc closes. With nothing to jump between (the recent searches, no results), Tab moves on as usual, to Clear or the empty state's button.
- **What opening does** (decision 4; search closes first, and the text goes into the recent searches): a channel or a programme on now plays full screen, zapping through its source's channels; Esc comes back to the screen search was opened from, and the stream stops there, as on Home. An upcoming programme opens the Guide on it: every channel listed, the cursor on its row and time, its sheet open (`GuideProgrammeRequest`, `GuideGridController.showProgramme`). A movie or a series opens its page in its own branch. **Show all in Live TV / Movies / Series** opens the screen on every channel or title, the text in its filter. A result from another source switches the browsed source where the screen it opens needs it (the Guide, Live TV).
  - **Decided:** no "Show all" after programmes: no screen lists the guide by text, and the five soonest are the ones that matter.
  - **Decided:** the channel menu's "Show in Live TV" opens Live TV on every channel filtered by the channel's name, with it in the preview — Live TV has no "scroll to this channel", and the filter gets the same place in one step. Its "Hide channel" searches again at once; the Undo toast comes with step 7.
  - **Decided:** "Show in Settings" under no results opens Settings → Categories until step 7 adds the Hidden channels tab.
- **States** (sketch C): the recent searches (Enter searches again, Delete removes one, Clear) or "Channels, what's on now and next, movies and series, from every source."; skeleton rows for the first answer; no results, with "2 hidden channels match." and Show in Settings; a failed search with Retry; no source with Add a source; a first sync still running ("Your catalogue is still arriving: results fill in as it does."). Offline changes nothing: everything search reads is on this computer.
- **Found on the way:**
  - **Live TV's filter field and the grids' showed only what was typed.** The field now follows its screen's query, so "Show all" fills it — and a category chip that clears the query clears the field too, where it used to keep the old text over an unfiltered list.
  - **A programme's sheet opened in the frame that moved the grid** tripped a semantics assertion in the widget tests (the tree half built when the sheet's route arrived). It opens a frame later.
  - Controllers that read Riverpod lazily (`late final … = ref.read(…)`) were first read in `dispose` on a screen with no source, where Riverpod refuses reads; they read in `initState` now.
  - A poster thumbnail too small for its title shows the stand-in's gradient alone (`PosterArtwork(showTitle: false)`), as the canvas draws it.

- **Tests:** the overlay (16: every state, the groups, their lines and Show all, the bold words, the cursor through the groups and the keyboard staying in the field, Enter on a channel, an upcoming programme and a movie, Show all into the grid, the menu, the recent searches, the Guide request taken once), the lines and bold ranges (10), the Guide opening on a requested programme (its sheet, then Enter on the same cell), goldens (results at 1280 × 800 and 1920 × 1080, the recent searches, no results — looked at against the canvas), and **`integration_test/search_keyboard_test.dart` on the `large` catalogue, keys only (exit criterion 1): Ctrl+K → a movie's name → Tab → Enter opens its page; Esc; `/` → a channel → Enter plays it → Esc back to Movies, the stream stopped; Ctrl+K → a word → ↓ to Show all in Movies → the grid filtered by it; Ctrl+K → the three searches in the recent list** (25 s under xvfb).

### Favorites: order and groups (step 5)
- **The order** (`FavoritesDao`, `FavoritesRepository` / `DbFavoritesRepository` in the new `favorites` feature): a favorite's `sort_order` is its place in its group, or among those in none; a group's, its place among the source's groups. F adds at the end of the ones in no group. A move numbers the list it lands in again from 0; the list it left keeps its order. Favorites from before v7, with no place, come after the placed ones by when they were added. Deleting a group puts its channels at the end of the ones in no group, in their order. Moving a channel that isn't a favorite into a group makes it one.
- **Every favorites list reads that order** (decision 7): `ChannelSort.number` on `FavoriteChannels` (the groups in their order, then the favorites in no group) and on the new `FavoriteGroupChannels` is the user's order, in `DbChannelRepository`, so Live TV's Favorites, a group's list, the player's ↑/↓ from either, and Home's Favorite channels all follow it. A-Z still sorts by name. `ChannelItem.favoriteGroupId` says a channel's group.
- **Live TV:** each group is listed under Favorites in the categories pane (sketch A), with its count of channels that show, as a list of its own — its title the group's name, its empty state "No channels in this group yet". On a favorites list the sort's first option reads **Order**, not No. The channel menu gains **Add to group…**: the groups (its own ticked), No group, and New group… (a name, then the group with the channel in it). The app's menus have no submenus, so the plan's "Add to group ▸" is a second menu.
- **Exit criterion 2, proved against the fake panel** (`favorites_resync_test.dart`): five favorites, two groups, moves in and out and a group moved first; the same order and groups after the database is closed and opened again, and after a re-sync that gives every channel a new row id (the channels deleted, then synced back).
- **Tests:** the repository (6: F at the end, moves within and between groups, a channel moved in becoming a favorite, the groups' order, counts without hidden channels, collapse, rename, delete; favorites from before v7; the source's removal), the restart and re-sync, Live TV (the groups under Favorites in the pane, their lists in order, "Order"; Add to group… → New group…), Home's row in the user's order. The Live TV keyboard walk still passes. **2,031 app tests** under `TZ=UTC`.

### The Favorites screen (step 6)
- **As the canvas draws it** (`favorites_screen.dart`): the tabs Channels · Movies · Series with their counts, underlined when chosen; "Drag to reorder" and **New group** on Channels; in the panel, each group's header (the fold chevron, NAME, its count, Rename) and its channels, then **UNGROUPED** once any group exists; 60 px rows — the grip, number, logo, name and quality badge, what's on with its bar, the time left in its 96 px column, the star — and at the panel's foot "Press F on any channel, movie, or series to add it here or remove it." `ChannelRow` gained the grip slot (`leading`) and the time-left column (`timeLeft`).
- **One flat list, one model** (`favorite_layout.dart`, pure): headers and rows in one list, `favoriteEntries` builds it, `keyboardMove` says where Alt+↑/↓ takes a channel (along its group, past the edge into the next group's first place or the one before's last, the favorites in no group after every group) and `dropPlace` where a drag lands (the group of the nearest header above; above every header, the first group). Both go through the repository's one move. A move into a folded group unfolds it, so the focus can follow.
- **Keyboard** (plan): the list is one Tab stop, ↑/↓ inside; Enter plays full screen zapping through the favorites, and the stream stops back on Favorites (no picture here); **F removes, with an Undo toast** that puts the channel back in its group and place; Alt+↑/↓ moves a channel, or a group from its header; on a header Enter folds and unfolds, ← folds, → unfolds; the menu key: a channel — Watch, Move to group…, Rename…, Hide channel (with Undo), Remove from favorites; a group — Rename…, Move up, Move down, Delete group. The mouse drags a channel by its grip (`ReorderableListView`, the lifted row raised with a shadow).
  - **Departure:** the canvas's accent line where a dragged row will land is Flutter's own gap for now: the reorderable list draws the space it will take.
  - **Decided:** deleting a group asks nothing: its channels stay favorites, at the end of the ones in no group.
- **Movies and Series tabs:** the grid's body is now a widget of its own (`CatalogueGrid`, extracted from the Movies and Series screens), showing the favorite titles; Enter opens a page in its own branch, as Home's posters do; F removes. Empty: "No favorite movies yet" with **Open Movies**.
- **States:** no source (Add a source), skeleton rows, no favorite channel yet (**Open Live TV**), an error with Retry.
- **Toasts can carry an action now** (`AppNotice.actionLabel` / `onAction`, passed to the shell's toast), for Undo.
- Shared on the way: the channel Rename dialog moved to `channel_rename_dialog.dart` (`renameChannel`), and New group's name dialog is `showGroupNameDialog`.
- **Tests:** the model (10), the screen (9: no source, empty, the canvas's parts, Alt+↑/↓ over a group's edge and back, F and Undo, a header folded, unfolded and deleted from its menu, New group, Enter playing, the Movies tab's empty state), goldens at 1280 × 800 and 1920 × 1080 (looked at: a group with three channels and their programmes, an empty group, Ungrouped). Three shell tests that used Favorites as "a placeholder" now use the Library, and a Live TV test that covered Live TV with Favorites (which looks up its own rows' guide) now covers it with the Library. **2,054 app tests** under `TZ=UTC`.

### Hide, unhide and reorder, everywhere (step 7, 2026-09-29)
- **One channel menu** (`channel_menu.dart`, `showChannelMenu`): Watch, the favorite, Add to group… (Move to group… once in one), Rename…, Hide channel — in Live TV, the Guide (the menu key on the grid, by the cursor row's channel column), Home's tiles, Favorites and Search (which adds "Show in Live TV"). Callers can replace the favorite toggle (Live TV keeps its preview in step; Favorites' remove has Undo) and hear changes (search runs again).
- **Hiding says so, with Undo** (`hideChannel`: "Channel hidden · Undo"); a category hidden from Live TV's pane likewise ("Category hidden · Undo").
- **Live TV:** the categories pane gets its item menu — Hide category, Rename… (the Categories manager's dialog, now `askCategoryName`), Move up, Move down — and Alt+↑/↓, moving in the order of all the kind's categories as the manager does (items keyed, so the focus follows); the channel menu has **Show hidden channels** / Don't show…, and a hidden channel shown is dimmed and marked **Hidden** (`ChannelRow.hidden`); the pane's foot reads "3 categories · 2 channels hidden · Manage", and Manage opens the Hidden channels tab when only channels are hidden.
  - **Decided:** "Hidden" is a small tag like the quality badge, not an eye-off icon: the icon set has no eye-off.
- **Settings → Categories → Hidden channels** (sketch B; `hidden_channels.dart`, the `HiddenChannels` list filter): a tab beside Live TV · Movies · Series, shown once a channel is hidden, with its count; the filter, Show all, rows of number, logo, name, category and Show; one Tab stop, Enter shows the channel and the focus goes to the next row; the line "Hidden channels are left out of Live TV, the Guide, Search and Home. They stay hidden after a re-sync." `HiddenChannelsRequest` / `openHiddenChannels` open it from Live TV's Manage and search's "Show in Settings".
- **Live TV's list keeps the keyboard when the mouse scrolls the focused row away** (Known issues; the poster grid's fix): a row going away with the focus says so in `deactivate`; the list holds the focus in a node of its own (never a Tab stop) while the view moves, then gives it to the first row wholly on screen; an arrow, PageUp/PageDown or Enter pressed meanwhile lands there first. The holder answers only its own keys (the rows' pass by it on their way up — found by the existing tests).
- **The Categories manager's list is one Tab stop** (Known issues since Phase 2): a `FocusPane(tabStop: true)`, arrows inside; a row's Rename button is the mouse's (out of the Tab order), and the menu key has Rename…, Move up and Move down, as Live TV's categories pane does. The sources and real-provider keyboard walks go down the list and rename from the menu.
- **Found by the tests and the walk, and fixed:**
  - **Toasts sat inside the shell**, under the search overlay's scrim: hiding from Search showed an Undo nobody could click. **Toasts now show over every route** (`AppToasts` at the app's root, around the router's navigator): over search, a dialog, the full-screen player (docs/05's "Downloads paused while you watch" toast is one that shows there). A toast's action was never reachable from the keyboard (a plain gesture): **Ctrl+Z runs the Undo of the toast shown** (`AppNotice.undoable`; a text field's own Ctrl+Z comes first), and an action ends its toast. (Decided under "decide UX".)
  - **Hiding from the Guide's menu tripped a semantics assertion** in debug builds (the grid changed under the closing menu, as step 4's sheet did). **A menu's item now runs once the menu has faded out** (120 ms; 60 with reduce motion), so nothing changes under a closing menu; tests that checked an item's effect at once wait for it.
  - **Tab from Live TV's filter never reached the list**, and Tab round the screen never reached the filter or the sort: in reading order the tall panes beside the list made one band of everything, where the list (on the filter's left edge) sorted before the filter. The list pane is its own traversal group now: Tab goes categories → Manage → the filter → No. → A–Z → the list, and round.
  - **Home's Recently watched channels kept a channel whose category was hidden** (it checked only the channel's own flag). `ChannelItem` carries its category's flag (`inHiddenCategory`) and decision 8's rule as `isVisible`; the row uses it and re-reads when categories change.
  - **Home's rows could miss a change made on another screen:** every row re-read on a `SELECT 1` query stream with its own tables, but drift shares query streams by their SQL and variables alone, so all of them were one stream watching the tables of whichever was made first. `tableChanges` names its tables in its SQL; Home's rows and Continue watching use it.
- **Tests:** Live TV (7: Hide channel with Undo; Show hidden channels, dimmed and tagged, and Show channel; the categories pane's Hide with Undo, Rename…, Move up/down and Alt+↑/↓ with the focus following, Manage to the Hidden channels tab or the categories; the Tab order round the screen); Settings → Hidden channels (6: no tab until a channel is hidden, the tab's count and rows, the filter and its no-match line, one Tab stop with Enter showing and the focus moving on, Show all, a row's Show); the Categories manager (one Tab stop, the menu key); the Guide's menu key and Hide with Undo; hiding from Search (the real row, Undo over the overlay, searching again); toasts (Ctrl+Z, a click on Undo, clicks beside a toast reach the screen); Home's recent row and a hidden category; `tableChanges` (two table sets, a pause). **2,086 app tests** under `TZ=UTC`.
- **Exit criterion 4, proved by `integration_test/hiding_walk_test.dart`** (keys only, the real app against the fake panel with its guide imported, 35 s under xvfb): a favorite hidden from Live TV's channel menu, and the category of a channel watched a moment ago hidden from the categories pane, are gone from Live TV's list, the Guide's (and its grid), Search ("No results", "1 hidden channel matches.") and Home's Favorite and Recently watched rows; then Search's **Show in Settings** → the Hidden channels tab → Enter brings the channel back, and the category's row → Space brings the category back, and both are in every list again. It found the three bugs above that the widget tests had not: the Tab order, Home's hidden-category rule, and Home's shared change stream.

### The phase exit (step 8, 2026-09-29)
- **The keyboard walk, in two files** (keys only, the real app, database, sync and player against the fake panel; each also runs in CI):
  - `integration_test/favorites_walk_test.dart` (28 s): Ctrl+K → a channel's name → Enter plays it full screen → Esc; Live TV → F on the first three channels → Favorites → New group "Sport" → Alt+↑ takes two of them into it → **the app closed and opened again on the same database, then the source synced again: the order and the group are still there** (exit criterion 2 again, end to end) → Live TV → Favorites → Sport → Enter → ↓ zaps through the group in its order, and round. `PanelApp.restart()` keeps the data folder and the keyring.
  - `integration_test/hiding_walk_test.dart` (step 7): exit criterion 4.
  - With `search_keyboard_test.dart` (step 4, exit criterion 1) and the name corpus (exit criterion 3), **every exit criterion is proved**.
  - Alt+↑, not the plan's Alt+↓: a new group comes before the favorites in no group, so they move up into it.
- **Measured:**
  - **Typing into search** on the `large` catalogue with its guide (`integration_test/search_typing_test.dart`, profile mode on the real display; six texts typed a letter at a time at 8 keys a second, and all of them again in one run): **0 of 488 frames over 16 ms, the worst build 9.0 ms, the UI isolate's longest pause 25 ms.** The test checks each text's groups show (a first run measured an empty panel — the typing hadn't reached the field under `flutter drive` until the test registered its text input).
  - **Search per query** again, with the write-ahead log: median 6.5 ms, p95 23.6 ms, worst 29.7 ms (budget 50 ms, now in docs/06).
  - **The name fill:** 50,000 names in 1.13 s.
- **Your panel's names, with your go-ahead** (one catalogue-only sync into a throwaway database, no stream; `test/tools/real_panel_names_test.dart`, tag `real_provider`, skipped unless asked for; the lists in `build/real_provider_run/names/`):
  - **12,608 channels: 11,149 names cleaned, 1,459 as the provider wrote them, 4,808 with a badge.** Read through: country prefixes gone (`US:` 538, `USA:` 345, `DE:` 258, `IT:` 209 … `Irq|`, `Leb|`, `PS|`), badges right (`AR: ROYA GALTA HD` → ROYA GALTA · HD, `SW: 6+ 4K` → 6+ · 4K), technical tags kept (`TR: EKOTURK HEVC` → EKOTURK HEVC), local stations' regions kept (`US: NBC 10 HD [EL DORADO]` stays whole — a resolution tag before a bracketed region is left in the name).
  - **Found and fixed:** a country written after the name behind a bar, as some panels write whole categories (`BEIN SPORTS FR 1 4K | France |`, `TVP SPORT HD | Poland |`), kept both the country and the resolution in the name — and where the name was one short word, the leading-tag rule took it for a tag: **`STV | UK |` showed as "UK" and `TVI | Portugal |` as "Portugal".** Now a country after a bar is dropped (`withoutTrailingCountries`, shared with the guide's matcher so both read the name alike), from a list of countries in English, in their own languages and as seen (`Turky`, `Srbija`, `BiH`) — not the UK's nations (`BBC One | Wales`), regions or languages, and not `Georgia`. A bare country after a lone tag is the name (`PS| Palestine` → Palestine). **Over your 12,608 names this changes 52, all for the better** (`BEIN SPORTS FR 1 4K | France |` → BEIN SPORTS FR 1 · 4K, `STV | UK |` → STV), and keeps `| P2P |` and `| Multi Quality |` (the feed's own words). The next sync writes the new names. The corpus has these cases now (17 lines).
  - **20,098 movie names — no cleanup recommended for v1** (decision 2's open question): the "leading tags" there are titles (`SAS: Red Notice`, `HIT: The Third Case`, `WWE: Raw`), which the channel rule would cut; 372 (1.9 %) are Arabic titles in `- HD` / `- FHD` pairs, the tag telling the copies apart; 756 (3.8 %) are release names with dots, nearly all dated WWE episodes (`WWE.RAW.2026.09.28`); 415 carry the year in parentheses, which reads fine; no entities. If anything later, the `Title - HD` pairs could get the channels' badge; recorded for the user to overrule. **Confirmed by the user 2026-09-29: no movie-name cleanup for v1.**
- **Docs:** docs/05 "As built" (Live TV's Phase 6 parts, Favorites, Search, Settings → Categories, toasts, menus, shortcuts, name cleanup); docs/02 (schema v7, the programmes' start order, the write-ahead log); docs/06 (the search budgets, how CI names failures).
- **All green at the exit:** analyze, format, `build_runner` without a diff, **2,101 app tests** under `TZ=UTC` (11 skipped), the fake provider's suite, and all 17 integration walks under xvfb (every file but the real-provider ones, the soak, the zap benchmark and the two profile-mode scroll measurements).
- **Left open:** "Remind me" on upcoming programmes (decision 5 — Phase 9 or after v1, the user's call); Favorites' drag shows Flutter's gap rather than the canvas's accent line; the Categories pane in Live TV takes the focus back on its first item, not the chosen one, when Tab comes round.
- **Answered 2026-09-29: "Remind me" goes into Phase 9** (the user: "go for step 9"). It stays decision 5's design: a system notification at the programme's start, not an in-app toast, since the window is often covered. docs/08's Phase 9 now has it as a step and an exit criterion. It goes on Search's upcoming rows, where the canvas draws it, and **also on the Guide's programme sheet** (decided under "decide UX"), because that is where upcoming programmes are browsed. The notification package and its Windows work are for Phase 9's plan.

### Alongside: CI and the Windows failures
- **CI's Linux failure since the plan push was `vod_walk_test.dart`** (Phase 5's exit walk, first run on CI with that push), named once CI annotated each integration test. Reproduced here with CI's 120 s samples (this laptop's are 600 s): the next-episode card plays the next episode with 10 s left, and **an episode left there was saved at 110 s of 120 — 92 %, under the 95 % that counts as watched** — so Home offered "S1 · E2 · 1 min left" instead of E3. A 600 s episode is left at 98 %, which hid it; a real short episode (a 3-minute clip) would have shown the same.
  - **Decided:** the card appears only in the credits, so moving on through it — the count or Play now — saves the episode left **as watched, at its end** (`playVod(finishedLeaving: true)`; with no known length, `setWatched`). Esc on the card still plays to the end, which was already watched.
- **CI names each failing integration test with its error**, not only the file: each file writes its own JSON report, and `tools/ci/failed_tests.dart` now reads a widget test's printed exception ("Expected … Actual …", and the test's line) where the error event only says "See exception logs above". A file whose app never starts is still named.
- **Windows: a file a job had open stayed open after the job was stopped.** A killed isolate's files close only when they are collected, so Windows refused to delete the tests' folders (`M3U a big file is only read to its first entries`, `removing a source mid-sync stops the sync first`) — and would have refused to move or delete a user's playlist for a while after a check or a cancelled sync. Now:
  - `parseM3u` ends early and cleanly on `StopReading` (thrown or returned by `onEntry`) or when its `stop` future completes: the input is cancelled, the file closed, before the summary of what was read comes back.
  - **The playlist check** reads with `readM3uInBackground(stopAfter:)` and waits for the reader to stop itself; the kill is only the timeout's last resort.
  - **A guarded job asked to stop** runs what its task registered with `JobCancellation.beforeStop` before it answers that it can be killed; the M3U sync registers its read there. A close that fails or hangs still ends in the kill (the grace period).
  - **Tests** read `/proc/self/fd` on Linux, where every isolate's files show: after a check, and after a source removed mid-sync, the playlist is not open. Both fail on the old code.
- **The database keeps a write-ahead log** (`configureAppDatabase`: `journal_mode = WAL`, `synchronous = NORMAL`, the log cut back to 64 MB after a checkpoint). With the rollback journal every commit waited for several disk syncs; the Windows CI's 30 s timeouts were all tests of many small commits on a file database (a sync in batches of 50, twice; an import cancelled and run again), and Windows' disk syncs are slow. **Measured here:** the cancel test's body 4 s → 1.5 s; the `large` first sync 6.5 → 5.8 s, re-sync 3.9 s either way, the 27 MB playlist 16.2 → 12.0 s; the whole suite 85 → 79 s. **The trade:** a crash of the app loses nothing committed; a power cut or a crash of the system may lose the last commits (a favorite, a watch position), never the file's consistency. The hard-rule-3 test now keeps its database the same way and scans the log file too. Whether it clears the Windows timeouts shows on the next CI run.

## ADR-014 · 2026-09-29 · Accepted 2026-10-13 without the TV sitting — Phase 7 casting choices
**Accepted on the user's word (2026-10-13: "the 7 and 8 are apporved"), with step 8's matrix on the real TV not run:** Living Room TV has been off the laptop's network since 2026-10-02 (the laptop is on 192.168.26.x). Everything below was verified against the fake receiver, and Phase 0's spike on the real TV (ADR-004). What only the real TV can say stays an open check, to run when it is reachable (about 35 minutes with Phase 8's rows, after asking): `test/tools/cast_tv_test.dart` and `test/tools/cast_matrix_tv_test.dart`; docs/04's "Verify" notes are unfilled until then.

**Decision:** the Phase 7 choices below, organized by step, as docs/plans/phase-7-casting.md proposed and the user approved on 2026-09-29 ("do the recommended") — **the recommendation in all eight decisions and the five sketches**, with the departures recorded where they happen.

The eight, in short: **1** movies and episodes cast in this phase too (a file the TV plays as it is goes direct with Range; anything else through the relay as one continuous stream, a seek restarting it). **2** while casting, everything played goes to the TV; the Live TV preview opens no stream of its own; Cast while watching moves what plays. **3** FFmpeg and ffprobe read the provider through a loopback proxy inside the relay (no credentials on a command line, the URL rebuilt per reconnect, the connection visible to the app). **4** the plan's facts from the laptop's player, then a probe remembered this run, then ffprobe (8 s). **5** the relay in its own long-lived isolate. **6** bonsoir and multicast_dns side by side, merged by device id, plus Add by IP address. **7** the desktop portal's Inhibit (logind as the fallback) over the `dbus` package on Linux, `SetThreadExecutionState` on Windows. **8** `tools/fake_receiver`, which fetches and checks what it is told to play.

### Discovery, known devices, FFmpeg in the build (step 1, 2026-09-29)
- **Domain** (`lib/core/cast/`): `CastDevice` (id, name, host, port, model, capability bits, what it runs, manual, answering), `KnownCastDevice`, `CastLearned` (the tallest picture taken, refused codecs, sources refused a direct play), `HevcSupport`; `CastDiscovery` (a stream that runs discovery while listened to), `CastAddressCheck` with its answers (`CastDeviceAnswered`, `CastAudioOnlyAnswered`, `CastNoAnswer`), `CastAddress.tryParse` (IPv4, IPv6 with or without brackets and a zone, host names, a port); `CastDeviceStore`; `CastReadiness` (ready, or FFmpeg missing).
- **Discovery** (`lib/data/cast/`, decision 6):
  - `MulticastDnsCastBrowser`: a round every 8 s — PTR `_googlecast._tcp.local` for 3 s, each answer's TXT, SRV and A taken as they come (a Cast device sends them with the PTR, so they are in the client's cache) — and a device dropped once it misses **two rounds in a row**. A fresh client per round, so its cache can't keep a device that left.
  - `BonsoirCastBrowser`: resolves what Avahi finds; a TXT update keeps the resolved address; a device goes when Avahi says it is lost. **Avahi resolves whichever of IPv4 and IPv6 it saw first** (both seen on this laptop, run to run), so an IPv6-only answer gets its host name's IPv4 address looked up (`<id>.local`, through the system's resolver).
  - `MergedCastDiscovery`: both side by side, merged by id — **the IPv4 address wins**, a field one lacks is filled from the other; listed while either sees it; by name. A browser that fails (Avahi out of reach, port 5353 refused) logs once and sees nothing; the other carries on. Everything stops with the last listener.
  - **TXT reading** (`cast_txt.dart`, tolerant): `fn` the name, else `md`, else the instance name without its id, else "Cast device"; `id` (lower case), else the 32 hex digits the instance name ends in; `ca` in base 10 — **bit 0 clear is a speaker, left out; a missing or unreadable `ca` is listed** (a LOAD it can't play says so; a TV hidden says nothing); `rs` what it runs (empty is nothing); control characters and U+FFFD out of names, 100 characters at most.
  - **A device only seen is not stored.** A row is written when one is added by address, cast to (step 6) or set up in Settings; a kept device found at a new address or under a new name is refreshed.
- **Departure: Add by address asks the device's mDNS port directly, not the Cast connection.** A DNS-SD PTR query sent straight to `<address>:5353` (legacy unicast, RFC 6762 §6.7) is answered with the name, model, id, port and address in one packet — **measured on Living Room TV: 0.2–0.5 s**, the Nest Mini answering as a speaker — and nothing shows on the screen. That gives sketch E its "Found "Bedroom" (Chromecast)", which a CONNECT and GET_STATUS can't (RECEIVER_STATUS has no name). Our own small DNS reader (`dns_message.dart`: compression pointers, never throws — a fuzz of 25,000 messages) rather than multicast_dns's internals. A device on another subnet may ignore the query (RFC 6762 §5.5): **the CONNECT and GET_STATUS check comes in step 2** with the client, as the fallback, and for a test receiver at `127.0.0.1:<port>`. A device added by address is asked again every 20 s while the list is watched, and listed as not answering when it doesn't.
- **Schema v8: `cast_devices`** as docs/02 lists it, plus **`last_port`** (8009; another only for a test receiver added by address). Adding by address keeps a kept device's settings, last use and learning; casting to one added by address leaves it manual. `learned_json` is tolerant: anything but what the app wrote reads as nothing learned, field by field.
- **FFmpeg in the build:** the Linux build **hard-links** `third_party/ffmpeg/linux-x64/{ffmpeg,ffprobe,LICENSE.txt,VERSION}` into `bundle/ffmpeg/` (the bundle is rebuilt on every build, and the two are 340 MB; a copy where the file system can't link); the Windows build installs `windows-x64`'s `.exe`s into `ffmpeg/` beside the app. A checkout without them builds an app whose `castReadinessProvider` says FFmpeg is missing. `FfmpegBinaries.locate` looks beside the executable, then for a checkout's `third_party/` from the working folder up (tests, `flutter run`).
- **Providers** (`lib/features/casting/data/casting_providers.dart`): `castDeviceStoreProvider`, `castAddressCheckProvider`, `castDiscoveryProvider`, `castDevicesProvider` (auto-dispose: discovery runs only while something shows the list), `ffmpegBinariesProvider`, `castReadinessProvider`.
- **Measured on this laptop** (`test/tools/cast_discovery_network_test.dart`, tag `real_network`, listening only): Living Room TV first seen by **bonsoir in 64–139 ms** (Avahi's cache; its IPv4 address 20 ms later when Avahi gave IPv6 first) and by **multicast_dns in 367–584 ms**; the Nest Mini left out by both. **With Avahi out of reach** (`DBUS_SYSTEM_BUS_ADDRESS` pointing nowhere): bonsoir logs and sees nothing, multicast_dns finds the TV in 584 ms. Stopping Avahi itself needs root, which this session hasn't: `sudo systemctl stop avahi-daemon.socket avahi-daemon`, then the test, then `start`.
- **Tests:** addresses (12 read, 17 refused); TXT records as the TV and the speaker send them and odd ones (no `fn`/`md`/`id`, five unreadable `ca`s, control characters, 300 characters, Arabic and emoji, bad ports, no address); the DNS reader on the TV's recorded answer (`test_fixtures/cast/`, its ids and serials replaced by made-up ones of the same length, so the compression is the TV's), cut at every length, pointer loops, and 25,000 random or corrupted messages; the address check against a loopback responder (answered, speaker, silent after one resend, a lost first query, garbage first, a PTR without TXT, a name that doesn't resolve); both browsers (multicast_dns on scripted rounds under fake time; bonsoir through a fake platform); the merge (IPv4 wins, fields filled, either browser, a failing browser, one list per change, the manual devices' checks and their interval, write-back of a new address); the store on a real database; the locator in temporary folders; the v7 → v8 migration. **148 new tests** (139 for casting; the migrations to v8), the casting ones stable 8 of 8 run in parallel; **2,249 app tests** under `TZ=UTC`.

### The Cast v2 client and the fake receiver (step 2, 2026-10-02)
- **Step 1 approved** (the user: "continue", 2026-10-02), with its departure (Add by address over the device's mDNS port) and the IPv4 address preferred.
- **Domain** (`lib/core/cast/cast_receiver.dart`), the seam step 6's coordinator talks to:
  - `CastReceivers.join(address)` → `CastJoined(session, launched:)`, or `CastJoinFailed` (`unreachable`, `noAnswer`, `launchRefused`, `launchTimedOut`).
  - `CastReceiverSession`: `load`, `play`, `pause`, `seek` (keeps paused or playing), `stopMedia`, `setVolume`, `setMuted`; `stop` (the receiver closes, the TV goes home) and `leave` (it plays on); `localAddress` (this computer on the connection to the TV, for the relay); `state` and `states`.
  - `CastSessionState`: the link (connected, reconnecting, ended), why it ended (stopped, left, closedOnDevice, otherApp with the app's name, lost), the media, the volume (`fixed` when the TV's remote sets it, as on Living Room TV).
  - `CastMediaStatus`: IDLE with `extendedStatus` LOADING reads as loading; a state the app doesn't know reads as buffering (neither an end nor an error).
  - `CastLoad` is a plain class whose `toString` leaves the URL out, like `PlayRequest`: a direct play's URL carries a provider's credentials.
  - Commands answer a sealed `CastCommandResult` (done, refused with a `CastRefusal`, unanswered, disconnected), not an `AppFailure`: a refusal is the device's answer, as step 1's `CastAddressAnswer` is.
- **The channel** (`lib/data/cast/`):
  - The generated `CastMessage` is committed in `proto/`; `tools/gen_cast_proto.sh` regenerates it byte for byte (the spike's protoc_plugin 25.1.0 matches protobuf 6.1.0).
  - `CastFrameReader`: a length past 64 KiB loses the framing, and the channel closes.
  - `CastChannel` runs on a `CastTransport` (TLS, or a pipe in tests). Request ids count from 1; the answer is the first message with its id, which is also passed on with every other message; null on a timeout or a close.
  - **The heartbeat:** a PING every 5 s, the device's PINGs answered, and the channel lost after **3 intervals with nothing heard** — any message counts, not only a PONG.
  - **Tolerant reading:** not protobuf, a binary payload, unreadable JSON, JSON that isn't an object, or an unknown namespace — skipped. The device's CLOSE ends that virtual connection.
  - **It logs message types only, never payloads** (a LOAD's URL); a test checks it.
  - **TLS:** TCP first, then the handshake with its own timeout (a port that never speaks TLS would otherwise hold the socket); any certificate is accepted.
  - `ReceiverChannel` and `MediaChannel` (`cast_namespaces.dart`), `loadPayload`, `castCommandResult`.
