  - **Status readers** (`cast_status_json.dart`): a RECEIVER_STATUS whose `status` isn't an object says nothing — never "the app is gone"; a MEDIA_STATUS without `media` keeps the session's URL, length and picture size; MULTIZONE_STATUS's devices.
- **The session** (`CastV2Receivers`):
  - **Join:** connect, GET_STATUS, then reuse the running Default Media Receiver or LAUNCH it. The answer to LAUNCH comes once the app runs; the LAUNCH_STATUS before it is not the answer. If the answer doesn't list the app yet, the status is asked every 0.5 s, up to 30 s. Then CONNECT to its transport id, and ask the media status.
  - **A LOAD is done once the TV took it**: the first answer is a MEDIA_STATUS (IDLE, LOADING). A LOAD_FAILED arriving later as a second answer to the same request (1.6 s later on the TV in Phase 0) shows at once as media IDLE/ERROR; the TV's own IDLE/ERROR follows. A status of an older media session is out of date (ids only grow).
  - **Following the TV:** a RECEIVER_STATUS without this session's app ends it (closedOnDevice, or otherApp with the name). The app's CLOSE makes the session ask GET_STATUS to tell which; receiver-0's CLOSE is a lost connection.
  - **Reconnecting:** a socket error or close, the heartbeat, or an oversized frame → `reconnecting`. Retries start after 0.5 s, doubling to 5 s, for 30 s. Each connects, asks GET_STATUS and joins the same app by its session id (or transport id), CONNECTs and asks the media status; the TV plays on meanwhile. The app gone → the session ends as above; nothing within 30 s → `lost`.
  - Commands while reconnecting answer `CastDisconnected` without sending anything. `stop` while reconnecting ends the session at once; the TV, out of reach, is not told.
- **Add by address's fallback** (`CastConnectionCheck`): CONNECT, GET_STATUS and the multizone GET_STATUS side by side, then CLOSE — nothing launched, nothing on screen.
  - MULTIZONE_STATUS gives the name and the id: its UUID without dashes, in lower case. **That this equals TXT `id` is assumed; the TV run checks it.**
  - Capabilities with bit 0 clear are a speaker. Without multizone the device is named by its address, with the id `address:<host>:<port>`.
  - `castAddressCheckProvider` is now `CastAddressChecks([unicast, connection])`: the DNS-SD answer first (it has the model), the Cast connection when that gets none. New: `castReceiversProvider`.
- **`tools/fake_receiver`** (decision 8, the protocol side):
  - TLS on 127.0.0.1 with a test-only self-signed EC certificate, in source and public on purpose.
  - **Its own hand-written `CastMessage` codec**, checked against bytes from `protoc --encode`, so the app's generated code and the fake can't share a mistake.
  - What it does like the TV is listed in its README. Faults: silent, heartbeat off, dropped or refused connections, a refused LAUNCH, refused LOADs, another app, the remote's pause, play and Back, raw bytes and JSON.
  - A CLI (`dart run tools/fake_receiver/bin/fake_receiver.dart --port 8010`); its own 19 tests, which CI now runs.
- **The TV run** (`test/tools/cast_tv_test.dart`, new tag `real_cast`, skipped unless run on purpose):
  - What it does: both address checks; join; a LOAD of the MP4 sample from a Range server on the Cast connection's own local address (port 38400–38499); PAUSE, SEEK to 1:00, PLAY; STOP, in a `finally`.
  - **Dry-run against the fake receiver:** joined in 1.5 s (the launch delay set), PLAYING 314 ms after LOAD, playing from 1:00 14 ms after the SEEK.
  - **Not run on the TV yet:** the laptop was on another network this session (192.168.26.x).
- **Tests: 83 new app tests.**
  - Framing: every split point, the 64 KiB edge, 500 random streams in random reads, 2,000 random byte runs.
  - The channel under fake time: ids, timeouts, the heartbeat, tolerant reading, a 300-round fuzz, a LOAD's URL never logged.
  - The status readers on the TV's recorded payloads, with made-up ids; the namespaces.
  - **The client against the fake over TLS (27):** every way to join, every command, the refusals, the remote, another app, junk, a dropped connection, heartbeat loss, an oversized frame, commands while reconnecting, the app gone while away, giving up, stop, leave.
  - The fallback check (7).
  - **Stability:** the casting tests 8 of 8 run in parallel (55 each), the fake's suite 8 of 8. **2,332 app tests** (13 skipped) under `TZ=UTC`.

### The probe and the planner (step 3, 2026-10-02)
- **Step 2 approved** (the user: "continue", 2026-10-02). Its TV run still waits: the laptop was on 192.168.26.x again.
- **Domain** (`lib/core/cast/`):
  - `StreamFacts` (container, `VideoFacts`, `AudioFacts` with FFmpeg's `0:a:<n>` numbering, length, bit rate, and where they came from); `streamFactsFromPlayer` reads the laptop's player (**mpv's `video-codec` is `h264 (H.264 / AVC …)`: its first word is ffprobe's name**, checked with mpv 0.34.1), `playerAudioIndex`, `channelCount`.
  - `StreamProbe` with sealed results (probed; failed: timed out, unreadable, no streams, couldn't start).
  - `CastDeviceProfile`: the `md` seed, then the user's HEVC setting, then what was learned. **Only "Chromecast Ultra" is seeded** (HEVC, 2160p): docs/04's other rows share `md` "Chromecast" or haven't been seen. HEVC "Automatic" with nothing learned is *tried*, as is an unknown height; a refusal teaches. **The user's HEVC "Yes" wins over a learned refusal.**
  - `CastPlan`: `CastDelivery` (direct HLS, direct file, relay HLS/TS, relay continuous fMP4, each with its LOAD `contentType`), sealed `CastVideo` (copy; transcode with height, bit rate, frame rate, deinterlace, the software cap and its reasons; none) and `CastAudio` (copy a track; to AAC with its reason; none), `live`, `CastOutput` (what the TV gets), and `quality` (the badge).
  - **`planCast` is pure and lives in `lib/core/cast/`, not `lib/data/cast/` as docs/04's table said:** step 6's coordinator is domain code, and domain code never imports `lib/data/`. docs/04 corrected.
- **The planner's rules beyond docs/04's**, decided on what the TV and Cast devices do:
  - re-encoded: H.264 of more than 8 bits, 4:2:2 or 4:4:4 (no Cast device decodes it), HEVC beyond Main and Main 10, an unknown video codec (to be safe), and VP8/VP9/AV1 (some models play them, but `md` can't tell which; IPTV hardly carries them). So WebM never goes direct.
  - A re-encode keeps the source's height, never more; an unknown height is held at 1080p. An interlaced picture re-encoded for any reason is deinterlaced, **one picture per field** (FFmpeg's bwdif `send_field`, step 4's arguments): 1080i25 → 50 fps, capped at 60.
  - Bit rates: docs/04's 1.3 × the source, **scaled by pixels when the picture is made smaller** (1.3 × a 4K source's rate at 1080p would waste the link), between a floor and a ceiling per height — ≤480: 1.5–4, ≤576: 2–5, ≤720: 4–8, ≤1080: 6–15, ≤1440: 10–25, above: 16–40 Mbps — in whole 100 kbps. An unknown source gets the floor. The picture's own rate, else the stream's less its sound's.
  - `aac_latm` (DVB's LATM AAC) is converted: the receiver's TS reader takes ADTS. Any audio codec the device refused before is converted, Dolby passthrough or not.
  - **Direct only without a User-Agent of the source's own** (the TV can't send one) **and on the stream's first audio track** (the TV picks its own). **Low-latency mode turns direct HLS off:** it is HLS's delay too. Files ignore Low-latency mode.
  - Audio track: the laptop's, then the first preferred language there is — **two- and three-letter codes both** (`en`/`eng`, `de`/`ger`/`deu`; a table of 41 languages, so `ar` never matches Armenian's `arm`) — then the stream's default, then the first.
  - **`CastLearned.refusedInterlaced`** (stored as `refused_interlaced`): docs/04's "interlaced → copy first; if the receiver errors, deinterlace" needs it, and learning "the codec" would wrongly refuse all H.264. Step 6 records it.
  - The software cap: with no hardware encoder (step 4 says), a re-encode stays at 1080p or below.
- **The badge's words** (`lib/features/casting/presentation/cast_plan_text.dart`), in the canvas's voice ("Video and audio go to your TV untouched. Nothing is re-encoded."): the details line ("1080p · 50 fps · H.264 · AAC 2.0"; 2160p as "4K", interlaced copies as "1080i"); one sentence per transcode reason and per audio conversion; "Your TV", not the device's name, as the plan's own example says. The 4K refusal carries docs/04's hint (the HDMI input may be limited to HD; Input Signal Plus on Samsung). Step 7 shows them.
- **The probe** (`FfprobeStreamProbe`, `lib/data/cast/`): `-analyzeduration 2000000 -probesize 5000000`. **Measured on the fake panel's live channels, sent at their real pace:** ffprobe's own 5 s took 4.0–4.8 s (1.9–2.2 s for the 4K ones, which fill 5 MB sooner); 1 s took 0.1–0.7 s; **2 s took 0.5–1.7 s**, every field the planner reads right in all three. 2 s, for streams whose keyframes come further apart (one channel on your panel needs 4.7 s to its first picture). The 8 s timeout, the User-Agent for HTTP only, the input never logged and replaced by `<input>` in ffprobe's words, then `redact()`. `readFfprobeJson` is tolerant (hard rule 1): numbers as strings, `N/A`, wrong types, cover pictures skipped (`attached_pic`, `still_image`), every audio stream counted for FFmpeg's numbers, Matroska's upper-case tags, lengths up to a year.
- **Departure: the process supervisor comes in this step, not step 5.** ffprobe is the app's first process, and hard rule 8 holds from the first one. `ProcessSupervisor` (`lib/data/process/`): a PID file per process under `<app support>/processes/` (JSON: pid, executable, owner, the app's pid and executable), written before `start` returns — **no PID file, no process** (it is killed and `start` throws); a timeout; SIGTERM, then SIGKILL after 3 s; `run` gathers standard output (capped) and the last 8 KiB of standard error, never throws; `stopAll` for quitting (step 6 wires the quit).
  - **The launch sweep** (`bootstrap()`, before anything starts a process): a leftover is killed only if it still runs the executable its file names (`/proc/<pid>/exe`, else its command line, on Linux; `QueryFullProcessImageName` through win32 on Windows — **not tried on a real Windows PC**; CI runs the tests there with PING.EXE) **and the app that started it is gone** (two copies of the app never kill each other's). Every file is deleted. **Checked on the real app:** a planted leftover was killed 0.56 s after launch and logged.
- **Decision 4's order** (`StreamFactsLookup`, `lib/features/casting/domain/`): the player's facts (remembered too), then a remembered probe (256 streams, least recently used out, no expiry: FFmpeg's report replaces them in step 5, `forget` drops one), then ffprobe — **the probe's input is asked for only when a probe runs**, so the relay's proxy opens a provider connection only then. Probes of one stream at once share one ffprobe; failures aren't remembered. Nothing in the app calls it until step 6.
- **Tests: 228 new.**
  - `readFfprobeJson` on every sample's recorded answer (`test_fixtures/cast/probe/`, the bundled ffprobe's, file names only), the tolerant cases, and 50,000 corrupted or mistyped answers — which **found a crash**: a huge `duration` overflowed `round()`. Fixed.
  - **The planner (119):** every live sample on five device profiles (Living Room TV as found, an H.264-only Chromecast, the TV on a 1080p HDMI link, a Chromecast Ultra, HEVC set to No), each rule of docs/04 and decision 1, each setting and learned limit, the bit rates, tracks and languages, missing facts.
  - The profile; the player's facts; the lookup (order, sharing, LRU, failures); the words for every reason.
  - **The supervisor against real processes:** PID files, the timeout, SIGTERM then SIGKILL on a process that ignores SIGTERM, `run`'s output, overflow and timeout, a folder that can't be written, the sweep (ours, a reused pid, another copy of the app's, our own, junk).
  - **The probe with ffprobe:** a clip it makes itself (so CI's Linux job runs it with the system ffprobe), a live stream over HTTP with the User-Agent, a server that hangs (the timeout, no ffprobe left), a 401 on a URL with credentials (the status named, the credentials nowhere in the result or the log), a page, subtitles alone, a missing ffprobe, and every media sample against its recording (bundled ffprobe only: the system's 4.4 gives an MP4 no field order).
  - **Stability:** the new tests 8 of 8 run in parallel (254 each); **2,560 app tests** (13 skipped) under `TZ=UTC`; the fake receiver's 19 and the fake provider's 141.

### Hardware encoders (step 4, 2026-10-02)
- **Step 3 approved** (the user: "continue", 2026-10-02). **Step 2's TV run is put off at the user's request** ("consider the test on living room tv to do it later"); the laptop was on 192.168.26.x again anyway. It can share one sitting with step 6's TV cast.
- **Domain** (`lib/core/cast/cast_encoders.dart`):
  - `CastEncoderKind` (FFmpeg's name, a label; docs/04's order for each system);
  - `HardwareDecode`: 8-bit 4:2:0 H.264, HEVC Main, HEVC Main 10, MPEG-2. `of(VideoFacts)` says which a picture is; anything else (10-bit H.264, HEVC range extensions, VC-1, VP9, AV1) is decoded on the processor;
  - `CastEncoder` (the kind, its render node, what its chip decodes, whether it needs the bundled libva);
  - `CastEncoders`: `available` in docs/04's order, `best`, `canTranscode`, `softwareOnly` (what feeds `CastPlanRequest.softwareEncoderOnly`), `after(failed)` for step 6's fallback;
  - `CastEncoderDetection` (`encoders()`, `detectAgain()`), and `NoCastEncoders` for a build without FFmpeg.
- **The arguments** (`videoTranscodeArgs`, `lib/data/cast/ffmpeg_video_args.dart`): what goes before `-i`, after the maps, and the process's environment. Audio, the maps and the muxer are step 5's.
  - **NVENC:** decoded on the chip with `-hwaccel cuda -hwaccel_output_format cuda`; `bwdif_cuda=mode=send_field:parity=auto:deint=all`; `scale_cuda=w=-2:h=<H>:format=nv12` (also 10-bit to 8); `-preset p4 -rc vbr`.
  - **VA-API:** `-hwaccel vaapi -hwaccel_device <node> -hwaccel_output_format vaapi`; `deinterlace_vaapi=rate=field` (the driver's best method); `scale_vaapi=…:format=nv12`; `-rc_mode VBR`.
  - **Quick Sync:** a `qsv` device on the node's VA-API (Linux) or Direct3D 11 (Windows); its own decoders (`h264_qsv`, `hevc_qsv`, `mpeg2_qsv`); `vpp_qsv` with `deinterlace=2:rate=field` and an explicit even width (it takes no `-2`); `-preset medium`.
  - **AMF (Windows):** decoded with `-hwaccel d3d11va`, filtered on the processor; `-usage transcoding -rc vbr_peak`.
  - **libx264:** `-preset veryfast`.
  - **A picture the chip doesn't decode** is decoded and filtered on the processor (`bwdif` with `send_field`, `scale`), then handed over as `yuv420p` (NVENC, libx264), `nv12` (AMF) or `nv12` uploaded (VA-API, Quick Sync).
  - **Every encoder:** `-profile:v high`; `-b:v` the plan's, `-maxrate` 1.5 ×, `-bufsize` 2 ×; `-g` two seconds of the output's pictures (docs/04's HLS segments). A picture is only ever made smaller: no scaling at the source's height, and `min(ih,<H>)` when its height isn't known.
- **Detection** (`FfmpegEncoderDetector`, `lib/data/cast/`):
  - FFmpeg's version line first. A result remembered for the same version, system and bundled-libva-or-not, under **7 days** old (a driver or a GPU can change without FFmpeg changing), is used as it is.
  - Otherwise, per candidate, **the one-second test encode**: a 1080p25 `testsrc2` from the processor, through the same arguments a re-encode uses. VA-API and Quick Sync are tried on each render node.
  - For each hardware encoder that passes, **a test decode of each `HardwareDecode` kind** on its chip, scaled (MPEG-2 also deinterlaced) there, with the re-encode's own arguments. The test pictures (1 s each, made by the bundled FFmpeg with libx264, libx265 and mpeg2video, the MPEG-2 one interlaced) are deleted after.
  - **A test passes when FFmpeg exits 0 and its `-progress` reports pictures made.** A chip that can't decode a codec makes FFmpeg fall back to the processor, and the GPU's scaler then refuses those pictures (exit 69; checked with AV1 on both GPUs here).
  - One detection at a time; anyone asking meanwhile shares it. A result with a test cut short (a timeout, the app quitting, a process that couldn't start) isn't remembered. It never throws. The log names what was found, and for each refusal FFmpeg's real reason, past the generic lines it ends every failure with.
  - Remembered in `<app support>/cast/encoders.json` (`castFolderProvider`, set by `bootstrap()`).
- **Departure: VA-API on Linux needs a libva bundled with FFmpeg.**
  - BtbN's FFmpeg 8.1 calls `vaMapBuffer2`, which libva has only since 2.21. Ubuntu 22.04 ships 2.14 and 24.04 ships 2.20 (checked: neither has it). So **every VA-API encode with the system's libva aborts** (SIGABRT and a core dump), and the plan's "NVENC and VA-API both detected" was impossible as planned.
  - `tools/fetch_libva.sh` builds libva 2.22.0 (MIT; 220 KB + 15 KB; needs glibc 2.34) in a throwaway Ubuntu 22.04 container (docker or podman) into `third_party/libva/linux-x64/` (gitignored; the source checksum is pinned). The Linux build hard-links it into `bundle/ffmpeg/libva/`. FFmpeg gets it through `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`, and only for an encoder that needs it.
  - The drivers stay the system's. Its driver path lists Debian/Ubuntu's, Fedora's and Arch's folders, and `LIBVA_DRIVERS_PATH` still wins.
  - **The bundled libva is tried first, then the system's.** On 22.04 and 24.04 the system's aborts. On a system newer than 2.22, whose driver may only offer a newer init function, ours fails cleanly and the system's works.
  - A checkout that hasn't run the script simply finds no VA-API, and the next candidate is used. **Phase 10's AppImage must carry it.**
  - Quick Sync fails here on its own grounds: the Intel driver (iHD 22.3.1) is older than the VA-API 1.15 it asks for. It comes after VA-API in the order, so nothing is lost on this laptop.
- **FFmpeg's default VA-API device is the first render node,** which here is NVIDIA's (renderD128, no VA-API driver), so every node is tried.
- **The first use of a CUDA filter compiles it: about 6 s** (6.2–6.6 s with `CUDA_CACHE_DISABLE=1`, against 0.7 s), cached in `~/.nv/ComputeCache`. Detection runs those filters, so a first cast doesn't pay it. The per-test timeout is 20 s, for this and for a GPU waking.
- **Measured on this laptop** (i7-10750H, 12 threads; GTX 1650 Ti; UHD 630 through the bundled libva) with `test/tools/cast_transcode_measure_test.dart` (tag `benchmark`). Each case is planned by `planCast` from the bundled ffprobe's facts and runs video only, 30 s at the stream's own pace. CPU is FFmpeg's processor time ÷ wall time, in % of one core; "speed" is the same re-encode unpaced:

  | Re-encode | NVENC | VA-API | libx264 |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | HEVC 2160p25 → H.264 1080p (6 Mbps) | 7 %, 11.1× | 8 %, 6.0× | 219 %, 4.0× |
  | MPEG-2 576i25 → H.264 576p50 (5 Mbps) | 4 %, 21.7× | 7 %, 17.6× | 117 %, 13.5× |
  | H.264 1080i50 → 1080p50, Smooth interlaced (10.7 Mbps) | 6 %, 6.6× | 7 %, 5.9× | 311 %, 3.4× |

  Both chips decode all four kinds. **Detection: 3.2 s fresh** (NVENC; VA-API on renderD129 with the bundled libva; libx264), **18 ms remembered.** In the experiments that led to these arguments (each path by hand, NVENC, VA-API and the processor), ffprobe found every output H.264 High, 8-bit 4:2:0, at the planned height, one picture per field, with the source's pixel shape (576i's 16:15 kept).
- **Not tried on real hardware:** Quick Sync, AMF, and everything on Windows. Their arguments are FFmpeg's documented ones; a wrong one only makes detection pass over that encoder (the test runs the same arguments), down to libx264.
- **For steps 5 and 6:** the relay adds the audio, maps and muxer to these. Step 6 asks `encoders()` when a session starts (alongside LAUNCH; step 7's picker can start it earlier), feeds `softwareOnly` to the planner, refuses a plan that needs a re-encode when nothing can, and on a failed re-encode calls `detectAgain()` and then `after()`.
- **Known gap:** HDR10 HEVC re-encoded to H.264 isn't tone-mapped, so its colours look washed out. That happens only when a TV refuses HEVC or 4K. `tonemap_vaapi` and `tonemap_opencl` exist; another option is HEVC 1080p out for a TV that plays HEVC.
- **Tests: 75 new** (and the measurement, skipped by default):
  - the domain;
  - the arguments for every encoder, on the chip and off, scaled or not, deinterlaced or not, an unknown height and frame rate;
  - **detection against a scripted FFmpeg behind the real supervisor:** this laptop's case, a system newer than the bundled libva, none bundled, no render nodes, Windows, the processor only, nothing, a test with no pictures, partial decodes, a test picture FFmpeg can't make, the folder cleared, the log's reasons;
  - remembering: version, a week, a clock gone back, a libva added, junk files, `detectAgain`, a timeout, the app quitting, a folder that can't be written; one detection at a time;
  - **with a real FFmpeg** (the system's on CI): libx264 found and remembered, and its arguments make what the plan says (MPEG-2 576i → 360 × 288 at 50 pictures a second, H.264 High);
  - the locator's libva; the supervisor's environment.
  - **Stability:** the new tests 8 of 8 run in parallel (103 each); **2,635 app tests** (14 skipped) under `TZ=UTC`.

### The relay, its proxy and its supervisor (step 5, 2026-10-02)
- **Step 4 approved** (the user: "continue", 2026-10-02), with its departure (the bundled libva). **The TV run still waits:** the user says Living Room TV isn't on the network for now ("I dont have the living room tv on network so we gonna do it later"). Nothing was cast and nothing played from the user's provider in this step.
- **Domain** (`lib/core/cast/cast_relay.dart`), the seam step 6's coordinator talks to:
  - `CastRelay`: `start(CastRelayRequest)` → `CastRelayStarted(session)` or `CastRelayNotStarted(failure)`, never throws; `openInput(CastUpstreamSource)` — the proxy's URL for ffprobe (decision 3), closed once the probe is done; `connections` (the provider connections the proxy holds, per source); `close()`.
  - `CastUpstreamSource` (source id, live, `resolve` → `Result<CastUpstream>`: URL, User-Agent, HLS or not, the source's connection limit). The coordinator adapts `StreamResolver` to it; the URL is asked for on every connection and never kept.
  - `CastRelaySession`: `url` and `contentType` for LOAD; `ready` (HLS: the playlist lists 2 segments; continuous: at once; or the failure); `events` — **one listener, which also gets what came before it listened** (FFmpeg says what it opened before the session is ready; a broadcast stream lost it); `renew({startAt, plan})` (a new URL: a continuous stream that ended, a file's seek, a new plan); `stop()`.
  - Events: `CastRelayFetched` (the TV asked: step 6's "never fetched within 10 s"), `CastRelayOpened(facts)` (decision 4, with the new `StreamFactsOrigin.relay`), `CastRelayStreamsChanged`, `CastRelayRestarted`, `CastRelayEnded(tvLeft)`, `CastRelayFailed`.
  - `CastRelayFailure`: the kind (provider refused, unreachable, unresolved, connections in use, FFmpeg failed, **encoder failed** — step 6 then tries `CastEncoders.after`, the restart budget, couldn't start), **the provider's status and the first 512 bytes of its answer (redacted)**, which `classifyStreamFailure` turns into docs/03's words exactly as for local playback, and FFmpeg's last warnings for Details.
  - `UnavailableCastRelay` for a build without FFmpeg; `castRelayProvider`, `relayFolderProvider` (bootstrap: `AppPaths.relay`, in the cache folder).
- **The relay's isolate** (decision 5): `IsolateCastRelay` (`lib/data/cast/relay/`) spawns `relayIsolateMain` with the first cast or probe, `errorsAreFatal: false` (one session's bug isn't every session's), and keeps it until `close()`, which stops every session, FFmpeg and folder. The app's side builds each session's FFmpeg job from its plan (`castRelayJob`) and answers the proxy's requests for a URL; the isolate's log lines come back through **`AppLog.forward`**, redacted again with the app's own secrets. An isolate that ends unasked fails its sessions. **Everything the isolate runs is plain Dart** (no Flutter import), so the SIGKILL test runs it in a `dart run` process.
- **The loopback proxy** (decision 3, `RelayProxy`):
  - On 127.0.0.1, its own server: nothing on the network can reach it. FFmpeg and ffprobe read `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/in/<token>`; **no credentials on a command line or in FFmpeg's messages** (the relay's log was checked for the panel's credentials in the end-to-end tests).
  - **Every connection of a live channel or a file asks the app for the URL**, built from the source with its User-Agent, so an expiring redirect is followed fresh (tested past the token's 10 s). A provider's HLS keeps its URL between playlist refreshes until something fails. Range passes for files and segments, never for a live edge.
  - **A live stream that ends — cleanly, cut, or quiet for 8 s — reaches FFmpeg as a cut** (no last chunk), so FFmpeg's `-reconnect` reconnects and gets a fresh connection. **Measured on the fake panel's drop and cut: FFmpeg reconnected within milliseconds, compensated the timestamp jump itself, and the segments went on with no restart and no discontinuity.**
  - A provider's HLS has its playlists rewritten (every URI and `URI="…"`), **its segments' proxied URLs keeping their extension**: FFmpeg's HLS reader refuses others (`allowed_segment_extensions`), found by the end-to-end test.
  - **Connections counted per source:** an input holds one while any of its requests is open (a provider's HLS for 3 s after its last: playlist and segments are one stream); another input of the source waits up to 10 s for one to come free, else a 503 and "connections in use". Tested on a one-connection panel: the probe's connection closed before the relay's opened, never two.
  - **Refusals:** a full-looking answer (429, or words naming max connections) is tried again after 1, 1 and 2 s — a panel can be slow to notice the connection just closed (ADR-010); no answer after 0.5, 1 and 2 s; anything else is passed on at once. Each is reported with its status and first bytes; errors are put in words that never carry a URL.
  - Timeouts: 8 s to connect, **15 s for the answer to begin** (8 s failed the matrix's 8 s slow start).
- **New: the program watch.** The fake panel's codec switch keeps its PIDs and changes their stream types; **FFmpeg, copying, says nothing and goes on sending HEVC as H.264** (no "New video stream" line either: that warning is for new PIDs). The proxy reads the PAT and the PMT of every live MPEG-TS stream it passes (`TsProgramWatch`: only those packets, a section in one packet, alignment found again after junk) and reports a change, which the session tells as `CastRelayStreamsChanged`: `0x1b 0xf → 0x24 0xf` 4.4 s into the stream. FFmpeg's "New … stream" warning is read too. A provider's own HLS isn't watched yet.
- **The server** (`RelayServer`, docs/04): bound to the Cast connection's local address, the first free port in 38400–38499 — **any port that won't bind, in use by this process or another, moves on; an address that isn't this computer's fails at once** (EADDRNOTAVAIL); 128-bit tokens; `/r/` HLS from the session's folder (segment names checked), `/p/` the continuous stream, `/f/` a file with Range (206, suffixes, 416; for step 6's picture and Phase 8); CORS on every answer, 404s and OPTIONS (204) included; docs/04's MIME types plus JPEG, PNG and WebP; `no-cache` on playlists and continuous streams; anything else a 404; the first fetch of each URL told.
- **FFmpeg's command line** (`relayArguments`, `castRelayJob`): docs/04's, with these departures:
  - **no `-user_agent`:** the proxy sends the source's own;
  - **`-reconnect_delay_max 1`, not 5:** FFmpeg's one peer is the proxy, which retries the provider itself, so a refused reconnect means the app is gone. **Measured after a SIGKILL of the app: FFmpeg ended by itself 16.1 s later with 5** (FFmpeg retries in two nested loops), **3.1 s with 1**;
  - **`-analyzeduration 2000000 -probesize 5000000`**, as the probe reads: on all eight live samples every stream found right, and ready (2 segments) in 4.0–4.5 s instead of 4.0–5.5 s. The rest of the wait is two 2 s segments at the stream's pace; step 8 tunes the 2;
  - `-loglevel repeat+level+info -nostats`: warnings logged (at most 10 a minute), the input's description read for `CastRelayOpened`;
  - `0:V:0` (never a cover picture), `-max_muxing_queue_size 1024`, HEVC copied into MP4 tagged `hvc1`;
  - **AAC copied into MP4 gets `-bsf:a aac_adtstoasc`:** FFmpeg 8 doesn't add it, and the first continuous HEVC cast failed with "Malformed AAC bitstream" after sending only the MP4's header;
  - HLS segments `seg%05d.ts`; a restart adds `append_list`, so the numbering carries on behind an `#EXT-X-DISCONTINUITY` and the TV keeps polling (FFmpeg 4.4 adds `#EXT-X-PROGRAM-DATE-TIME` lines; both readers tolerate them);
  - a file's start as `-ss` before `-i`.
- **The supervisor** (`RelayRuntime`, in the isolate):
  - **HLS:** ready at 2 segments; **a stall** (the newest segment older than max(3 × 2 s, 10 s)) **or no first segment in 20 s** restarts FFmpeg behind the same URL — the stuck one **SIGKILLed at once** (`SupervisedProcess.kill`: blocked on a stalled input it ignored SIGTERM for the whole 3 s grace); an FFmpeg that ends is restarted; one that ends before any output fails the session as `encoderFailed` when it re-encodes, else after two; restarts wait 0.5, 1, 2 s.
  - **A recent refusal** (within 20 s) explains an FFmpeg that ended: the session fails with it, except "unreachable", which is retried. **FFmpeg's last words are read before any decision**: its exit can be noticed first, and Details needs them.
  - **Continuous:** an FFmpeg per request of the TV (a second request takes over). **The stream owns its socket** (ADR-010): it is written on the TV's own connection, whose close is seen at once. An `HttpResponse` held back by the TV's pace never noticed the TV leaving; only the 10 s quiet rule caught it, in the test written for this. Quiet for 10 s → ended; FFmpeg's end → a clean end, which the TV plays out before saying FINISHED; `renew` → a new token, the old URL a 404.
  - **5 restarts or new URLs within 2 minutes fail the session**, with FFmpeg's last warnings.
  - Stopping closes the session's input first (on a one-connection source the next session needs it now), then FFmpeg, its URL and its folder.
- **Folders:** `<cache>/relay/<pid>/<session>-<n>`. `bootstrap()` runs `sweepRelayFolders` after the process sweep: a folder whose app no longer runs is deleted; another running copy's and this run's are kept. **Checked on the real app:** a planted stale folder deleted at launch, and logged.
- **The fake receiver plays** (decision 8, `FakePlayback`, `FakeWatch`): it fetches what LOAD names as the TV does — the HLS playlist polled from the live edge and every new segment checked with ffprobe (codec, height, channels and **frames**: the HEVC failure above sent a header that named its streams with nothing behind it, and the first version of the check passed it), a continuous stream read from `Range: bytes=0-`; requests carry `Origin: https://www.gstatic.com`, and an answer without CORS is dropped. PLAYING once the first check passes; BUFFERING after three segment lengths with nothing new; IDLE/ERROR on a playlist gone unreadable or numbered backwards; FINISHED after an end. **Device profiles:** `FakeDevice.tv4k`, `chromecastHd` (1080p, H.264 only), `tvOnHdLink` (refuses above 1080p with a bare LOAD_FAILED, as docs/04 describes). The CLI has `--ffprobe` and `--device`.
- **CI:** the media samples are generated before the unit tests now, with H.264 1080i and MPEG-2 576i added (5.7 s here with 4.4), so the relay's tests run there with the system FFmpeg 4.4; the 4K samples aren't generated, and their tests skip themselves. All 30 real-FFmpeg relay tests pass here with 4.4 too (`RELAY_FFMPEG_DIR=/usr/bin`).
- **Measured** (`test/tools/cast_relay_measure_test.dart`, tag `benchmark`; the fake panel and the "TV" in processes of their own; 30 s each):

  | | |
  |---|---|
  | The UI isolate's longest pause while HEVC 4K + E-AC-3 passes through the relay | **7.1 ms** (99th percentile 1.16 ms); with nothing relayed 2.8 ms |
  | H.264 1080p50 + AAC copied into HLS | **FFmpeg 1.6 %** of one core; the app's process (proxy, server, idle UI) **4.2 %** |
  | H.264 1080p25 copied, AC-3 → AAC | FFmpeg 11.1 %; the process 3.1 % |
  | HEVC 4K (≈ 20 Mbps) copied, continuous, E-AC-3 → AAC | FFmpeg 12.3 %; the process 4.6 % |

  The process's share was 9.8 % at 1080p50 before each chunk's HTTP frame was written around its bytes instead of copying them into a new list. The plan's proposed budget for step 8, "relay copy of H.264 1080p50 ≤ 5 % of one core", is met by FFmpeg; FFmpeg and the app's side together are 5.8 %.
- **Exit criterion 2, as a test** (`relay_kill_test.dart`, Linux): a `dart run` process runs the relay's isolate as the app does, FFmpeg relaying a fake-panel channel (HLS, and continuous with a TV reading), and gets SIGKILL. FFmpeg ends by itself in about 3 s (its input or its output was the dead app); the next launch's sweep kills nothing and leaves no PID file and no folder.
- **The matrix without the TV** (`relay_cast_test`, `relay_faults_test`, `relay_refusals_test`: probed through the proxy, planned by `planCast`, relayed in the isolate, loaded with the app's own Cast client on the fake receiver, which checks what arrives):
  - H.264 1080p50 + AAC (HLS, both copied), H.264 + AC-3 (to AAC), H.264 1080i + MP2, HEVC 1080p (continuous), HEVC 4K + E-AC-3 (continuous), HEVC to a device set to "HEVC: No" (libx264), MPEG-2 576i (re-encoded and deinterlaced), 4K to a TV on a 1080p link (refused, as the TV does: step 6 learns), the provider's own HLS, a one-connection source.
  - **Ridden through:** a drop, a cut, a stall (restart), an 8 s slow start, an expiring redirect, a drop on the continuous stream.
  - **Failed with docs/03's words:** HTTP 401 (auth), 404 (offline), 500 (server), a full account (connection limit, after its retries).
  - **Told:** the codec switch.
  - The TV leaving a continuous stream; a renew after it; the relay closed with a session on.
- **For step 6:** `castRelayProvider` is ready; nothing in the app starts a cast yet. The coordinator listens to `events` right after `start`; LOADs after `ready`; renews on a continuous stream's FINISHED (counted); on `CastRelayStreamsChanged` or facts that differ, plans again; on `encoderFailed` calls `detectAgain()` then `after()`; classifies a refusal with `classifyStreamFailure(status, body)`; adds `connections` to its own count (local playback, the TV's direct connection).
- **Tests: 156 new app tests** (and the measurement, skipped by default):
  - unit (131): FFmpeg's log reader on 8.1's and 4.4's own lines, playlist rewriting and reading, the command lines, the proxy against scripted providers (22), the server (27), the supervisor's every rule with a scripted FFmpeg behind the real process supervisor (24), the program watch (on FFmpeg's own MPEG-TS in random chunks, misaligned, and 2,000 random runs), the folders' sweep, `AppLog.forward`, the app side's refusals;
  - with real FFmpeg (25): the matrix above, the faults, the refusals, the SIGKILL.
  - The fake receiver: 13 new (32 in all), with FFmpeg 8.1 and 4.4.
  - **Stability:** the relay's seven unit files 8 of 8 with eight copies running at once (124 each); the real-FFmpeg ones 3 of 3 three at once. **2,791 app tests** (15 skipped) under `TZ=UTC`; the fake provider's 141; `app_launch_test` under xvfb.

### The cast coordinator (step 6, 2026-10-03)
- **Step 5 approved** (the user: "continue to the next steps, no living room tv yet till I tell u we have it", 2026-10-03). **The TV runs wait** until the user says the TV is back; nothing in this step was cast to it, and nothing played from the user's provider.
- **CI after the step 5 push** (run 36998211274): Linux green, Windows 4 failures, fixed in their own commit (6ebff8e):
  - `relayArguments`' HLS test expected POSIX paths; the product joins them with the system's separator, so the test does too.
  - **Windows refuses a closed loopback port only after about 2 s** (it sends the SYN again): the proxy test's 2 s connect wait read that as a timeout (now 5 s; the product waits 8 s), and the runtime test's closed provider let the 0.8 s first-segment rule fire first (its provider now hangs up without answering, which is instant everywhere and just as unreachable).
  - The sync cancel test's 30 s timeout came back (intermittent on Windows since Phase 4; gone on three runs after the write-ahead log). Its reason is still unknown, so the test now notes each phase with `printOnFailure`, and **`tools/ci/failed_tests.dart` keeps a failure's whole first paragraph** (Expected, Actual, Which: "Expected: [" told nothing) **and a timed-out test's last printed lines**.
- **Domain** (`lib/features/casting/domain/`):
  - `CastCoordinator` (`cast_coordinator.dart`): `connect(device)`, `disconnect()` (Stop casting), `playHere()`, and as `RemotePlayback` `play`, `retry`, `seek`, `setPaused`; `setVolume`, `setMuted`; `state`/`states`, `timeline`/`timelines`, `notices`; `shutdown()` for quitting.
  - `CastingState` (`casting_state.dart`, freezed): `CastPhase` (off, connecting, idle, preparing, playing, ended, failed), the device, the item, the plan, paused, buffering, reconnecting (the pill), the volume, and `CastProblem` (device unreachable, receiver refused, the stream — with docs/03's `PlaybackProblem` —, this computer unreachable, the device can't play it, can't re-encode, the relay, nothing to play). `CastNotice` for step 7's toasts: `CastSessionClosed` (the remote, another app with its name, lost), `CastPlanChanged` (a quiet fallback: before and after), `CastStoppedOnDevice`.
  - `CastItems` (`cast_items.dart`): a `Playable` to its facts key, the planner's `CastSourceInfo` (HLS, a User-Agent of its own, a file's extension), the relay's `CastUpstreamSource` (built through `StreamResolver` on every connection) and the TV's metadata ("201 · Arena Sports 1" and the programme on now; a movie and its year; an episode, its series and number; the logo, poster or still). `CastPictures`, the cached copy of a picture.
  - `learnFromRefusal` (`cast_learning.dart`), pure.
- **The playback coordinator stays the one owner** (docs/03, decision 2):
  - `castStarted(remote)` hands over what plays (`PlaybackHandover`: the item, a file's place — saved —, the player's `StreamInfo` and tracks when it had a picture) **after closing the laptop's stream**; from then on `playLive`/`playVod`/`retry`/`seek`/`setPaused` go to the cast and the state is the new **`PlaybackCasting(item, deviceName)`** (Live TV still sees its channel). **`stop()` while casting does nothing:** screens stop what they showed when they're left, and only Stop casting ends a cast. `castShows(item)` follows what the cast shows; `castEnded()` → idle, nothing plays here by itself.
  - **`SourceConnections`** (`source_connections.dart`): each holder's connections per source — the player, the cast (the relay's proxy from `castRelay.connections`, plus 1 while the TV reads the provider directly), and Phase 8's downloads. The player's open waits up to 5 s for room on the source (after Stop casting or Play here, the relay's connection may still be closing), then opens anyway (a refusal is the watchdog's); the cast's probe and direct play wait the same way.
  - `ResolvedStream.customUserAgent`: the source or its line sets its own (never direct, docs/04 rule 1).
- **A session:** `connect` → the hand-over → the device's profile read (its HEVC setting, what it learned) → **`encoders()` and the join run side by side** → `markUsed` once joined (the device is kept from its first cast). **`CastReceivers.join` gained `onConnected(localAddress)`**, said as soon as the TLS connection is up: **the relay listens on that address and makes its first segments while the TV launches its receiver** (the plan's overlap of the 3–6 s LAUNCH with the relay's ~4 s); the LOAD waits for both. A join that fails is `failed` (Try again joins again, Play here plays on the laptop), and a relay started for it is stopped.
- **A play:** the facts by decision 4 (the hand-over's player facts and audio track, then the run's memory, then ffprobe through the proxy, its input closed once read); `planCast` with the device's profile, Settings → Casting (`castSettingsProvider`, defaults until step 7) and the preferred languages; a plan that re-encodes with nothing to re-encode with fails at once. Direct: a fresh URL (a token may expire), the LOAD with a file's place as `currentTime`. Relay: `start` (with the best encoder for a re-encode, a file's place as `startAt`), events listened to at once, the LOAD on `ready`. **The TV's picture:** the artwork cache's copy (fetched if need be), served by the relay's `/f/` route on the TV's address, typed from its first bytes (JPEG, PNG, WebP; the cache's files have no extension); the LOAD goes without it after 3 s. Zapping stops the old relay session (its provider connection first) before the new one starts.
- **Following the TV:** the media this cast loaded only — matched by its media session, or by its URL until the LOAD's answer names the session (an older load's news, an IDLE/INTERRUPTED, is ignored). PLAYING → playing (**PLAYING also proves the TV reached the stream**); PAUSED; BUFFERING shown, never a stall (docs/04); IDLE/FINISHED: a live stream is loaded again (a continuous one renewed first, ADR-004), a file cut short (more than 10 s before its length) carries on from where it was, a file at its end is `ended` and saved at its length; **IDLE/CANCELLED** (the TV's remote): the session stays, nothing plays, a notice; IDLE/ERROR and a LOAD_FAILED (one refusal, however many answers) → learning. The link: reconnecting → the pill; ended by the device (its remote closing the receiver, another app — "started YouTube" —, lost) → the session ends with a notice.
- **Learning** (docs/04), when the refusal comes **within 10 s of a direct LOAD or 15 s of a relayed one**, in this order: a direct play → the relay for the source from then on; **a picture sent taller than 1080p** (the plan's output, so a re-encode kept at 2160p is caught too) → 1080p; a copied picture codec other than H.264 → refused; an interlaced picture copied → deinterlaced; **a copied sound other than AAC** (MP3, Dolby passed through) → converted. Saved to the device (`setLearned`), planned again — a plan equal to the refused one fails ("can't play") —, a `CastPlanChanged` notice, the old relay session stopped and the new plan carried out. At most 4 lessons a play. **A refusal later than that:** a live stream that played is loaded again; a file fails with Try again.
- **Following the relay:** `CastRelayFetched` (and PLAYING) cancel the **10 s "couldn't reach this computer"** rule, which otherwise fails the cast (the TV is told to stop). `CastRelayOpened`: remembered in the facts lookup; **planned again only when the picture's codec or height or the sound's codecs differ** (FFmpeg reports fewer fields than ffprobe, and a field it leaves out mustn't change the plan) **and the new plan does something else** (delivery, picture or sound, a bit rate apart). `CastRelayStreamsChanged` (a codec switch): forgotten, probed again on a fresh connection after the old session let go of its own, planned again, a new LOAD. `CastRelayFailed`: a re-encode tries the next encoder (`detectAgain`, then `after`; at most 4), anything else fails with docs/03's words (`classifyStreamFailure` on the provider's status and first bytes; unreachable → network; unresolved → unavailable; the relay's own connections → the connection limit; FFmpeg and the budget → the relay).
- **Automatic LOADs are budgeted:** a live stream the TV dropped, a codec switch, a file cut short — 3 within 2 minutes, then the cast fails. The relay's own restart budget (5 in 2 minutes) counts its renews too.
- **One attempt at a time:** every new attempt at what plays (a re-LOAD, a new plan, a seek, a codec switch, the next encoder) **bumps the cast's generation**; work of an older one stops counting after each wait, and a relay session started for it is stopped at once. (Found while writing the codec switch test: a TV error and the switch arriving together could each start a session and leave one running.)
- **Files** (decision 1): the place is the TV's time plus where the relay started, moved on by a clock between the TV's reports; a seek of a direct file is the TV's own, of a relayed one a `renew(startAt:)` and a new LOAD, "Preparing…" meanwhile; the place saved every 10 s while it plays, on pause, after a seek, on Stop casting, on a failure, and at the end; Play here continues it on the laptop from the TV's place.
- **Sleep** (decision 7): held while a cast prepares or plays, let go otherwise. `SleepInhibitor` (`lib/core/platform/`), `DbusSleepInhibitor` (`lib/data/platform/`): **the desktop portal's `Inhibit` with suspend and idle (flags 4 | 8) and the reason "Casting to Living Room TV"**, let go by closing its request; **logind's `Inhibit("sleep:idle", "block")`** when there is no portal, let go by closing its descriptor; false and a log line when neither answers. **Checked on this laptop (GNOME):** `gnome-session-inhibit --list` shows "Casting to Living Room TV (test) (suspend, idle)" while held, and nothing after. Windows: `SetThreadExecutionState(ES_CONTINUOUS | ES_SYSTEM_REQUIRED)`, **not tried**. **`dbus` 0.7.15 becomes a direct dependency** (ADR-002 style: pure Dart, Canonical's, MPL-2.0, already in the lock through `bonsoir_linux`; it also gives the tests an in-process bus).
- **Quitting:** the window now asks first (`setPreventClose`): closing runs the quit tasks — the cast's `shutdown` (the TV goes home; 3 s at most), the relay's `close`, every supervised process's `stopAll`, only for what ran — for at most 4 s, writes the log out, then destroys the window. **Checked on the release build under Xvfb (X11),** its window asked to close the way a window manager asks (`WM_DELETE_WINDOW`): the quit ran (1 ms, nothing being cast), the log was written out, and the app exited with code 0, 50 ms after the close — no crash on exit, window_manager #585's case. (A cast on at quit time is the unit tests' `disconnect`; step 7's walk quits with one on.)
- **The Windows firewall:** `relayFirewallNoticeProvider` is called once, before the first relay start; null until step 7 gives it the dialog (Windows only).
- **Found by the tests:** the relay's file route typed its answer by the file's own name, so a cached picture (no extension) went out as `application/octet-stream`; it now types by the name it's served under.
- **Tests: 108 new app tests.**
  - The coordinator on fakes (58): the session (idle, a play, zapping, the zap back not probed again, Cast while watching with the player's facts and audio track, the relay before the TV's LAUNCH ends, a join that fails and Try again, a receiver that won't start, Stop casting, Play here, a screen's stop, another app, the pill, lost, the remote's stop, pause, the volume, the picture, the firewall notice once, sleep, the connections); the TV and the relay failing (never fetched, fetched in time, a 401, the connection limit, the budget, a relay that won't start, Try again, a stream that can't be built, a probe refused with a 404, a probe that times out, nothing to play, no encoder, the next encoder); learning (4K at once, HEVC on IDLE/ERROR, learned before, nothing to learn, one refusal from two answers, late errors then giving up); direct plays (HLS, its fallback, a User-Agent of its own); live ends (a continuous renew, a renew that fails, the budget, a codec switch, FFmpeg's facts); files (from a place, a seek, pause, saving, the end, cut short, a direct MP4 and its own seek, Stop casting saves, Play here).
  - `learnFromRefusal` (9), `CastItems` (9), the playback coordinator's side (10), `SourceConnections` (4), the inhibitor on an in-process D-Bus with a fake portal and a fake logind, descriptor passing included (4), the relay's pictures (5).
  - **Through the app, real except the screens and the TV** (`cast_e2e_*_test.dart`: the coordinators, the relay in its isolate with FFmpeg, ffprobe, our Cast client, the fake panel, the fake TV checking what arrives): a channel, a zap, Stop casting on a one-connection panel (never 2 streams; the picture fetched from the relay as an image; no FFmpeg, no folder left); Cast while watching (no probe); the panel's own HLS direct, refused by the TV (no CORS), then relayed; HEVC to an H.264-only device (learned, re-encoded with libx264, playing); 4K to a TV on a 1080p link (1080p learned); a continuous stream whose FFmpeg is killed (FINISHED, a new LOAD, playing); a codec switch (probed and planned again, then HEVC playing); a Matroska movie from 1:00, a seek to 5:00, Stop casting (its place saved); an MP4 direct, refused, then relayed.
  - **Stability:** the new unit files 8 of 8 with eight copies at once; the end-to-end files 3 of 3 with three at once. **2,899 app tests** (15 skipped) under `TZ=UTC`, about 2 min 40 s; the fake receiver's 32, the fake provider's 141; `app_launch_test` under Xvfb.
- **Measured with the fakes** (the fake TV launches in 0.1 s; Living Room TV takes 3–6 s): from Cast to the TV playing, an H.264 channel over HLS **5.9 s** (the join 0.1 s, ffprobe 1.7 s, the relay ready 4.0 s, PLAYING 50 ms after the LOAD); HEVC as one continuous stream **7.3 s** (the fake TV's own check of the first 256 KB took 5.5 s of it). With the laptop's facts (Cast while watching) the probe's 1.7 s goes. Step 8 measures on the TV against the proposed ≤ 8 s (receiver launched) and ≤ 5 s (running).
- **Not done in this step:** the cast through the app to the TV (it waits for the TV), and everything step 7 shows: the picker, the casting view, the bar, the toasts, Settings → Casting, the firewall dialog.

### The casting UI (step 7, 2026-10-03)
- **The TV runs still wait** for the user ("no living room tv yet till I tell u we have it", 2026-10-03): nothing in this step was cast to Living Room TV, and nothing played from the user's provider. Everything below ran against the fake panel and the fake receiver.
- **The canvas's frames, built** (the canvas read before building; goldens at 1280 × 800 and 1920 × 1080 checked against it): the device picker (`CastPicker`), the casting view (`CastingView`) and the casting bar, with sketches A (a movie), B (Live TV while casting), C (Settings → Casting), D (the view's states) and E (Add by address).
- **Where things are** (`lib/features/casting/presentation/`): `cast_picker.dart`, `add_cast_device_dialog.dart`, `cast_help.dart`, `casting_view.dart` and `casting_view_state.dart` (whether the view is open, the state and timeline as providers), `cast_preview_card.dart` (sketch B), `cast_settings_section.dart`, `cast_actions.dart` (what Cast buttons, C and menus do), `cast_text.dart` (every sentence), `cast_shell_slots.dart` (the shell's cast button, bar and overlay; the toasts; the Windows firewall dialog). The shell gained three slots a feature fills, as sources fill theirs (`castShellOverrides`, in `bootstrap()` beside `sourceShellOverrides`).
- **Design system:** `AppCastTokens` (the view's 64 px inset and gap, 360 px artwork with a 28 px radius, 640 px text column, 48 px controls, the picker's 540 px width, the device tile, the bar's 64 px height and 40 px logo) and the text styles `castTitle`, `castKicker`, `qualityTag`; `AppIconButton.filled` and `danger`; `AppButtonVariant.dangerOutline` (Stop casting); `AppSpinner` (an icon under reduce motion); `AppSlider(compact:)` (no bubble row); `QualityTag` (ORIGINAL QUALITY / CONVERTED AUDIO / TRANSCODED, tinted) and `StreamQuality.sentenceLabel`; the `CastingBar` rebuilt to the canvas.
- **UX decisions taken while building** (the user's standing rule: pick the best experience and record it):
  - **The casting view is an overlay on the content pane, not a page:** the rail, the top bar and the bar stay; the screen under it stays as it was (off stage, out of the focus order, its tickers off), so **Esc goes back exactly there** while the cast goes on, and the focus returns to the content pane. It opens when Cast moves something to the TV, on Enter on a channel while casting, from the bar, and from Live TV's card. **The full-screen player hands over to it**: Cast in the OSD, or the player opened while casting, closes the player and opens the view.
  - **Its keys:** ↑/↓ and PageUp/PageDown change channel (live; the channel shows at once, the TV loads it 350 ms after the keys rest, as zapping does here); ←/→ seek a file by 10 s (600 ms after the keys rest: one renew for several presses); Space pauses a file; M mutes; Esc goes back. **Live has no pause** (the canvas has none; a paused live relay would run out of segments). The view takes the focus when it opens, so its keys work at once; Tab reaches its controls.
  - **A TV whose volume is fixed** (Living Room TV, through an AV receiver) shows the slider disabled with "This TV's volume is set with its own remote"; M still sends the mute (whether Living Room TV heeds it is step 8's to see).
  - **On a wide window** the artwork and the text stay together in the middle of the card (the canvas's widths, capped), rather than pinned left with half the card empty.
  - **The bar:** the programme (or the title) and "Casting to Living Room TV · Original quality", or what is happening instead ("Connecting…", "Preparing…", "Couldn't play", "Reconnecting to Living Room TV…"); the channel's logo or its monogram; Pause only for files; Stop casting as a red icon button. A click or Enter on its text opens the view.
  - **Live TV while casting (sketch B):** the preview shows the card instead of a picture (what plays on the TV, its programme and times, "Enter on a channel plays it on the TV.", Open casting view, Stop casting); **a click only chooses a channel** and the card offers "Play on Living Room TV"; Enter plays it there and opens the view. The channel menu gained Cast….
  - **The picker:** what will be cast under its title ("Arena Sports 1 · Continental Cup · Semi-final", or "Choose a device, then play something."); "Looking for devices on your network…" with a spinner, and **after 10 s with none, "No devices found yet. Still looking…" and the troubleshooting list open**; rows of icon, name, model line (what is known or learned: "Chromecast · 1080p · H.264 only") and status (Available, Casting, Not answering, Busy · playing music — busy and not answering dimmed, still choosable); the list is one Tab stop with the first device focused; Enter casts. While casting, a "Casting to Living Room TV" row with Stop casting heads it. A build without FFmpeg and a computer on no network say so in a banner (Enter then casts nothing).
  - **Casting from the picker** moves what plays here to the TV (the hand-over) and opens the view; with nothing playing, the session starts and waits ("Choose a channel, a movie or an episode, and it plays on Living Room TV."). A details page's Cast with a session on plays on the TV at once.
  - **Add by address (sketch E):** one field (`192.168.1.60`, `192.168.1.60:8009`, a name, IPv6 in brackets); "Looking at 192.168.1.60…" with a spinner, then "Found "Bedroom" (Chromecast)." and the device kept; "Type an address like 192.168.1.60.", "Nothing answered at 192.168.1.99.", ""Kitchen speaker" is a speaker: it has no screen to cast to."
  - **The toasts:** a quiet fallback says what changed ("Living Room TV couldn't fetch it directly, so this computer sends it", "Now re-encoding the video for Living Room TV", "Now converting the audio for Living Room TV", "Now sending 1080p to Living Room TV"); an end the user didn't ask for says why ("Living Room TV started YouTube", "Casting stopped on Living Room TV", "Lost the connection to Living Room TV" — the only one in the error tone), and "Stopped on Living Room TV" for the TV's remote.
  - **Settings → Casting (sketch C):** "Applies to the next thing you cast."; DEVICES: each kept device with its model and address ("Added by address · 192.168.1.60"), **Forget for every device** (the plan said for manual ones; a found one is forgotten too — its HEVC choice and what it learned go — and kept again at its next cast), HEVC Automatic / Yes / No, "Learned: 1080p at most · no HEVC" with Reset; then Dolby passthrough, Low-latency mode, Smooth interlaced (each with its sentence); "Your TV reaches this computer on ports 38400–38499." with Firewall help. No device kept yet says how one is.
  - **Windows:** before the first relay, once, "Windows will ask about the firewall" says why the prompt follows and to allow private networks (`relayFirewallNoticeProvider`, Windows only; remembered in settings).
  - The top bar's button says Cast, or Casting (the connected icon) during a session; **C** opens the picker from anywhere but a text field.
- **Domain changes:** `connect` returns once joined (what moved to the TV plays on in the background, so the view opens at once on "Preparing the stream…"); `PlaybackCoordinator.castDeviceName`; a cast that ends leaves the laptop idle in every case. `CastSettings` is stored (`cast.settings`, `CastSettingsController`); the relay's ports are one constant (`cast_relay_ports.dart`, plain Dart: the relay's isolate can't import Flutter).
- **Found by the keyboard walk, fixed — two relay bugs that would have broken every relayed movie on the TV:**
  - **A TV that holds back was taken for a stall.** A TV reads a continuous stream only as far ahead as its buffer holds, and not at all while paused; FFmpeg copies a file far faster than it plays, so the relay waits on the TV for tens of seconds at a time. The continuous stream's quiet rule (10 s) ended the stream then, and the proxy's (8 s) cut FFmpeg's input: the TV would play out its buffer, say FINISHED early, and the cast would carry on, renew and fail within minutes; a pause longer than ~10 s did the same. **Both rules now run only while their reader reads** (paused with the stream, re-armed when it resumes).
  - **Stopping it then took the 3 s grace:** FFmpeg blocked on its output ignores SIGTERM. Stopping, renewing (a seek) or replacing a continuous stream now **ends the TV's connection first**, so FFmpeg exits on the broken pipe at once. Stop casting with the TV's buffer full: 3.0 s before, immediate after.
  - **Why the tests missed it:** the fake receiver read a continuous stream as fast as it came. **It now reads like a TV's buffer** (30 s ahead, again below 10 s, nothing while paused; each fragment's `tfdt` says how far the bytes go — `Fmp4Clock`), and plays out what it holds before FINISHED. The new end-to-end test pauses a relayed movie for 14 s and fails without the fix (FFmpeg gone); the unit tests hold a raw TV socket back with 32 MB queued.
  - Also: the fake's scratch folders (the head of each stream it checks) were left in `/tmp` when a test closed it mid-watch (98 here); cancelling now removes them.
- **Tests:**
  - widget tests: the picker (10: devices and what is known of them, nothing found and the help, no FFmpeg, no network, C / focus / Esc, Enter casts with the hand-over, Stop casting from it, Add by address answered and refused three ways, Troubleshoot), the view (9: live with the badge and the bar, ↓ zaps after the keys rest, connecting without holding the relay, reconnecting, failed with Details / Try again / Play here, a file's seek and pause and the bar's own Pause, Esc and back from the bar, Stop casting, M), Live TV and the player while casting (3), Settings → Casting (5), the toasts and the Windows dialog (4), the bar and the quality tag (2 new);
  - goldens (6): the view at both sizes, the picker at both sizes, a movie (sketch A), "couldn't reach this computer" (sketch D); the controls sheet, the player's OSD and failure card and four Settings pages recorded again (the Cast button, the components);
  - the relay (3) and the fake receiver (3) for the fixes above, one end-to-end test;
  - **the keyboard walk** (`integration_test/cast_walk_test.dart`, the plan's): the real app with the fake panel and the fake TV, keys only — Live TV → Enter: full screen → C → nothing found → Add device by IP address → the fake TV's address → Enter on it: the channel moves to the TV and the player hands over to the view → the TV plays it from the relay → ↓: the next channel there → Esc: Live TV's card and the bar → C: the picker, casting → Esc → Ctrl+4: a movie → Play: on the TV (directly, refused without CORS, then relayed, with its toast) → → ×3: on from 0:30 → Tab to Stop casting: its place kept, the TV home, no FFmpeg left, the panel's connection closed, nothing playing here. Discovery runs on no network browser (CI has none, and it must never find a real TV). 40 s; 4 of 4 here, one of them as CI runs it (FFmpeg 4.4, no picture). `PanelApp` takes extra overrides and quits a cast as `bootstrap()` does; `WALK_LOG=1` prints a walk's log.
- **Noted for step 8:** a relayed file that starts at a place (a seek, a resume) is LOADed with the movie's whole length while its stream starts at the TV's 0:00: what the TV's own screen shows of the time is to be checked on Living Room TV (the app's own place is right).

### Step 8, before the TV: the matrix runner (2026-10-03)
- **The TV runs still wait** (the user: "continue", then "stop at a proper point and do the hand off"; the TV hasn't been said to be back). Nothing was cast to Living Room TV.
- **`test/tools/cast_matrix_tv_test.dart`** (tag `real_cast`, skipped by default) runs the plan's step 8 matrix **through the app's casting stack** — the fake panel on one connection, listening on the address the TV reaches this computer on; the relay in its isolate; ffprobe; the encoders this laptop really has (`FfmpegEncoderDetector`); our Cast client; the coordinators — so the session with the user watching is one command: `CAST_HOST=192.168.1.155 flutter test --tags real_cast --run-skipped test/tools/cast_matrix_tv_test.dart` (about 20 minutes; `CAST_HOLD` seconds a row, 30; `CAST_ROWS=1,4,9` for some rows).
- **Its 16 rows:** docs/04's twelve (H.264 1080p50 + AAC with a zap there and back; 1080p + AC-3; 1080i + MP2; HEVC 2160p + E-AC-3 as one continuous fMP4; HEVC 1080p with the TV set to "HEVC: No"; 4K on a 1080p HDMI link; MPEG-2 576i; a codec change mid-stream; a provider drop every 60 s through HLS and through a continuous stream; the relay's FFmpeg killed mid-stream; an 8 s slow start; Cast while the laptop plays it on a one-connection source — the laptop's "player" really reads the stream, so the panel counts it), then the plan's movies (an MP4 straight to the TV if it can, with seeks to 1:00 and 5:00; a Matroska movie relayed from 1:00, a seek, Stop casting and resume from the kept place) and the TV's remote (Pause, Play, Back). **Rows 6 and 16 ask something of the user first** (Input Signal Plus off; the remote in hand) and run only when named.
- **Each row** prints what to watch the TV for, starts from a clean device profile and panel, and records: Cast to playing, zap and seek times, the plans in order, new LOADs, relay restarts, refusals learned from, the toasts, and the most provider connections at once (more than one fails the row). A failing row doesn't stop the run. The summary goes to `build/real_cast_run/matrix.md`, the whole log to `matrix.log`.
- **`CAST_HOST=fake` runs every row against the fake receiver** (the 1080p-link row on `FakeDevice.tvOnHdLink`, the remote's presses done by the fake), as the runner's own check: **16 of 16 pass** (about 5 minutes, holds of 4 s, drops every 10 s). On the fake, which launches in 0.1 s: Cast to playing 3.5–7.1 s (the relay's ~4 s of first segments, ffprobe, and on the first cast the encoder detection's 3 s), zaps 4.1 and 5.8 s, the 1080p-link row learned and re-encoding in 16 s, the killed FFmpeg's stream back in 4.0 s, the movie's seeks and resume right (kept 5:01, resumed at 5:01).
- **Found while writing it:** "Playing on" is logged once a play, not after a re-LOAD (the runner waits for "loading it again", then playing); flutter_test answers HTTP with 400 unless `HttpOverrides.global = null`.
- **To see on the TV, besides each row's picture and sound:** the cast-start budgets (≤ 8 s with the receiver launched; ≤ 5 s running — the zaps stand in for it), zapping ≤ 6 s; a relayed movie paused for a minute (the backpressure fix); what the TV's own screen shows of the time for a movie started at a place; whether the fixed-volume TV heeds mute; whether the TV plays the panel's MP4 directly without CORS (the fake refuses it, as a browser would for HLS).
- **Steps 6 and 7 approved** (the user: "all approved based on ur recommendations", 2026-10-04), with step 7's UX decisions as listed above. **The TV runs still wait:** the laptop was on 192.168.26.x. Phase 8 goes ahead meanwhile (ADR-015, decision 1); this ADR is accepted at the TV sitting.

### The first run on a real TV (2026-10-13, the Bedroom TV, `test/tools/cast_tv_test.dart`)
- **It found that no cast through the app could have played on a real TV.** The Cast client took `Socket.address` for the connection's local address; in dart:io that is the address the socket was connected **to**. So "this computer's address" was the TV's own (`192.168.1.231`), the file server had no such address to listen on, and a relay's URL would have pointed the TV at itself. The fake TV listens on 127.0.0.1, where both addresses are the same: every test since step 2 passed, the matrix against the fake TV included.
- **Fixed** (`lib/data/cast/cast_transport.dart`): dart:io has no local address for a connected socket, so it is the one of this computer's interfaces that shares the most leading bits with the TV's (`pickLocalAddress`: a home network's addresses differ in their last bits, a virtual bridge's sooner; loopback stays loopback). Unit tests with this laptop's own three addresses.
- **Seen after the fix, on the Bedroom TV** (a Chromecast, 192.168.1.231; the user allowed the short check, twice, each time asked first): the receiver launched in 2.4 s, the 10-minute MP4 sample was **playing 0.7 s after LOAD** with Range requests served from 192.168.1.254, PAUSE and a SEEK to 1:00 worked (playing again 0.7 s after it), STOP closed the receiver.
- **Still never run on a real TV:** the matrix (rows 1–23) — the relay through FFmpeg, HEVC as fragmented MP4, the fallbacks, subtitles, the MP4 made beside the relay. The user chose the short check only. What this run says about them: a direct file plays; nothing yet about the relay.
- **The two real-TV tools have no default TV now.** It was a shared TV's address: a run without `CAST_HOST` would have cast to it. And `cast_tv_test` closes the receiver whatever fails: the first run threw before its `try`, and left the TV on the receiver's idle screen until a second connection stopped it.
- **The lesson, again:** a fake that shares this computer's address can't show which address is whose. A fake on another address of this machine would not have shown it either; only a second device does.

## ADR-015 · 2026-10-04 · Accepted 2026-10-13 without the TV sitting — Phase 8 downloads and library choices
**Accepted on the user's word (2026-10-13: "the 7 and 8 are apporved"), with the library matrix on the real TV (rows 17–23) not run,** for the reason ADR-014's header gives. Open until the TV is reachable: whether the real receiver fetches and shows a side-loaded WebVTT track and how it styles it, whether it takes `EDIT_TRACKS_INFO` while it buffers, and whether it plays and seeks in the MP4 made for it.

**Decision:** the Phase 8 choices below, organized by step, as docs/plans/phase-8-downloads-and-library.md proposed and the user approved on 2026-10-04 ("all approved based on ur recommendations") — **the recommendation in all twelve decisions and the eight sketches**, with the departures recorded where they happen.

The twelve, in short: **1** Phase 8 starts while Phase 7's TV sitting waits. **2** downloads move their bytes in an isolate of their own (dart:io), the queue's rules stay in the app's isolate, and the URL is asked for on every connection. **3** downloads yield through `SourceConnections`: the newest on a source lets go at once when the player or a cast needs room, and they come back 10 s after the source is free. **4** an item that answers with HLS is saved by FFmpeg through a loopback proxy, with no resume. **5** a download keeps a copy of its details (`library_items.details_json`) and `poster.jpg`. **6** a two-pass scanner as a guarded job: walk, stat, quick hash and parse first; ffprobe after; thumbnails when first shown. **7** watched folders, rescanned 3 s after the last change, with "Updates when rescanned" as the fallback. **8** every Play of a downloaded title uses the file (the resolver looks for it first); your own files are a `PlayableLibraryItem`; no reconnects on disk. **9** Enter opens a page for movies and shows, plays a video. **10** Delete goes to the trash through our own freedesktop trash spec on Linux, `SHFileOperationW` on Windows, else a second confirmation. **11** offline from NetworkManager over D-Bus (Windows' Network List Manager), the app's own failures as the fallback. **12** library files cast directly when the TV takes them; otherwise the continuous relay at once and an MP4 made beside it so later seeks are native; text subtitles as WebVTT.

### Schema v9, the domain, the download folder (step 1, 2026-10-04)
- **Schema v9** (`lib/data/db/library_tables.dart`, `library.drift`): docs/09's `library_folders`, `library_items`, `downloads` and `library_fts` (title, show title; external content kept by triggers like the others, recreated on every upgrade). **Departures from docs/09's table**, each small:
  - `library_items.details_json` (decision 5); `library_items.provider_series_key` (a downloaded episode's history key is an `EpisodeRef`, which needs its series);
  - `library_items.mtime` is **milliseconds since the epoch in an integer**, not a drift date: the scanner compares it with the file's own time, and a date column's text would compare by format;
  - `library_folders.label` (the canvas names folders: "Movies HDD", "USB drive");
  - `downloads.sort_order` (docs/05's drag to reorder), `downloads.show_title` and `downloads.year` (the Downloads list shows "Glass Tide · S2 · E5 · Northbound" and "Movie · 2025" without a catalogue that may have swept the title);
  - **`favorites_local` and `watch_history_local`**, unique on `(item_type, remote_key) WHERE source_id IS NULL`: SQLite counts NULLs as distinct, so the old key allowed two rows for one local file. The migration first keeps the newest of any duplicates (nothing wrote local rows before v9).
- **The shown columns hold the user's edit.** `title`, `year`, `kind`, `show_title`, `season`, `episode` are what screens show, sort, filter and search; `user_edits_json` says what the user set, so a rescan writes only the rest, and "Use the file's name" drops it. One set of columns keeps the Library's queries and its search index simple.
- **Keys:** a folder's path is unique; an item is unique by `(folder_id, rel_path)`; a download by `(source_id, item_type, remote_key)`. Removing a folder removes its items (the files stay); removing a source removes its queue but only unlinks its downloaded files (`provider_source_id` → NULL); a removed item leaves its download row pointing nowhere.
- **The domain:** `lib/core/library/` (`LibraryKind`, `VodType`, `LibraryFolder`, `LibraryItem`, `ExternalSubtitle`, `ProviderLink`, `LibraryItemEdit`; `LibraryRepository` with `LibraryQuery` and `DeleteOutcome`), `lib/core/downloads/` (`DownloadTaskState` — not `DownloadState`, the design component's enum; `DownloadProblem`; `DownloadTask` with `progress` and `timeLeft`; `DownloadRequest`; `DownloadService`; `DownloadSettings` with `DownloadSpeedLimit`, stored under `downloads.settings`, tolerant of anything odd). The DAOs: `LibraryDao`, `DownloadsDao`.
- **The download folder:** the system's Videos folder + `IPTV Player`. On Linux `XDG_VIDEOS_DIR` is read from `user-dirs.dirs` here: `xdg_directories.getUserDirectory` runs `xdg-user-dir` synchronously, which would block the UI isolate (hard rule 2); a value equal to the home folder means turned off. Windows' Videos known folder (untried here). `bootstrap()` registers it after the first frame as the library's download folder, called "Downloads"; a new choice takes over and the folder before stays as an ordinary one. Nothing is made on disk until the first download.
- **Tests:** 36 new (the migration from every version, the v8 → v9 data cases including a duplicated local history row, the DAOs, the folder rules, the settings); **2,989 app tests** (16 skipped) under `TZ=UTC`.

### The fake panel's downloads, and the library tree (step 2, 2026-10-04)
- **The fake panel's download faults** (`tools/fake_provider/lib/vod.dart`), each per request or through `/admin/faults`: `change_etag` (a new ETag and Last-Modified on every answer, so a resume's `If-Range` never matches); `wrong_content_length` (4 KiB more than the body, then the connection closes); `size_mb` (the file padded to that many MiB with filler made as it is sent — `fakePaddingByte(offset)` says what each byte is — so the 4 GB measurement needs no 4 GB file; ranges across the sample's end and inside the padding work); `vod_as_hls` (the item answers an HLS VOD playlist; its 4 s TS segments are cut once per sample with ffmpeg into the run folder and served from `/vodhls/<sample>/`, each holding a connection slot while it is sent).
- **Fixed on the way:** the fake checked a range before `If-Range`, so a resume past the end of a changed file got a 416. HTTP checks the validator first: a mismatch is the whole file. That is what a download's resume rule (docs/09: 200 means start over) relies on.
- **`tools/media_samples/library_tree.sh <dir> <count>`**: movies (40 %; folders with posters and subtitles, release names, bracketed tags, loose files), episodes (50 %; three naming styles, nested seasons, a two-episode file per show, forced subtitles), unsorted videos (10 %; home videos and camera names; one in 25 with an embedded title), and junk on top of `count` (samples, trailers, featurettes, a hidden folder, a `.mkv.part`, a file under the minimum, `.nfo`). **Departure from docs/06's "each with a unique title tag":** every video is a copy of one of two 2-second clips with its own number appended — sizes and quick hashes still differ, and 5,000 files take 15 s instead of minutes of ffmpeg runs; only one video in 25 gets a real title tag, for the scanner's embedded-tag rule. `<dir>/.manifest.tsv` says what the scanner should make of every file, for step 4's tests. Checked: all 5,000 listed videos exist with distinct contents; ffprobe reads a clip with the number appended.
- **Tests:** the fake panel's 146 (5 new: the three byte faults, and `vod_as_hls` read end to end by ffprobe and refusing a segment with no slot); **2,989 app tests** under `TZ=UTC`.

### The download engine (step 3, 2026-10-04)
- **Built:** `lib/core/downloads/` — `download_paths.dart` (docs/09's layout; `safeName`: Windows' refused characters and control characters dropped, no trailing dots or spaces, reserved names get `_` after the stem (`LPT9_.mkv`), 120 characters a part by letters not bytes, `Untitled` for nothing; `freeName` adds ` (2)`; on Windows title parts shrink until the path fits 240), `download_runner.dart` (the runner's interface and its sendable messages); `lib/core/platform/shared_sleep.dart` (one system hold for the cast and the downloads); `lib/data/downloads/` — `HttpDownload`, `HlsDownload`, `download_isolate.dart`, `IsolateDownloadRunner`, `DbDownloadStore`, `FileDownloadFinisher`; `lib/data/library/quick_hash.dart` (size + FNV-1a 64 over the first and last 64 KB); `lib/data/platform/disk_space.dart` (`statvfs` through FFI; `GetDiskFreeSpaceExW`, untried); `lib/features/downloads/` — `DownloadQueue` (domain), `download_ports.dart`, `CatalogueDownloadTitles`, the providers. `SourceConnections.giveWay` and `changes`. `bootstrap()` starts the queue after launch and shuts it down in the quit tasks.
- **Decision 3 as built:** a holder that gives way registers with `SourceConnections.giveWay`; `room()` asks it to let go before waiting, so the player's wait ends as the download's connection closes (milliseconds), not after 5 s. The queue stops the newest run on that source (or, while it is still asking for its URL, drops it at once) and marks it `waitingForConnection`. When the other holders' count at a source falls to 0 the source stays quiet for 10 s; a zap within that keeps the downloads waiting. "Downloads paused while you watch" is told once a viewing. A download queued while the player has the source waits for the connection too.
- **docs/09's rules, as built:** resume from the `.part`'s size with `Range` + `If-Range` (ETag, else Last-Modified); 206 appends; 200 empties the `.part`; a 206 from the wrong place or a 416 that doesn't match starts over without Range (twice at most); a 416 ends it when the size matches **the size the 416 names** (a provider whose Content-Length lies, `wrong_content_length`, then still finishes, and verification judges the file). The errors: network and 5xx → 2, 4, 8, 15, 30, 60 s, then every 60 s, failed after 30 min without progress (progress resets the count); 401/403 → every download of that source paused with the account problem until resumed; a 403 whose body speaks of connections is the connection limit, as docs/03 classes it; 404/410 and a title gone from the catalogue → failed; 429 and the limit → wait 60 s; a write error → failed. Disk space: the rest of the file + 1 GB before starting and every 256 MB; short of it everything pauses with a banner until Resume all. Speed limit by pausing the read. At launch, what was running goes back in the queue from its `.part`'s size (or paused with resuming off); at quit, runs stop and flush and stay as they were.
- **Departures:**
  - **The file is forced to disk (`fsync`) every 64 MB, not 8** (and on pause and at the end): Dart's `flush()` is an `fsync`, and at 8 MB the syncs took the speed from about 1 GB/s to 580 MB/s on this laptop's SSD. A crash of the app loses nothing already written (the page cache keeps it), and the `.part`'s size is what a launch trusts; only a power cut can lose the last 64 MB, which the next launch fetches again. The database row is still written every 8 MB of progress.
  - **Cancel deletes the `.part` and takes the download off the list** (the canvas has no "canceled" section, and the title's Download button is back); `DownloadTaskState.canceled` stays for a row an older build may hold.
  - **ffprobe that can't start or times out doesn't make a download damaged:** the size check stands alone and the item has no length until the scanner probes it. Only a file ffprobe opens and can't read, or reads no length in, is damaged.
  - **No idle timeout wrapped round the body:** a check every second (or a quarter of the idle time) closes a connection quiet for 30 s; the wrapper cost a fifth of the speed at 1 GB/s.
