- **The finisher:** the size against the provider's, then ffprobe on the `.part`, then the rename (a name taken meanwhile gets a number), then `poster.jpg` from the app's picture cache (a show's poster in the show's folder), the details copy (decision 5: a movie's row and `movie_details`; an episode's and its series'), and the library item with its provider link, quick hash and length. A crash between the rename and the database finishes from the final file at the next try. A damaged file's `.part` is deleted: **no file under a final name was ever unverified.**
- **HLS (decision 4):** a playlist answer (the content type, or a body starting with `#EXTM3U`) hands the download to FFmpeg in the same isolate, reading through a `RelayProxy` there (no credentials in its arguments, each segment's connection counted); Matroska out; no resume. The fake panel's `vod_as_hls` saves end to end and ffprobe reads 9.0 s of a 9 s clip.
- **Found on the way:** awaiting a `StreamSubscription.cancel()` waits on a root-zone future, which leaves a test's fake time (the first queue tests hung on it); `SourceConnections.room()` and the queue no longer await their cancels. The finisher's ffprobe types bring Flutter in (`stream_facts.dart`), so the SIGKILL victim runs the queue with a stand-in finisher.
- **Measured** (`flutter test --tags benchmark --run-skipped test/tools/download_measure_test.dart`, the fake panel in its own process, both clients writing to the same SSD): **at network rates the app is 99.6 % of curl** (1 Gbps: 118 vs 119 MB/s; 100 Mbps: 12 vs 12 MB/s). **Over loopback, unthrottled, it is 35 % of curl** (675 MB/s vs 1.9 GB/s): a plain `HttpClient` writing to a file reaches about 0.8–1 GB/s here, so loopback measures Dart's HTTP stack, not the downloader. **For your review: docs/06's "≥ 90 % of curl" is now checked at network rates, with loopback reported.** Memory over a 4 GiB download at 1 Gbps: **+20 MB and +31 MB** at the peak in two runs (budget 30), the same in every quarter of the file — a working set, not growth; over loopback +22 MB steady after a first peak of +37–61 MB.
- **Exit criterion, as tests** (`download_kill_test.dart`, a `dart run` victim killed with SIGKILL): killed at 12 MB of a 40 MB download, nothing exists under the final name, the next launch resumes from the `.part` (it opened at the `.part`'s size) and every byte of the file is right; a playlist's FFmpeg ended with its app (its input was the dead app's proxy: gone by the first look) and the next launch's sweep left no PID file.
- **Tests:** 79 new (paths and names, the quick hash, disk space, giving way, the shared hold; the HTTP downloader against every fault of the fake panel and a scripted server; HLS; the isolate runner; the queue under fake time, 14; the data side, 11; end to end, 5; the SIGKILL pair); **3,068 app tests** (17 skipped) under `TZ=UTC`; the fake panel's 146.

### Names and the scanner (step 4, 2026-10-04)
- **Built:** `lib/data/library/` — `name_parser.dart` (`parseVideoName`), `library_scan.dart` (the guarded scan job), `library_probe.dart` (ffprobe for a library file), `library_thumbnails.dart`, `system_trash.dart`; `lib/core/library/library_media.dart` (`LibraryMedia`: length, picture, sound and subtitle tracks, the title tag, the quality line "1080p · H.264 · AC-3 5.1"); `lib/features/library/data/` — `LibraryScans` (one scan at a time, at launch, on Rescan and a folder added, and on a watched folder's changes), `DbLibraryRepository`, the providers. `bootstrap()` starts the scans after the download queue; thumbnails go in `<cache>/library/thumbnails`.
- **NameParser** (pure, never throws): `SxxEyy` with `E05`, `-E05` or `-05` for a second episode; `2x04`; a numbered file in a season folder (`Season 2`, `S02`, `Series`, `Saison`, `Staffel`, `Temporada`; `Specials` is season 0) with the show from the folder above; `[Group] Show - 04` as season 1; `Title (Year)`, `Title [Year]`, `Title.Year…`; else unsorted. The year is the last one before the release tags (`Stars 2049 (2017)`, `1917 (2019)`, `2001 A Harbour Odyssey (1968)`). **Two rules the corpus led to:** a bare year counts only in parentheses or brackets, in a dotted scene name, or followed by release tags — "Birthday at the lake 2021" stays a home video; and dots and underscores part words only in names without spaces — "Mr. Robinson's Garden" keeps its dot. A tag right after an episode's code is a tag (`S01E01.720p…` has no title). The corpus is `test_fixtures/library/names.json`: docs/09's seven and 43 more, all fictional.
- **The scan (decision 6), as built:** pass 1 walks with docs/09's skips (hidden, `.part`, under the minimum, `sample`/`trailer`/`featurette` words, and folders named Sample(s), Trailers, Featurettes, Extras, Behind the Scenes), stats every video, quick-hashes only new or changed ones, parses names, finds subtitles (`Movie.en.srt`, `Movie.eng.forced.srt`, `Movie.English.srt`; SDH/CC words ignored) and pictures (`<name>-poster.jpg`, `<name>.jpg`, `poster`/`folder`/`cover`, and a season folder's videos take the show folder's), and writes in batches (500) as one transaction each. **A row whose file is gone waits until every folder is walked:** a new file with its hash, in that folder or another, is the same file moved (its id, history, favorites, edits and hiding go with it); what nobody claims is removed at the end (its history stays, keyed by the hash). A changed file is hashed and probed again; the user's edits are never overwritten. A folder that can't be read: not available, its items "not connected" from now, removed after 30 days; back in time, as before. Pass 2 probes what has no `probe_json`, 4 at a time, 20 s each; an unreadable file is marked so and not asked again until it changes; an unsorted video without the user's edit takes its file's title tag.
- **Decision 7 as built:** `package:watcher` per available folder; changes rescan that folder 3 s after the last; `.part` and hidden files never count; a watch that fails marks the folder "Updates when rescanned" (`LibraryScans.watched`).
- **The repository:** a folder is refused when it is already in the library, inside one that is, or holds one that is (a file would be listed twice); its label is the folder's name (renamable); the download folder can't be removed. Edits write the shown columns and `user_edits_json`; none goes back to the name. **Remove from library deletes the row and remembers the file** (settings `library.removed`, by folder), so later scans leave it out; the file stays. Removing the folder forgets them. **Delete file** puts the video in the trash first — no trash for it, nothing moved, and the screen asks again (`DeleteOutcome.noTrash`) — then its subtitle files; for a download, its poster and the folders left empty, up to the download folder.
- **Decision 10 as built:** the freedesktop trash spec in Dart: the mount from `/proc/self/mountinfo`, the uid from `getuid` (FFI); the home trash for the home drive, else the drive's `.Trash/<uid>` (a real folder with the sticky bit) or `.Trash-<uid>`; `.trashinfo` with the escaped path (relative on a drive's own trash) and a local `DeletionDate`; a name already there gets `.2`. Windows: `SHFileOperationW` with undo, untried.
- **Thumbnails** (decision 6): FFmpeg at 10 % of the length (a minute's 10 % when unknown), 480 px wide, into the cache by quick hash, 2 at a time, 15 s each; one request per item at a time; kept in `thumbnail_path`.
- **Fixed on the way:** `AppFailure.fromError` wrapped an `AppFailure` thrown inside `Result.guard` as "unexpected"; it now passes through as itself.
- **Measured** (`flutter test --tags benchmark --run-skipped test/tools/library_scan_measure_test.dart`, a guarded job on a file database, three runs): **5,000 new files 41.7–45.9 s** with every one probed (budget 5 min), **the first rows after 3.1–3.3 s** (browsable while it runs), the UI isolate's longest pause **12–16 ms**; **5,000 unchanged 2.8–3.0 s** (budget 5 s), pause 6–9 ms. **For step 6:** mapping all 5,000 rows on the UI isolate takes about 30 ms, so the Library's grids need windowed queries, as the Movies grid has.
- **Tests:** 85 new (the corpus, 52; the scanner on a `library_tree.sh` tree against its manifest, with rescans, moves within and between folders, a changed file, an unplugged drive and the 30 days; the trash, 6; the scans, the repository and thumbnails, 8; the media type); **3,139 app tests** (18 skipped) under `TZ=UTC`.

### Playback of library items, offline (step 5, part 1, 2026-10-04)
- **Built:** `PlayableLibraryItem` (no source, history by quick hash) and `LocalRef` (a third `VodRef`); `ResolvedStream.local` and `subtitleFiles`; `StreamResolver.libraryFile`, and **`movie`/`episode` answer with a downloaded file first** (`downloaded: false` asks for the provider's); `PlayRequest.subtitleFiles` → mpv's `sub-files` (a path holding the list separator is left out); `PlaybackProblemKind.fileUnreadable` ("This file is missing or damaged"); local history and favorites (`WatchHistoryDao.touchLocal`/`watchLocal`/`forgetLocal`/`dismissLocal`/`recentLocal`, `FavoritesDao.addLocal`/`removeLocal`/`watchLocalKeys`, under step 1's unique indexes); `DbWatchProgress` keeps a `LocalRef`'s place and lists `ContinueLibraryFile` in Continue watching (between a minute and 95 %, shown and available only); `VodLauncher.playLibraryItem`; `LibraryRepository.episodeAfter` (a show of the user's own, across folders); `LibraryFavorites` (a download is its title's favorite — a movie's, an episode's series' — a file of the user's own a local one).
- **The coordinator with a file on this computer:** no connection rule (no close-and-wait, no `room()`), and it holds none — playing a downloaded movie frees its source's connection; any trouble (a failure, a stall, the open timeout) fails at once as `fileUnreadable`, never reconnecting, and the provider is never asked why; an end before the length is the end.
- **The player:** the next-episode card is a `NextEpisode` value (a provider's episode, or the next file of a local show), so a local show gets the same countdown; a library item's title line (`libraryTitleLines`); its end and Esc go to the Library.
- **The cast, until step 7:** it asks the resolver for the provider's stream (`downloaded: false`), since the TV reads URLs; a library file asked to cast answers "A file from the library can't be cast yet."
- **Tests so far:** the coordinator with library files and downloads (5); the existing suites adjusted (the controller's next episode, the fakes). **3,144 app tests** (18 skipped) under `TZ=UTC`.
- **Left for part 2:** unit tests of `DbStreamResolver`'s download-first rule and `libraryFile`, of local history in `DbWatchProgress` (save, watch, dismiss, Continue watching's local rows, hidden ones left out), of `LibraryFavorites`, of the controller's next local episode, and of `sub-files` in `mpvOptionsFor`; then the integration test with the real player (a library file plays with the fake panel stopped, its external subtitles listed, and resumes; a downloaded movie plays from its file and its progress shows on the provider's page).

### Playback of library items, offline (step 5, part 2, 2026-10-04)
- **The rest of decision 8: the failure card's Show in folder and Remove from library.** `FileReveal` (`lib/core/platform/file_reveal.dart`, `lib/data/platform/file_reveals.dart`): on Linux the file manager's own D-Bus interface, `org.freedesktop.FileManager1` — `ShowItems` selects the file, `ShowFolders` opens the nearest folder still there when the file is gone, and the bus starts Nautilus (or Dolphin, Nemo…) when it isn't running — else `xdg-open` on the folder; on Windows `explorer.exe /select,` (untried). `LibraryRepository.item` and `downloadOf`; `failedFileProvider` finds the file of a play that failed as `fileUnreadable` — a library file, or a title's download that can't be read (one whose file is gone already plays from the provider: the resolver looks for the file first). **The UX calls** (docs/05): Show in folder takes the window out of full screen first, or the file manager opens behind the player; Remove from library takes the item out (the file stays, remembered so rescans leave it out), says so in a toast, and leaves the player for the page it came from — a download's title then plays from the provider.
- **Decision 11, offline, as built:** `NetworkStatus` (`lib/core/platform/network_status.dart`, pure, fake-time tested) joins the system's word and the sources' answers: the system says online → online, whatever a source does (a provider that doesn't answer is down, not the internet); the system says offline → offline until a source answers; the system can't say → offline after a request with no answer at all, until one is answered. A new word from the system forgets what the sources said. Going offline is told once it has lasted 3 s (a Wi-Fi roam passes through "connecting"); coming back at once. `NetworkManagerNetwork`: `State` and `StateChanged` on the system bus — 70 (connected, global) is online, 0 unknown, everything else offline; no NetworkManager on the bus, unknown. `homeOfflineProvider` is the state step 6's banner draws.
- **Departures:**
  - **A source's answer outweighs the system's "offline".** The plan had the system decide alone where it answers; but NetworkManager says "site" (60) when its connectivity check fails, which is also what a provider on the home network looks like with the internet down — that provider still plays, and "You're offline" over it would be wrong. A source that answers since the system last spoke counts as online.
  - **Windows asks `GetNetworkConnectivityHint` every 5 s**, not the Network List Manager: the same information from a plain function in `iphlpapi.dll` (Windows 10 2004 and later; missing, it is unknown), where the Network List Manager is COM and would need an apartment on Flutter's UI thread. Untried here, with the rest of Windows.
  - **The sources' answers come from playback only:** a source's picture or any HTTP answer is an answer; the prober reaching nothing (a network failure with no status) is none; a stall proves neither; a file on this computer says nothing (`sourceAnswered`, `playbackReachabilityProvider`, read once by `bootstrap()`). Syncs run per source in an isolate and aren't wired in: both systems answer on the targets, so the fallback is for a Linux without NetworkManager.
  - The providers (`fileRevealProvider`, `systemNetworkProvider`, `networkStatusProvider`) live in `lib/core/platform/` with do-nothing defaults, and `bootstrap()` gives them the system's, as `windowControlsProvider` does: presentation may not import `lib/data/` (the layering test caught the first draft), and no widget test touches the real buses.
- **Tests:** the resolver's download-first rule and `libraryFile` (a file gone, a folder not available, subtitle files still beside it); local history in `DbWatchProgress` (save, watched at 95 %, one row per file, mark watched and forget, Continue watching among the provider's titles, hidden, not connected, removed and moved files, dismiss); `LibraryFavorites` (a local file, a downloaded movie is the movie's, a downloaded episode its series'); the player's next file of a local show (the card, the countdown, Play now, the last file and a movie have none); `sub-files`; the file reveal against a fake file manager on an in-process bus, `xdg-open`, Explorer's arguments; `NetworkStatus` under fake time; NetworkManager on an in-process bus; Windows' poll; `sourceAnswered` and the wiring; the failure card's file actions (a library file, a damaged download, a provider's movie offering neither). **3,184 app tests** (18 skipped) under `TZ=UTC`.
- **Integration** (`integration_test/library_playback_test.dart`, the real player under Xvfb, with and without a picture, 11–14 s): Play on the provider's page of a downloaded movie plays its file with no connection at the panel, and after a minute Esc leaves the page offering Resume; with the panel stopped, a film of the user's own plays, its `.en.srt` is its one subtitle track, its place is kept and Continue watching lists it, and played again from there it opens there. The library's rows are written by the test; the scanner end to end is step 8's.
- **CI:** step 4's push failed Windows' Analyze step with nothing local reproducing it; the step now keeps its output and `tools/ci/analyze_findings.dart` names each finding as an annotation (or the log's last lines when the analyzer stopped without findings), so the next push says what it was.

### The UI (step 6, 2026-10-04 and 2026-10-11)
The canvas's Library, Library · Downloads and Settings · Downloads and library were read (the published artboards match `design/*.dc.html` byte for byte). Step 6 is built in parts, each committed on its own.
- **Part 1 — the lists and pictures from disk** (8325ad3): `LibraryRepository.watchCount` (count, size and a revision that moves on any change to the library, its history or favorites), `range` (a window in `watch()`'s order), `watchShows` (the user's episodes grouped by show title, case aside, across folders; a provider series' downloads by series), `watchShowEpisodes`, `watchFolderTotals`. `ArtworkImages` reads `file:` URLs (`localArtworkUrl`) from disk, never through the network cache, so screens keep calling `artworkFor` and presentation stays free of `dart:io`.

The files stay where they are."); Add folder's refusals are toasts in our words ("Movies HDD is already in the library."); the Library's pages live in its branch (`/library/movie/<id>`, `/library/show/<key>`), and a provider's page opened from the Library sits there too (`/library/movies/…`, `/library/series/…`, "‹ Library"), so Esc comes back to the Library.
