### The first download that wouldn't play, names that repeat the code, and downloads kept as MP4 (2026-10-11)
The user downloaded an episode from the real provider; the app said "This file is missing or damaged" (details: "… is not there") while the file sat in the folder and played elsewhere. They also asked that downloads be in a format most players support.
- **The cause:** the download folder is made by the first download (step 1), and the launch scan marked the folder that wasn't there yet "not connected". The download made the folder, but nothing marked it present until the next launch's scan, and the resolver refused any file in a folder marked away. The file and its library row were right throughout.
- **Fixed three ways, each enough alone:** the resolver goes by the disk (a file that is there plays; the folder's mark is as old as the last scan); the finisher marks a folder it has just written into as present, with what it held; the scan leaves a download folder that has never held a file alone instead of calling it a drive that went away. A download folder that held files and is gone is still "not connected".
- **Names:** the provider titles this show's episodes `S01E01` (11 of the 815 cached episodes; 75 more are release names like `The.Simpsons.S02E12`), which gave `The Simpsons - S01E01 - S01E01.mkv`. `providerEpisodeTitle` reads a provider's episode title as the scanner reads a file's name: a stand-in, the code alone or a release name around it says nothing; a title after the code is the title. The file name, the library row and the Downloads list use it. The name parser moved to `lib/core/library/` (pure; the paths in core call it).
- **Decision: a download is kept as MP4 whenever MP4 holds everything in the file as it is; otherwise it stays as it came** (docs/09 "Format"). On this panel 46 % of the movies and 9 % of the cached episodes are MKV.
  - **Why MP4:** it is the container phones, TVs' USB players, browsers and every desktop player open; MKV is refused by Apple's players and many TVs. It also casts directly (decision 12's direct path needs MP4 with AAC or MP3), with native seeks and no relay.
  - **Why only when nothing is lost:** the streams are copied, never encoded again, so the picture and sound are the provider's bit for bit. Where MP4 can't keep something (styled ASS or picture subtitles, Dolby Vision without stepping outside the standard) or few players read it from MP4 (DTS, TrueHD, Opus, FLAC, VP9, AV1, XviD), the file is left alone: a file that plays in fewer places beats one that has lost its subtitles.
  - **Rejected: re-encode what doesn't fit** (DTS → AAC, XviD → H.264). Minutes to hours a file, on battery, with a loss of quality the user didn't ask for.
  - **Rejected: a setting** ("Keep the provider's format"). The rule already keeps everything that would change; a switch would only choose between two containers of the same bits. It can be added to Settings → Downloads & library if someone wants MKV.
  - **Rejected: remaking files already downloaded.** The app never changes a file in the library on its own; the episode downloaded before this stays MKV.
- **Measured before building** (the bundled FFmpeg 8.1.2, `-c copy -c:s mov_text -movflags +faststart`): the user's own episode (H.264 720p + AAC, 235 MB) in 0.46 s, the same length to the millisecond; the samples `vod_h264_ac3_10min.mkv` 0.34 s (`avc1` + `ac-3`), `vod_hevc_eac3_subs.mkv` 0.18 s (`hvc1` + `ec-3` + two `tx3g` tracks with their languages), `h264_1080p50_aac.ts` 0.10 s (ADTS AAC into `mp4a` with no flag needed), `hevc_2160p25_eac3.ts` 0.15 s. Lengths agree within 11 ms.
- **Hard rule 11 through the new step:** the MP4 is written as `<name>.mp4.part` and checked against the original by ffprobe (codecs, track counts, length within 2 s) before the download's own `.part` is deleted; the rename to `<name>.mp4` is last. Each stop in between leaves files that say how far it got, and `DownloadFinisher.whole` lets the launch finish a download whose bytes are all there with no connection — which also closes an older gap: a stop between the rename and the database used to start the download again and end as `<name> (2)`.
- **Hard rule 8:** `FfmpegDownloadRemux` runs FFmpeg through the `ProcessSupervisor` (owner `download-mp4`, PID file, the launch sweep); a copy whose file hasn't changed for 60 s is killed (the second pass that moves the index prints nothing, so the watch is on the file, not on FFmpeg's progress); cancel and quit kill it and delete the half-made file.
- **The queue:** `DownloadFinishStopped` (quit: the row stays `verifying`; a cancel meanwhile: the download goes); a cancel or removal while a file is being checked or remade waits for that work to stop; `DownloadFinished.path` is stored as the download's path, so Show in folder finds a file that was numbered or remade.
- **On the way:** the library row gets its `probe_json` from the format step's ffprobe, so a download's quality line shows at once instead of after the next scan.
- **Tests:** the rule (23 cases); the real FFmpeg on made clips (MKV with SubRip → MP4 with the same video packets, MPEG-TS with AC-3, three kinds kept, no room, an FFmpeg that fails, hangs, is stopped); the finisher (each outcome, each stop between the steps, discard); the queue (the final name kept, a whole file finished at launch with no connection, cancel and quit during the step); end to end, a Matroska movie from the fake panel lands as MP4 with nothing left beside it; the scan and the resolver for the folder's mark.

### Casting library files, part 1: a file casts, directly or through the relay (step 7, 2026-10-11)
- **Built:** `CastUpstreamSource.file` and `CastRelay.serveFile` (`lib/core/cast/cast_relay.dart`); `RelayJob.localInput`, `RelayServeFileCommand`, `RelayRuntime.serveFile` and `_input` (`lib/data/cast/relay/`); `CastItems` resolving a library file and a download's file; the coordinator's `_deliverFile`; `lib/core/library/library_names.dart` (`libraryItemNames`, `libraryItemLine`, `libraryItemPicture`); `castLibraryItem` and Cast in `showLibraryItemMenu`, on the library's movie page (the button and C) and its show page; `ArtworkCastPictures` handing a `file:` picture over as it is.
- **Decision 12's first half, as built:** direct when the planner says the TV plays the file as it is — the relay's server gives it with Range and the TV seeks by itself; otherwise the continuous relay, whose FFmpeg reads the path. A downloaded title casts from its file (`StreamResolver.movie`/`episode` look for it first, as for playing), so the cast no longer asks for `downloaded: false`.
- **Choices made here:**
  - **FFmpeg reads the path, not the proxy.** The proxy exists to keep credentials out of FFmpeg's arguments and to count and retry a provider's connection; a file has none of the three. It also keeps the proxy (938 lines, tested against every fault) unchanged.
  - **A file is known and learned about as itself.** Its facts are remembered by its path and its planner source is `file:<path>`: a download remade as MP4 is not the provider's Matroska, and a TV that refuses one file directly has said nothing about the provider or the next file. The learned set gains one short entry per refused file.
  - **No reach timer for a direct file.** The relay's "the TV never fetched the stream" rule hangs on a session's events; a served file has no session. A TV that can't reach this computer fails its LOAD, the file goes through the relay, and that path says "couldn't reach this computer".
  - **UX (decided, as the provider's pages have it):** Cast sits after the plays in the item menu with C; the movie page has the bordered Cast button between the favorite and ⋯; the show page's Cast is beside Continue and casts what Continue would play, each episode's own Cast being in its menu. A file on a drive that is away says so instead of opening the picker.
- **The fake TV learned to play a file** (`tools/fake_receiver`): a file with a length is read as it plays (its head checked, `bufferAhead` past its place, nothing while paused), a SEEK or a start past 0:00 is a new Range request at that time's byte, FINISHED at its end. Before, a plain file was read whole and finished at once, so a direct play could prove nothing about seeks. `FakeWatchListener.position` is new.
- **Tests:** end to end against the fake TV (`cast_e2e_library_test.dart`): an MP4 of the user's own direct from its place (`bytes=0-`, then the bytes at its place), a seek as a further Range request with no new LOAD, its place saved under its own key, its URL gone afterwards, no FFmpeg, no provider request; a Matroska + AC-3 file through the relay with a seek, no provider request; a downloaded movie the provider lists as Matroska cast directly from its MP4 with nothing learned for the provider. The coordinator on fakes (4), `CastItems` (4), the names, the pictures, FFmpeg's arguments for a file, the menu's Cast and the drive-away case, the pages' keyboard order; the fake TV's own (4). Goldens re-recorded and looked at: the library's movie and show pages with their Cast buttons.
- **Left in step 7:** text subtitles as WebVTT with S in the casting view (the fake TV's text tracks and `EDIT_TRACKS_INFO`); the MP4 made beside a relayed file for native seeks; the library rows of the matrix runner; a keyboard walk that casts from the Library to the fake TV.

### Casting library files, part 2a: the fake TV's text tracks, and what FFmpeg does (step 7, 2026-10-11)
The session stopped here at the user's word; the app's side of subtitles followed the next day (part 2b).
- **The fake TV** (`tools/fake_receiver`): LOAD's TEXT tracks are fetched with the receiver's `Origin` and read as WebVTT (`FakeReceiver.textTracks`: cues, the first cue's time and text, or the problem); `activeTrackIds` from LOAD and from `EDIT_TRACKS_INFO`, in every MEDIA_STATUS of a media with tracks; an id the LOAD didn't name is refused. A track that can't be read doesn't fail the LOAD, as on a TV. Its own tests: two tracks read, a 404, a file that isn't WebVTT, no CORS, off / another / an unknown id.
- **Measured for the app's side** (the bundled FFmpeg 8.1.2): `-i <file>.srt -f webvtt` in 10 ms; **`-ss <offset>` before `-i` drops the cues before the offset and moves the rest back by exactly it** (the cue at 60 s is at 0:00 after `-ss 60`), which is what a relayed stream started past 0:00 needs; an embedded track of a 10-minute Matroska file (`-map 0:s:<n>`) with `-ss 120` in 20 ms.

### Casting library files, part 2b: subtitles on the TV, and where a relayed file really starts (step 7, 2026-10-12)
- **Built:** `CastTextTrack`, `CastLoad.textTracks` / `activeTextTrack` and `CastReceiverSession.showTextTrack` (`lib/core/cast/cast_receiver.dart`); LOAD's `media.tracks` + `activeTrackIds` and `EDIT_TRACKS_INFO` (`lib/data/cast/cast_namespaces.dart`, `cast_v2_receivers.dart`); the port `CastSubtitles` / `CastSubtitleSet` with `castSubtitleLabel` (`lib/core/cast/cast_subtitles.dart`); `FfmpegCastSubtitles` (`lib/data/cast/ffmpeg_cast_subtitles.dart`) and `shiftWebVtt` (`lib/data/cast/webvtt.dart`); the coordinator's `_textTracks`, `setSubtitle`, `_showChosenSubtitle`, `_loadAgainHere` and `_startOf`; `CastingState.subtitles` / `subtitle`; S, the Subtitles button and the line in the casting view; `languageTag`; `subtitleBeside`; `relayInputFlags`.
- **Departures from the handoff's design, and why:**
  - **The cues are moved in the app, not by `-ss` in FFmpeg.** Each subtitle is made into WebVTT once, whole; a relayed start past 0:00 rewrites the cue times in Dart. A file's own tracks cost a reading of the whole file (seconds on an SSD, a minute on a slow drive): doing that again at every seek would make every seek wait for it.
  - **A relayed file doesn't start where it was asked to.** Measured on a file with a key frame every 10 s: `-ss 27.5` with the picture copied starts at the key frame of 20.0 s (the sound with it, in step), and the fragmented MP4 counts from 0 there. Cues moved back by 27.5 s would have shown 7.5 s early. The app asks FFmpeg for the first copied packet's time and counts from it — the timeline and the saved place too, which were ahead of the picture by the same amount until now.
  - **The file's own tracks may come after the LOAD.** The LOAD waits 2 s for them; later ones are listed when ready, and choosing one loads the file again where it is. Waiting for them always would hold the picture for as long as the drive takes; leaving them out would lose them.
- **Choices made here:**
  - **Which subtitle shows first** (UX, decided): as the player starts a file — a handed-over cast follows the player (the language it showed, or none); otherwise the most wanted of Settings' Preferred subtitles that has one, else a subtitle file beside the video (the user put it there), else none. The TV's own status isn't followed for it: what the user chose here is what the next LOAD asks for.
  - **Files beside the video are listed before its own tracks**, so the first press of S gives the user's own file.
  - **A subtitle file that isn't Unicode** is read in its language's code page (the file name's tag, else the first Preferred subtitle language, else Windows-1252). FFmpeg refuses such a file outright otherwise, and Arabic or Cyrillic SubRip files are often in 1256 or 1251. It is a guess by language, not detection.
  - **Only files on this computer.** A provider's movie would need a second connection to read its tracks from.
  - **The WebVTT files live in the relay's folder for this run** (`<relay>/<pid>/subtitles-<n>`): deleted with the cast, with the relay at quit, and by the existing launch sweep after a crash. Every FFmpeg run goes through the `ProcessSupervisor` (owners `cast-subtitles` and `cast-start`, timeouts 30 s, 10 min and 10 s; a set's runs are killed when the cast ends).
- **Fixed on the way:** a file the TV plays itself that was cut short carried on with its place counted twice (`offset` was set to the place and added to the TV's own time); a direct play's offset is 0 now, with a test.
- **Tests:** the LOAD's JSON with and without tracks; `showTextTrack` against the fake TV (on, another, off, an id the LOAD didn't name); `shiftWebVtt` (7); the maker with the real FFmpeg (9: files beside and own tracks in order, moved back, 1252 and 1256, junk and empty and missing files left out, deleted mid-way, the start at the key frame with the picture or only the sound copied); the coordinator on fakes (13, with `FakeSubtitles`); the view's S, button and line; end to end against the fake TV (`cast_e2e_library_test.dart`, 3: a subtitle file through the relay read by the TV as WebVTT with its first cue 4.0 s after a seek to 67 s on a file with a key frame every 2 s; a film's two own tracks with German preferred and a switch to English without a LOAD; an MP4 played directly from 0:30 with its cues unmoved).
- **Not verified:** all of this is against the fake TV. Whether the real receiver fetches a track that isn't showing, how it styles the text, and whether it takes `EDIT_TRACKS_INFO` while it buffers belong to the library matrix on the TV (step 8).
- **Left in step 7:** the MP4 made beside a relayed file for native seeks; the library rows of the matrix runner; a keyboard walk that casts from the Library to the fake TV.

### Casting library files, part 3: the MP4 made beside the relay (step 7, 2026-10-12)
- **Built:** the port `CastMp4Copies` / `CastMp4Copy` (`lib/core/cast/cast_mp4_copies.dart`); `FfmpegCastMp4Copies` (`lib/data/cast/ffmpeg_cast_mp4_copies.dart`), which takes its stream arguments from `castRelayJob`, so the MP4 holds exactly what the relay sends; the coordinator's `_makeMp4`, `_moveToMp4`, `_backToRelay`, `_dropMp4` and `_tvReads` (which replaced the `plan.delivery.direct` tests where "the TV fetches it itself" was meant); `castMp4CopiesProvider`.
- **Decision 12's second half, as built:** the MP4 starts with the relay, is taken at the next seek once whole (or at any LOAD that was coming anyway), and the relay stops then. Playback is never interrupted to move to it.
- **Measured, against the plan's guess:** the plan expected "a 2 GB movie under a minute". The copy itself is instant; the sound's conversion to AAC runs at 54 times real time on one core here (FFmpeg's AAC encoder uses one), so a two-hour film takes about 2¼ minutes. `-aac_coder fast` was no faster. Splitting the sound across processes would cut it, at the cost of joins that can click; not done. Until the MP4 is whole, seeks are the relay's, as they were.
- **Choices made here:**
  - **The cast's plan stays the relay's** when the TV moves to the MP4: the badge still says the sound was converted, which is true of the MP4 too. A flag on the cast (`onMp4`) says the TV reads it by itself.
  - **A refusal of the MP4 teaches nothing.** The learning rules would have recorded "this file can't go direct", which was never tried; the cast goes back to the relay and makes no second MP4.
  - **Written as `media.mp4.part`, renamed when checked,** so a half-made file is never served; the check is ffprobe reading a picture and a length within 2 s of the original's.
  - **The room it needs is asked of the drive that holds the relay's folder** (the app's cache), not the film's: that is where it is written.
- **Tests:** the maker with the real FFmpeg (7: H.264 + AC-3 → H.264 + stereo AAC with the index in front and the original untouched; HEVC tagged `hvc1`; no room; a re-encode or a live plan; FFmpeg failing, a short MP4, a missing file; a copy that stops writing; deleted mid-way); the coordinator on fakes (6, with `FakeMp4Copies`); end to end against the fake TV — the 10-minute Matroska sample relayed, its MP4 ready after 11 s, a seek to 67 s loading `/f/<token>/media.mp4` with no FFmpeg left running, the place the very second asked for, its subtitle's first cue at 0:00, and a second seek as the TV's own SEEK with no LOAD.
- **Left in step 7:** the library rows of the matrix runner; a keyboard walk that casts from the Library to the fake TV.

### Casting library files, part 4: the matrix runner's library rows, and the walk from the Library (step 7, 2026-10-12)
- **The matrix runner** (`test/tools/cast_matrix_tv_test.dart`) has docs/04's library matrix as rows 17–23, with the subtitles and the MP4 wired as the app wires them. New in the runner: `_own` (a film of the user's own from the samples, with or without the subtitle file beside it), `expectPlan`, `expectNoProvider` (a row fails if the panel saw a connection or a URL was built), `mp4Ready`, `shows` and `subtitle` (on the fake TV they also ask it what it shows and whether it could read every track); `seekTo` allows the key frame before the place. Against the fake TV: 7 of 7, in 50 s.
- **The walk** (`integration_test/library_cast_walk_test.dart`, 35 s; 54 s with FFmpeg 4.4): keys only on the real app, from the Library to Stop casting, through the relay, the subtitles, the MP4 and the TV's own seek. CI runs it with the other integration files.
- **FFmpeg 4.4, which CI runs, differs from the bundled 8.1 in two ways that showed:** a clip it makes starts 6 ms off 0, so its subtitle cues come out at 1.006 s where 8.1 gives 1.000 (the tests compare cue times to 40 ms now, not as text); and it converts sound to AAC three times slower (29 s for the 10-minute sample against 11 s). Found by running the new tests with `RELAY_FFMPEG_DIR=/usr/bin` before the push; docs/06 says to.
- **Step 7 is built.** What it leaves for step 8: the library matrix on the TV with the user watching (rows 17–23), where the things only a real receiver can say are checked — whether it fetches and shows a side-loaded track, how it styles it, whether it plays the MP4 made for it.

### The phase exit's tests (step 8, 2026-10-12)
The plan's three integration tests, without the TV:
- **Download → kill the app → relaunch → resume → play offline:** `download_kill_test.dart`'s first test already killed a separate process part-way and had the next launch's queue resume and finish it; it now closes the provider's server and plays the title through the real resolver, which gives the file, with no connection asked for.
- **Library scan → play → resume:** `integration_test/library_scan_test.dart`, on the real app with keys only. It is the first test where nothing writes library rows by hand: Add folder (the dialog answered with a temporary folder), the real scan, the film's page showing what ffprobe read and its subtitle file, the player, Resume. A text file and a 4 KB "video" are left out; the folder is the same before and after.
- **After SIGKILL + relaunch, no FFmpeg left:** the relay's and the HLS downloader's were proven before (they end by themselves: their input or output was the dead app's). **The cast's helpers are different, and the test says so:** the MP4 and the subtitles read a file and write a file, nothing of theirs ends with the app, and they run on after it is killed — for as long as their work takes, a couple of minutes at most for a film's MP4. `cast_helpers_kill_test.dart` kills a process running both and proves the next launch's sweep kills them, leaves no PID file and deletes the half-made MP4 with the relay's folder for that run. Their commands moved into `lib/data/cast/cast_file_jobs.dart` (plain Dart) so the killed process runs the app's own.
  - **Accepted, not fixed:** making them end with the app would need a tie FFmpeg doesn't offer for file-to-file work (it ignores a closed pipe and an end of its input). They are bounded, and what they leave is swept. The download's own MP4 step behaves the same way and finishes at the next launch.
- **Left for the exit:** the library matrix on the TV with the user watching (rows 17–23), then ADR-015 Accepted. One thing was looked at and left: a provider's relayed file still counts its place from the place asked for, not from its key frame, because asking FFmpeg would take one of the provider's connections (progress.md, Known issues).

## ADR-016 · 2026-10-13 · Accepted 2026-10-14, at the phase's exit, with what it did not meet listed under "The exit" (plan approved 2026-10-13) — Phase 9 settings, diagnostics, reminders and polish choices
**Decision:** the Phase 9 choices below, organized by step, as docs/plans/phase-9-settings-diagnostics-polish.md proposed and the user approved on 2026-10-13 ("the 7 and 8 are apporved and then do the step 9, but not the 8-hour soak") — **the recommendation in all sixteen decisions and the nine sketches, except decision 15's night:** the 8-hour soak is not run, by the user's choice, so docs/08's exit criterion for it is left unmet and said so at the exit. The departures are recorded where they happen.

The sixteen, in short: **1** Phase 9 starts while the TV sitting waits. **2** Appearance follows the desktop until told otherwise: accent, text size (System · 100 · 115 · 130 %, capped at 130 %), density, reduce motion, clock. **3** at larger text a row that holds text grows with it and pictures don't; at 100 % nothing moves. **4** Settings → Playback gets Hardware decoding: Automatic · Off. **5** Keyboard shortcuts is a list to read, and F1 shows it anywhere; no rebinding. **6** Export holds settings and choices, never a password, and adds no source. **7** no switch to turn channel-name cleanup off. **8** the log is read inside the app. **9** Copy diagnostics shows its text first and leaves out who the provider is. **10** a reminder is a system notification at the programme's start, while the app is open. **11** our own D-Bus client on Linux, flutter_local_notifications on Windows, behind one port. **12** a reminder follows its programme through a guide refresh. **13** set in Search, the Guide's sheet and grid; managed in one dialog with two doors. **14** the audit is a test matrix that stays. **15** the soak plays the app as it is used now (its night not run). **16** profile-mode numbers where a frame or a launch is measured; the benchmarks as they are for the rest.

### Appearance, and what it rests on (step 1, 2026-10-13)
- **Built:** `AppearanceSettings` (`lib/design/appearance.dart`: accent, text size, density, reduce motion, clock; each "System" answered by `resolve(SystemAppearance)` into an `Appearance`), kept as one JSON value under `appearance.settings`; the port `AppearanceStore` (`lib/features/settings/domain/`), `DbAppearanceStore` and the providers (`lib/features/settings/data/`: `appearanceStoreProvider`, `desktopAppearanceProvider`, `appearanceControllerProvider`, `appearanceProvider`, `timeStyleProvider`); `bootstrap()` opens the store before `runApp`, so the first frame already has the look. `IptvPlayerApp` builds the theme from it and wraps the router in `AppTextScale`. Settings → Appearance is sketch A.
- **The text-scale rule** (`lib/design/text_scale.dart`, decision 3): `context.scaled(height)` for a height that holds text, `context.scaledAround(box, fixed:)` where a picture inside keeps its size, `context.textScale`. At 100 % both return their argument untouched, so nothing can move by a rounding. The design system's own components are on it: buttons, chips, text fields, the search field, a tab's count, the channel row and its skeleton, the channel tile (`ChannelTile.heightOf`), the casting bar, the step indicator. `SegmentedControl` is a `Wrap` now: one line where it fits, more where it doesn't. Screens' own heights follow in step 7.
- **The clock:** `TimeStyle` in `lib/core/text/format.dart`; `formatClock` and its family take it as a required argument, so the compiler found all 24 call sites. Screens read `context.tokens.timeStyle`. The Guide grid caches a programme's words per clock.
- **Density:** `AppDensity.row(comfortable)` takes 12 px off a long list's row, never under 40 px. It reaches Live TV's channels, the player's channel panel, Favorites' channels (all three are `ChannelRow`), the Categories manager (48 → 40) and the Guide (72 → 60, with less air inside a cell so two lines still fit).
- **Departures from the plan:**
  - **Live TV's category rows stay 40 px in Compact.** The plan lists them; they are already at docs/05's smallest hit target, and going under it to look denser isn't worth a harder click.
  - **The text size is clamped at both ends,** 100 % to 130 %: a desktop set under 100 % gets the canvas's sizes (rows scaled under 100 % would drop below the 40 px target).
  - **"Off" for reduce motion can't undo the desktop's switch entirely:** when the desktop has animations off, Flutter itself runs every animation at a twentieth of its length, whatever the app asks. Off then means only that the app doesn't shorten or drop anything more.
  - **A change of the desktop's clock format alone isn't announced to the app** (text size and animations are): it is read again when the window comes back to the front.
  - **A setting's control goes under its words when the two don't fit side by side** (`SettingRow`, a small render object): at 130 % in the smallest window the five accents are wider than the room beside "Accent".
- **Found and fixed on the way:**
  - **A channel number of four or more digits wrapped onto a second line in every channel row, at 100 % too** (a 32 px slot; "1284" is 32.2 px wide). The user's provider numbers channels past 12,000. The slot now takes the room its digits need, on one line; up to three digits it is the canvas's 32 px, so no golden moved.
  - The top bar's search hint wrapped onto two lines at 130 % and was cut by its box; it is one line with an ellipsis.
- **Tests:** the settings' JSON round trip, damaged values, the cap and each System choice (10); the store on a database, with a restart, a damaged value and a read that fails (5); the providers and the desktop's values read from the platform (7); the page by keyboard, each change showing at once, the first frame, a failed read with Try again, a failed save, and 130 % in the smallest window (11); the helper and the components at 100, 115 and 130 % (17); the clock family in both styles. **Every golden recorded before this step is byte-identical,** except the five of Settings, which differ only where "Phase 9" stood beside Appearance (checked by diffing the images: one 46 × 10 px box). New goldens, each looked at: the page, the page at 130 % in Compact and Rose in the smallest window, the component sheets at 130 %, the media sheet in Compact with Teal, the Guide in Compact with a 24-hour clock.
- **Left for step 7:** the screens' own heights (the top bar, Settings' list, the Guide's rows, grids' captions) don't grow with the text yet.

### Keyboard shortcuts, hardware decoding, About (step 2, 2026-10-13)
- **The shortcut list is one table in the code** (`lib/app/shortcut_table.dart`: groups of rows, each an action, its keycaps and, for the global layer's keys, the activators it documents). `ShortcutList` (`lib/app/shell/shortcut_list.dart`) draws it for Settings → Keyboard shortcuts and for the **F1** panel (`showShortcutsPanel`, a dialog on the root navigator, bound in `AppGlobalShortcuts` like Esc and C). Two columns of groups where there is room.
- **Three checks keep it true:** every activator `AppGlobalShortcuts` binds has a row; every activator a row names is bound; and **docs/05's shortcut tables are written from the table** (between two markers; `test/app/shortcut_table_test.dart` fails when they differ and rewrites them with `UPDATE_SHORTCUT_DOCS=1`). The doc's old table had drifted: it gave F as full screen only, where F is favorite everywhere but in the player.
- **F1 over the player:** the panel is a route above it, so the player keeps playing and gets none of the panel's keys (↓ scrolls the list and doesn't zap; M doesn't mute); Esc closes the panel and not the player. A second F1 while it shows does nothing. Tested on the player's own widget rig.
- **`ScrollPane`** (`lib/design/components/scroll_pane.dart`), new: something to read that is longer than its box, as **one Tab stop with the focus ring**; ↑/↓ a line, PageUp/PageDown a boxful, Home/End the ends. At an end the arrow isn't taken, so the focus leaves the way it came (no trap). Nothing inside it is a stop of its own. The shortcut list and a licence's text use it; step 4's report preview and log viewer will.
- **Hardware decoding** (decision 4): `PlaybackSettings.hardwareDecoding` (stored as `hwdec: auto | off`; anything else, and settings from before, read as Automatic) → `PlayRequest.hardwareDecoding` → mpv's `hwdec` set before every open (`auto-safe` or `no`), so it applies to the next thing played. Two details:
  - **With no picture (`vo=null`) Automatic leaves `hwdec` alone,** as before this step: the headless runs (CI, the soak under Xvfb) decode exactly as they did.
  - **The stored choice is read before the player is made:** media_kit's video controller sets `hwdec` once by itself, some time after it is constructed, and would otherwise undo an Off chosen in an earlier run for the first thing played.
- **Measured, on this laptop under Xvfb with a picture:** Automatic decoded H.264 1080p50 with `nvdec-copy`; the same engine opened with Off reported `hwdec=no` and no hardware decoder, played, and the next open with Automatic was `auto-safe` again (`integration_test/player_engine_test.dart`).
- **About:** the name, the version and build, and the system. The version comes from `version.json`, which every Flutter build writes beside the app's assets (`readAppVersion`), so no package was added; a bundle without it says "version unknown". **Licences** is our own dialog over Flutter's `LicenseRegistry` (Flutter's own page is a Material screen with none of the app's keyboard rules): packages A to Z as one Tab stop, the chosen one's text in a `ScrollPane`; loading, empty and failed states.
- **FFmpeg's and mpv's licences are bundled** (`assets/licenses/`: GPL 3.0, GPL 2.0, LGPL 2.1, copied from the system's `common-licenses`) and registered at launch with a notice each (`lib/core/platform/tool_licenses.dart`): FFmpeg's builds are BtbN's GPL variant; libmpv is the system's on Linux (GPL 2+) and media_kit's on Windows (LGPL 2.1+). **Phase 10 must look at this again** when it decides what the installers carry.
- **A dialog keeps 24 px clear of the window's edges** (`AppDialog`): in the smallest window (640 px high) the shortcuts panel filled it top to bottom. Dialogs in larger windows are where they were (no golden moved).
- **Tests:** the table's three checks and its wording (6); the F1 panel over the shell, in search and over the player (5); `ScrollPane` (6); the shortcuts page (3); About, the licences dialog by keyboard and its states (7); the version file, damaged and missing (3); the licence list and the tools' texts (4); the settings' JSON, the request and mpv's options for both choices, with and without a picture (5); the Playback page's row. Goldens, each looked at: the shortcuts page, the F1 panel in the smallest window, About, Licences, Playback with its new row. The other Settings goldens differ only where "Phase 9" stood beside two more sections.

### Data: clear pictures, clear the guide, export and import (step 3, 2026-10-13)
- **Built:** Settings → Data (sketch C). **Pictures:** the port `ArtworkStore` (`lib/core/images/artwork_store.dart`; `CachedArtworkStore` over the app's cache), `ArtworkCache.size()` and `clear()`, both in an isolate; Clear also drops the decoded pictures from Flutter's own cache. **Guide:** `guideTotalsProvider` adds every source's programmes and takes the furthest end; Clear guide calls the existing `clearGuide` per source (programmes and guide channels go, the user's mappings stay) and is off while any guide is importing. **Export… / Import…:** the file's model and tolerant reader (`lib/features/settings/domain/settings_export.dart`), the port `SettingsTransfer` with its plan and summaries, and `DbSettingsTransfer`.
- **Format 1, as built** (docs/02 has the shape): `app`, `format`, `exported_at`, `app_version`, `settings` (five pages: appearance, playback, guide, cast, downloads) and `sources`, each with its name, type, a fingerprint, favorite groups, favorites, categories, channels, guide matches and reminders.
- **Decision 6's choices, as built:**
  - **A source is found by a fingerprint:** SHA-256 over its type, its server (lower-cased, without a trailing slash) and its username. The file holds neither. `crypto` moved from the dev dependencies to the dependencies for it (ADR-002's table says so). Pinned by a test against a value worked out with Python.
  - **A playlist file is recognized by its file name,** not its path: paths differ between computers, and between Linux and Windows the separator does too (the first draft used the path package's `basename`, which on Linux doesn't split a Windows path; a test with `C:\…` caught it).
  - **Every category of a source is in the file,** hidden or not: the picks made by hand come out the same on the other computer whatever it had picked. **Channels are in it only when hidden or renamed,** and an import leaves the others as they are here.
  - **Favorites merge:** one in the file that isn't here is added; one in both takes the file's group and place; one only here stays. Groups are matched by name. Favorites and guide matches are kept by key even when the channel isn't in this computer's list yet: they attach when it is.
  - **The download folder never travels:** it is left out of the file, and an import keeps this computer's.
  - **Window size, the rail, the source being browsed, the library's folders and removed items, watch history and known cast devices are not in the file.**
  - **An import adds no source;** one that isn't here is named in the preview ("not on this computer — add it under Sources, then import again") and skipped.
  - **One transaction:** settings and every source's choices apply whole or not at all (a test throws just before the commit and finds nothing changed).
  - **A file is refused as a whole only for three reasons,** each with its own words: damaged or cut short (not JSON, or not text), not ours (no `app: iptv-player`), or from a newer version (a higher `format`). Anything inside it that is unknown or malformed is skipped.
- **After an import** the settings pages in memory are read again (each settings controller gained `reload()`), so a new accent or text size shows at once, and every source that got guide matches is matched again. **The days of guide kept apply from the next refresh:** an import doesn't start a re-import of every guide.
- **Reminders have their place in the file** (`reminders` per source, and `ReminderTransfer`, which step 5 implements); until then the list is empty.
- **Keyboard detail worth keeping:** a button is disabled while it works, and a disabled control can't hold the focus, so the focus fell to wherever Flutter put it. The page gives it back to the button that was pressed when the work ends, and moves it to the next button when the pressed one has nothing left to do (Clear, once nothing is kept). `AppDialog.focusSecondary` starts a dialog on Cancel without making its primary button a danger button (the import's preview).
- **Tests:** the reader on six fixtures in `test_fixtures/settings/` (good, cut short, not JSON, not ours, newer, unknown keys) and what it writes (13); end to end on two databases — everything arrives, a source that isn't here, merge, twice, a list that changed, a failure part-way, which source a file's source is, the files that can't be imported (19); hard rule 3 on a source whose server, username and password are strings found nowhere else, with the provider's raw account answer in the database (none in the file); the cache's size and clear (5); the page's sentences (7); the page's states and both dialogs by keyboard, and at 130 % in the smallest window (25). Goldens, each looked at: the page, the import's preview. The other Settings goldens differ only where "Phase 9" stood beside Data.
- **Not done here:** a real file moved between two real computers (the Windows PC).

### Diagnostics: the report and the log viewer (step 4, 2026-10-13)
- **Built:**
  - **What decoded what:** `DecoderNotes` (`lib/core/player/decoder_notes.dart`; `DbDecoderNotes` keeps the newest twenty, one per kind of video, under `diagnostics.decoders`). The playback coordinator asks the player at the first frame and notes it; a note that says what the list already says isn't written, so zapping through fifty 1080p channels is one write.
  - **The system's facts** behind `SystemFactsReader` (`lib/core/platform/system_facts.dart`). Linux (`lib/data/platform/linux_system_facts.dart`) reads files only, in an isolate: `/etc/os-release`, `XDG_SESSION_TYPE` and `XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP`, `/sys/class/drm/card*/device/{vendor,device,uevent}` for the cards and their drivers, and the PCI id list for their names. No program is run. Windows (`windows_system_facts.dart`) uses `Platform.operatingSystemVersion` and `EnumDisplayDevices`; **it has never run**, CI compiles it.
  - **The report** (`buildDiagnosticsReport`, pure; `DbDiagnostics` gathers its facts): the version, the system, the cards, the player's version (`PlayerVersion`, a capability only the real engine has), the decoder notes, FFmpeg's version and the encoders the last detection remembered (read from its file: the report never tests encoders), each source's type, account status, connection limit, counts, last sync and last guide import with their skipped rows, the settings that change behaviour, the library's and the downloads' counts, and the log's last 50 warnings and errors. Each part that can't be read is left out rather than failing the report.
  - **The log viewer** (`FileLogReader`, `LogView`): the newest 2,000 lines, older ones as the top comes near, All or Warnings and errors, a text filter applied 250 ms after typing stops, new lines once a second, Copy shown, Open log folder.
- **Departure from sketch D: the report and the log are two views of one space,** chosen with a Diagnostics · Log control, not stacked. Stacked, each got about a hundred pixels of height in the smallest window; as views, each fills the page.
- **What the report may not say, as built** (decision 9):
  - A source is "Source 1", never its name.
  - **A log line's addresses are replaced:** a URL's host and any IPv4 address become `<address>`, each source's own server `<server>`, each source's name "Source N". This computer's own addresses (`127.0.0.1`, `localhost`) stay: they name nobody, and say which part was talking. **The first draft passed the log's lines through as written;** the hard-rule-3 test (below) failed on it: the log legitimately names the server its lines are about, with the sign-in masked.
  - The home folder is `~`; no path comes from the settings (the download folder is left out).
  - The whole text goes through `redact()` once more, with the registered secrets.
- **The exit's check, on the user's own source** (2026-10-13; from a **copy** of this laptop's app database and log, read-only: no sign-in, no network, no stream): the report, every line the log viewer reads and an export file were searched for the real server's host, username and password, taken from the login file outside the repository. **The report and the log were clean. The export was not:** its `name` field held the server's host, because the app names a source the user didn't name after its server (`suggestedSourceName`), and the user's source is named so. **Fixed:** a name that says the server, the address or the username is written "Xtream source 1" / "Playlist 2" in a file (`DbSettingsTransfer.exportedName`); the check then passed on all three. The made-up sources in step 3's tests had names of their own, which is why they missed it: **a fake that is kinder than the real thing hides bugs, again.**
- **Measured** (`log_view_benchmark_test.dart`, 5 × 5 MB of generated log, 240,888 lines): the first page in **12 ms** (median of five, worst 23; the step's bar was 100), an older page 16 ms, Warnings and errors 88 ms, a text filter 96 ms, a filter nothing matches (all 25 MB read) 656 ms, a look for new lines 1 ms; **worst UI-isolate gap 9 ms** (bar: 16).
- **Two things about lists worth keeping:**
  - **The log is a list drawn from the bottom** (`reverse: true`): the newest line is where a log is read, and older lines added at the far end move nothing on screen. `ScrollPane` gained `reverse`, so ↑ still shows what is above and Home the top.
  - **Rows of a fixed height are updated in place when lines are added, and that alone doesn't lay the list out again:** its length stayed as it was, so "scrolled up, it holds still" drew the wrong lines and a key press at the old top found nowhere to go. The view marks the list's viewport for layout after every change of its lines (`_layOutList`), and corrects the offset first when scrolled up.
- **Tests:** the decoder notes and their store (9), the coordinator's note at the first frame (2), the Linux reader on a made-up system tree (7), the log's lines (7) and its reader — pages across files, records kept whole, both filters, following, a file that filled up (17), the report's text (9), the service on a database with a failed sync, skipped rows and no source at all (5), **hard rule 3 across the log, the report and an export** on a source whose server, username and password are found nowhere else (1), the views by keyboard in every state (22). Goldens, each looked at: About with the report, About with the log.
- **Not done here:** Windows (the facts reader, the clipboard, Save).

### Reminders: the store, the scheduler, the notification (step 5, 2026-10-13)
- **The spike, as far as it goes without a click** (this desktop: GNOME Shell 42.9 on Wayland, GTK 3.24.33). Asked over D-Bus, nothing shown: the notification service answers specification 1.2 with `actions`, `body`, `persistence`; **its interface has `ActionInvoked` and `NotificationClosed` and no `ActivationToken` signal.** So on this desktop a press on a notification's button can hand the app nothing to raise its window with, and Wayland then says "IPTV Player is ready" instead: the plan's fallback, now the known behaviour here. **What does bring the window forward on GNOME is a click on the notification's own body when the notification has no default action:** the shell then activates the app's window itself, and tells the app only that the notice was closed (`FdoNotificationDaemonSource.open`, read from GNOME Shell's source; **not yet seen on this screen: it needs the user's click**).
- **So, as built** (`DbusNotifier`, `lib/data/platform/dbus_notifier.dart`): on GNOME Shell a notice has the **Watch** button and no default action; a notice the user closes is reported as "closed, not chosen", and `ReminderAlerts` treats it as Watch if the window is in front within a second. Every other server gets the default action too (a click on the notice is Watch). Where a desktop does send `ActivationToken`, it is kept and passed to the window.
- **Bringing the window forward:** `WindowControls.bringToFront({activationToken})` and `isFocused()`. On Linux a method channel in the runner (`iptv_player/window`, `linux/runner/my_application.cc`) sets the token as the window's startup id (`gtk_window_set_startup_id`: X11 startup notification, xdg-activation on Wayland) and presents it; elsewhere window_manager's `show` and `focus`. Checked in the launch test under Xvfb: the channel answers with and without a token.
- **Schema v10:** `reminders` (source, the channel's remote key, the guide channel's XMLTV id, the channel's name when set, the title, start and end as epoch milliseconds, when set), unique per source, channel and start, cascading from `sources`, with `reminders_start`. Keyed by the remote key like all user data.
- **The domain** (`lib/features/reminders/domain/`): `Reminder`, `ReminderRepository`, `ReminderGuide`, `followReminder` (decision 12 as one pure function), `ReminderScheduler`, `ReminderFollower`.
  - **The scheduler:** one timer to the next start, a look every minute, and a look whenever the list changes. **A reminder is removed from the store before it is shown, and shown only by whoever removed it,** which is what makes "never twice" true whatever else fails; one that can't be removed isn't shown. Shown within 90 s of its start it says "Starting now"; later, while its programme is on, "On now … until 10:30 PM"; once over, nothing.
  - **The follow:** the nearest showing of the same title on the same guide channel within 12 hours; a move of 5 minutes or more is said; not found in a guide that has something before and after that time on the channel → removed and said; a guide that doesn't reach the time → kept. A programme that moved onto another reminder of the same channel leaves one reminder.
- **Where it is said** (`ReminderAlerts`, decision 10): a system notification when the window isn't in front, a toast with Watch when it is, and the toast whenever the system can't (no service: `show` answers false). Watch from either goes out on one stream (`watches`), which step 6 plays.
- **Windows:** `WindowsNotifier` over flutter_local_notifications with a fixed GUID and the app id; **never run.** If it can't start, every reminder is the toast.
- **Reminders are in a settings file now** (`DbReminderTransfer`, step 3's hook): out with their source, in on the other computer; one whose programme has started by then is left out.
- **The guide says when it went live:** `EpgImporter.wentLive` (on the importer itself, not on the screens' `GuideImportService`, which four test fakes implement). `ReminderService` (started by `bootstrap()` after the first frame, before the syncs) listens and follows.
- **A generator trap:** a provider function named `systemNotifier` becomes `systemProvider` (riverpod_generator drops a trailing "Notifier"); it is `systemNotifications`.
- **Tests:** the scheduler under fake time — on time, never twice, late and still on, late and over, a sleep across the start and across the whole programme, the clock set back and forward, two at one minute, one set or removed while running, a store that fails (15); the follow rule and the follower (11); the store, the guide lookup and the settings file on a database (13); the migration, with data (2 new, 62 in all); **the Linux client against a fake notification service on a private bus** — what is sent, Watch, a click, a token, closed unread, another app's notice, GNOME's rules, no service, no bus (15); where a reminder is said and what Watch does (15).
- **Left for step 6:** every screen (Search, the Guide's sheet and grid, the Reminders dialog), what Watch plays, and **one real reminder on this desktop with the user there**, which is also the spike's missing half.

### Reminders on screen (step 6, 2026-10-13)
- **Built** (decision 13, sketches F–I):
  - **One set of actions** (`lib/features/reminders/presentation/reminder_actions.dart`): `toggleReminder` (set, or remove the one it has, each with a toast and Undo), `removeReminder`, `showReminderInGuide`, `reminderFor`. Made from the app's container, not a screen's `ref`: a toast's Undo and a jump to the Guide outlive the overlay or dialog they started in.
  - **Search:** "Remind me" at the end of an upcoming row, "Reminder set" with a filled bell once it has one; a click sets or removes; **Ctrl+R** on the cursor's row; the row's menu has it.
  - **The Guide:** **R** on the focused programme; a bell after the title in a cell that has one; Remind me / "Reminder set · Remove" in the sheet; a bell with the count in the toolbar.
  - **Reminders** (`reminders_dialog.dart`): the list, soonest first, behind the toolbar's bell and a row in Settings → Guide.
  - **Watch** (`reminder_watch.dart`): `reminderWatchesProvider`, read once by `bootstrap()`, listens to the alerts' `watches` stream itself (a provider nobody watches is paused) and calls `watchReminder`: the window forward with the desktop's token, the channel found by its source and remote key, then the TV while casting, else the player full screen.
- **Choices the sketches left open, as built:**
  - **The bell is ours.** The canvas draws "Remind me" as words and has no bell; `bell` and `bell-filled` are drawn to its set (24 px grid, 1.8 px round strokes) and added to the extractor's hand-drawn list.
  - **The search footer says what Ctrl+R does** ("Ctrl R Remind me" / "Remove reminder") while the cursor is on an upcoming programme, and nothing otherwise: a shortcut that shows nowhere on the screen it works on isn't found.
  - **In search the toast's Undo is the mouse's;** Ctrl+Z belongs to the text field there, and Ctrl+R again is the keyboard's way back. In the Guide and in the list Ctrl+Z undoes.
  - **R is only plain R:** with Ctrl, Alt or Meta held the grid leaves it alone.
  - **A programme that is on, or over, gets no reminder:** R, Ctrl+R and a sheet opened before it started all say "<title> has already started." The sheet has no Remind me for one.
  - **A bell shows on the channel the reminder was set on,** not on every channel sharing its guide id (an HD/SD pair shows one).
  - **The toolbar's bell is there with no reminders too:** its empty list is where "Press R on an upcoming programme in the Guide, or choose Remind me in Search" is said. The count is over every source, as the list is.
  - **The list's rows are one stop each:** Enter opens the Guide, Delete removes; a row's Remove button is the mouse's (the keys are written under the list). A removed row hands the focus to the one that takes its place, to Close after the last. Rows draw the channel's logo, read from the channel as it is now.
  - **A reminder whose channel is gone stays in the list** when Enter can't open it, and says why; it can be removed there. Watch on one says the same and plays nothing.
  - **Watch does what a channel opened from search does:** search closes, the player is pushed over wherever the user was, Esc comes back and the stream stops. With the player already up (a movie, another channel) it shows the new channel and is not opened twice. A dialog that was open stays under the player.
  - **The programme sheet is 640 px wide** (was 520), so Watch channel, Remind me and Add to favorites share a line; the longest labels wrap and the Esc keycap sits beside the last line.
  - **The toolbar keeps 504 px for everything but the day pills** (was 440): room for the bell.
- **Settings → Guide's row is one line,** and the page's two layouts change over at 532 px of height (was 460): the new row would otherwise have taken the channels' last two rows in a short window.
- **Found and fixed on the way:** Settings → Guide's **loading rows overflowed whenever the channel list had under 224 px** (four fixed rows in a Column), which the page allowed in windows 670–840 px high with a failed refresh showing; the new row made an existing test hit it. They are cut at the list's height now.
- **Departure from the plan's verification:** the list's 130 % picture is taken over Settings → Guide, not over the Guide: **the Guide's own rows don't grow with the text yet** and overflow at 130 % (step 7's list already has it).
- **Left for step 7's list:** Shift+Tab back into a one-stop list lands at its near end, not on the row it was left from (the Reminders list, as Live TV's categories).
- **Tests:** the screens by keyboard and mouse (30): search (Remind me and LIVE, Ctrl+R twice with the focus kept, the menu, a click, on now, a store that fails), the Guide (R, the bell, the count, Ctrl+Z; on now and no guide; another channel's or source's reminder; the sheet's button and its focus; a programme that started meanwhile; the bell's place in the Tab order; Settings → Guide's door), the list (order and words; one Tab stop; Delete with Undo and where the focus goes; the last and the only row; the mouse's Remove; Enter to the Guide's sheet; a channel that is gone; none set; unreadable with Try again), Watch (the toast; the desktop's notice with its token; search closing; the player already up; a channel that is gone; while casting, on the fake TV). Goldens, each looked at: the Guide with bells, the sheet with its reminder set, the list, the list at 130 % in the smallest window, search with a reminder set. Re-recorded, differing only where they should: the three Guide goldens (the bell, a 45 × 36 px box), the two search results (Remind me, 66 × 10 px), Settings → Guide (the row), the shortcuts page (three rows). **`integration_test/reminder_walk_test.dart`**, keys only on the real app against the fake panel, with the app's clock and a recording notifier: search → Ctrl+R → the Guide's bell → the app closed and opened again → the list → Enter to the sheet → the clock reaches the start with the window covered → one notification → Watch → the channel plays. 18 s.
- **Not done here:** one real reminder on this desktop, with the user there (the spike's missing half): whether a click on the notice's text brings the window forward on GNOME 42, and what Watch does to the window.

### The audit (step 7, started 2026-10-13; parts 1 and 2 of it built)
- **The harness** (`test/audit/`, decision 14): `audit_look.dart` has the seven looks a scene is drawn in — 100, 115 and 130 % in 1024 × 640 and in 1920 × 1080, and once more with reduce motion — and `currentAuditLook`, which `pumpApp` and `appearanceOverrides` read. `audit_harness.dart` has the checks:
  - **anything that overflows,** every time it happens, named by the file and line of the widget that made the box (the framework's own "relevant widget");
  - **text cut off by its box** (taller than the box it was given, or wider with nothing to say so);
  - **the keyboard:** Tab from where the screen leaves the focus until a stop comes round again; a walk that never does, or leaves the focus on nothing; a stop with no focus ring on or around it; and any control that neither the walk nor the arrow keys reach (the arrows are followed from every stop until nothing new turns up);
  - **with reduce motion,** anything still animating 200 ms after the screen settled.
- **Every golden is an audit scene.** `sceneTest` (`test/golden/golden_harness.dart`) runs a golden's body once as itself, compared with its picture, and once in each look, where `golden(tester, …)` is the audit's look at the screen instead of a comparison. So the fixtures and the steps that open a screen are written once, and **a screen that gets a golden is audited with nothing more written.** `auditScreen` (`test/audit/`) is the same for a state that has no picture; `test/audit/guide_audit_test.dart` uses it.
- **`AUDIT_REPORT=1 flutter test test/golden test/audit`** prints every finding as one line (`AUDIT|scene|look|finding`) and fails nothing: the whole list at once, for a sweep. Without it each scene and look is a test that fails with its findings.
- **Departures from the plan:**
  - **Reduce motion is drawn once per scene, not at every size:** what it changes is what moves, not where things are. Seven looks a scene, not twelve.
  - **The keyboard is walked in two looks** (100 % and 130 % in the smallest window), not all seven: its order doesn't depend on the window's size except where a row of controls wraps, which is there.
  - **A text field and a slider show their focus their own way** (the caret and the accent border; the halo round the thumb), and the audit takes that as shown.
  - **The player is one stop with no ring** (allowed, by name, in its two scenes): its keys are its own.
  - **The component gallery's goldens are not scenes:** it is no screen of the app.
- **What the first pass found, all fixed:**
  - **Loading placeholders stacked in a `Column`** overflowed in a short window or at a larger text size — Live TV's channel list, and Pick categories at 100 % in the smallest window — **and a placeholder with no width of its own drew at no width at all:** Sources, Categories, Settings → Guide, Licences and Pick categories loaded as a blank area where rows should have shimmered. `SkeletonColumn` (`lib/design/components/skeleton.dart`) stretches its placeholders and cuts what doesn't fit; fourteen loading states are on it.
  - **Rows that didn't grow with the text and overflowed:** the Guide's rows (`guideRowHeight`: only the two lines of text in it grow, rounded up to a whole pixel), poster cards' captions in the grids and Home's rails (`cardCaptionHeight`), search's rows, bars and headings (`searchRowHeight`), Pick categories' tiles.
  - **Live TV's cast preview card overflowed by 43 px in the smallest window at 100 %:** it scrolls now, and the focus brings a button into view.
  - **A channel row's progress bar overflowed a narrow row at 130 %:** the bar gives way before the title does.
  - **The casting view's own focus node was a Tab stop that showed nothing:** it holds the focus as the view opens and is skipped by Tab.
- **What the audit can't see, found by reading every fixed height in the screens** (a row that fits its text but didn't grow): the top bar and its chips, Settings' list of sections, Live TV's category rows, the Categories manager's and Hidden channels' rows, Favorites' group headers, the catalogue's chips row, the Library's folder rows, the Downloads rows (the poster keeps its size), a details page's back chip, Settings → Guide's channel rows and offset stepper, the Match picker's rows, Pick categories' group headers, Sync's stage rows. All on `context.scaled` now.
- **Kept at its size, on purpose: the nav rail's items (48 px).** Ten of them must fit the smallest window, and a label at 130 % fits its row; scaled, the rail overflowed there.
- **At 130 % in the smallest window Settings' list of sections scrolls** (ten rows of 52 px): the keyboard and the wheel reach the last; scenes and tests that tap it scroll to it first (`tapShown`).
- **Every golden at 100 % is unchanged** through both parts; two 130 % goldens were re-recorded and looked at (the Appearance page and the Reminders list over Settings: the top bar and the list's rows are taller).
- **Measured:** the 55 goldens as scenes in seven looks are 364 more tests and 30 s more of `flutter test` (4,192 tests in 4 min).
- **Part 3 (2026-10-13, the third session): what the audit couldn't see, and the loose ends.**
  - **One line cut at the right** is caught now: a single line with `TextOverflow.clip` is laid out no wider than its box, so the check asks what it would take with all the room. It found the Guide's channel numbers: 26 px at every text size, so "201" was drawn as "20" at 130 %, and a number past three digits was cut at any size. The slot is as wide as its digits now and grows with the text.
  - **Five more windows** in every scene's looks (1280 × 720, 1366 × 768, 1600 × 900, 2560 × 1440, 3440 × 1440), and `AUDIT_SHOTS=<folder>` to draw each scene in each look: ADR-017 has what looking at them found.
  - **Pictures at 130 %** (`sceneTest(large:)`, 1280 × 800): Live TV, the Guide, search's results, the Library, a details page. The checks passed on all five; looking found two faults, fixed: the Guide's channel column stayed 220 px and cut "Arena Sports 1" to "Arena Sp…" (its name's part grows with the text now, `guideChannelColumnWidth`), and a generated poster's lettering grew with the text and broke a long word in two (it is part of the picture and keeps its size).
  - **Decision 14's loose ends:**
    - **Tab on a two-column details page passed Play: fixed.** A one-stop pane left for "the nearest control below it", which on a page of two columns skipped everything beside the pane, and wrapped to the top of the screen: from a series' episodes Tab went to the way back and the seasons and never came to Play again, and Shift+Tab never reached the episodes. `FocusPane` now leaves for the stop after (or before) it in Flutter's own traversal order, in which a group is one unit — the order Tab already follows between plain controls. So a pane sits in the walk where the eye puts it: the way back → the seasons → Download season → Play → the favorite → Cast → the episodes.
    - **Tab goes round the whole window now, from a pane as from any control:** past a screen's last stop it goes to the nav rail (and the top bar), then into the screen again. Before, a pane that ended a screen wrapped inside the screen and a plain control that ended one went to the rail.
    - **Live TV's categories pane forgot the chosen category, and Shift+Tab into any one-stop list landed at its near end: fixed.** A one-stop pane remembers the item it was left from whether or not it has a controller, and Tab into it (from either side) lands there, else on its first item. A click, an arrow or a screen placing the focus is left alone: the pane only redirects while Tab is held.
    - **The provider's Series page swapped its season when a mark moved Continue: fixed.** The season shown is the one to continue in as the page opens (once the marks are read); from then on only the user changes it, as a library show's page already did.
    - **A subtitle the TV refuses said nothing: fixed.** The casting view went back to what the TV shows, silently. It now also says "<TV> couldn't show those subtitles" (`CastSubtitleRefused`). A TV that just isn't ready is told again when it plays, and nothing is said.
    - **Favorites' drag keeps Flutter's gap: decided, not built.** The four lists that reorder by dragging (Favorites, Sources, Categories, Downloads) all use Flutter's reorderable list, which opens a gap the height of the row where it will land. The canvas's accent line would take a reorderable list of our own for all four. The gap says where the row lands as plainly as a line would, and it is the same in every list; Alt+↑/↓ is the keyboard's way and unchanged.
  - **Offline is said from the app's network status, on every screen.** "You're offline — downloads and local files still play." was Home's own banner; it is the shell's now, under the top bar of every destination, with Open Library (not on the Library itself). The Guide's "Offline · guide from 2 h ago" reads the same status (`appOfflineProvider`), not what its last refresh failed with: a guide server that doesn't answer while the internet is up says "Couldn't refresh".
  - **Scenes for what no golden draws** (`test/audit/states_audit_test.dart`, 25 scenes): every destination with no source; offline over Movies, Series, Live TV, Home, Favorites, the Library and Settings; a movie's page (its details, details that can't be fetched, a provider with no plot); a series' page (its episodes, episodes that can't be fetched, no episodes); Connect; Sync running and failed; Settings → Casting. **They found one fault:** the provider's Series page overflowed in the smallest window at 115 % (2 px) and 130 % (14 px) — its left column had nothing that could give. It scrolls from its bottom now (the actions stay in view), and in a window narrower than 1280 px the column takes the same share of the width instead of a fixed 560 px, which had cut the episodes' titles to "Low …".
- **The table of screens by state.** "golden" is a picture that is also audited in all twelve looks; "audit" is a scene with no picture, audited the same way; "widget test" is tested at one size and text size only, and is not drawn by the audit. The golden and audit cells are the scenes that exist; the widget-test cells are what each screen's own test file is there to cover (hard rule 4), found by its tests' names, not re-read one by one for this table.

  | Screen | Content | Empty | Error | Offline | Loading |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Home | golden | golden (first run); audit (no source) | widget test | audit | widget test |
  | Live TV | golden | audit (no source); widget test (no channels, no match) | widget test | audit | widget test |
  | The Guide | golden; audit (grid, a sheet, reminders) | audit (no guide yet, no source) | widget test (a refresh that failed) | widget test (its line) | widget test |
  | Movies, Series | golden (Movies) | audit (no source); widget test (nothing in a category) | widget test | audit | widget test |
  | A movie's page | audit | audit (no plot) | audit | — | widget test |
  | A series' page | audit | audit (no episodes) | audit | — | widget test |
  | Favorites | golden | audit (no source); widget test (none yet) | widget test | audit | widget test |
  | The Library | golden (five tabs, a movie, a show) | golden (the first run) | widget test | audit | widget test |
  | Search | golden (results, a reminder set) | golden (recent, no results) | widget test | — (it reads this computer) | widget test |
  | The player | golden (a channel, a movie paused, an episode) | — | golden (the failure card) | — | widget test (Preparing…) |

- **The keyboard walks at 130 %** (`WALK_TEXT=130`, under Xvfb, one file per run): `favorites_walk`, `library_walk`, `vod_walk`, `cast_walk`, `reminder_walk`, `hiding_walk`, `home_continue`, `search_keyboard`, `library_cast_walk`. Eight passed as they were. **The ninth found a fault:** in the casting view a film with subtitles has one control more in its row, and at 130 % the row was 5 px too long. Stop casting goes under the other controls where they don't fit side by side; the case is an audit scene now (`casting_view_subtitles`). The walks that don't open the app through `PanelApp` (Live TV's, the Guide's, the sources') have no such switch and were not run at 130 %.
- **Left of step 7, said plainly:** the loading states and most error states are tested by each screen's widget tests at one window and text size, and are **not** drawn by the audit; the exit criterion "every screen passes the audit at 100, 115 and 130 %" is met for the content, empty and offline states above and not yet for those.

### CI after the first push of steps 1–5 (2026-10-13, run 38365432183)
- **Windows, 13 failed:** `dbus_notifier_test.dart` ran there and every test that needs a session bus timed out. The file is `@TestOn('linux')` now, like the other D-Bus tests. Ten of the thirteen were named; **GitHub shows ten annotations a step**, so `tools/ci/failed_tests.dart` now puts one annotation with every failed test's name first when ten or more fail. The three unnamed ones are taken to be the same file's: it has 15 tests, one uses no bus and one expects a bus that doesn't answer, which leaves 13. The next run says.
- **Linux, 1 failed:** `relay_runtime_test.dart` expected a refused connection on a port it had just freed, and another test file's relay had taken it in between. A 404 from whoever has the port is as right as nobody answering.
- **Nothing after the test step ran on either system** (a job stops at its first failed step): the Windows build with the notification package, the Linux build, the fake provider's and fake TV's suites and the integration tests under Xvfb are all still to be seen on CI for Phase 9.


### Performance in profile mode (step 8, 2026-10-13)
Every row of docs/06's table was run again on 2026-10-13 and is in docs/progress.md's Measurements with its date: the frame and launch rows from the profile build on the real display (decision 16), the rest from their benchmarks.

- **Three measurements are new:**
  - **The channel list's scroll** (`integration_test/channel_list_scroll_test.dart`): Live TV's All channels on the `large` catalogue's 50,000 rows. Flings, the wheel and PageDown: no frame over 16 ms (worst 6.7, 7.2, 12.9). ↓ held at thirty rows a second: 1 frame of 364 at 37 ms. The player is a stand-in that "plays" at once, so the list, its guide lines and the preview's words are what is measured. The plan listed End too; **the list has no End or Home** (a first run pressed them and measured nothing), and none was added.
  - **Idle memory** (`test/app/idle_memory_benchmark_test.dart`): the app's own profile build, launched as a person launches it on a data folder with the `large` catalogue and a 286 MB guide (611,328 programmes), left on Home: **372 MB after ten minutes** (374 at most, flat from the first minute; budget 450). The fake panel is stopped before the launch, so the launch sync and guide refresh add nothing to what was prepared.
  - **The player's processor use** (`integration_test/playback_cpu_test.dart`): the fake panel's H.264 1080p50 channel full screen in the app's own player for a minute: **1.5 % of the whole processor** (18.2 % of one of 12 cores; budget 15 %), `vaapi`, no frame dropped.
- **A test window whose size is overridden never draws the player's picture.** The measurement's first run said 6.5 % of a core with 2,996 of 3,000 frames dropped. A probe (`integration_test/manual/picture_probe.dart`) showed why: with `tester.view.physicalSize` set, as every whole-app walk sets it for a fixed layout, Flutter never asks the player's texture for a frame, the picture stays the 1 × 1 placeholder and mpv drops everything. **The app is not affected** (a picture mounted after playback starts is drawn; the probe's second case), and the engine-only tests, the zap benchmark and the old soak never set a size. What it means: **the walks prove that playback is Playing, not that a picture is drawn**; the two tests that need the picture (this one and the soak) use the window as it is and fail if the picture isn't at the stream's size. Why the override does this was not looked into.
- **The two known misses:**
  - **The Guide's PageDown: fixed.** A page was a jump, and a jump builds a whole screen of new rows in one frame (40 ms measured on this day's first run; 59–77 ms in September). **A page now glides there** in the same 200 ms the grid takes through time (`_page` in `guide_grid.dart`: `animateTo`, a second page pressed during the glide starts from where the first was going, a jump with reduce motion). Measured after: **0 of 150 frames over, worst 11.0 ms.**
  - **What that measurement had hidden:** its Home press, counted with the pages until now, is where two of its slow frames were. **Home, back to now from hours away: 2 of 56 frames over, worst 43.6 ms** — every row's programmes change at once. Measured apart from now on; not fixed.
  - **The poster grid's fastest flings: better, not fixed.** Top to bottom of 30,000 posters by fast flings: 7 of 11,900 frames over, worst 23.4 ms (13–17 over in September). Flings, the wheel, ↓ and PageDown at a person's pace: none over. End and Home: one frame each (16.6 and 25.2 ms), a jump across the whole grid. Nothing was changed for it this session.
  - Also still over by a frame or two in the Guide: the sideways wheel (1–2 of ~170, at most 21.1 ms) and → (0–1 of ~180, 17.8 ms).
- **So "every row within its budget" is not met in three places,** all of them a frame or two on a jump or the fastest scroll, none while moving at a person's pace: the Guide's Home and sideways moves, the poster grid's fastest flings and its End and Home, and one frame while ↓ is held in the channel list. They are in docs/progress.md's Known issues.
- **The rest, against their budgets:** cold start 620 ms median (2.0 s) · zap 951 / 966 ms (1.5 / 3 s) · typing into search, no frame over 16 ms (worst 9.1) · decoded pictures 150.0 MB (the cap) · sync 6.0 s, 4.1 s again (60 s) · the 286 MB guide 21.8 s, +164 MB (4 min, +300 MB) · search 8.3 ms median, 40.5 ms worst (50 ms) · library scan 46.3 s, rescan 3.0 s (5 min, 5 s). One run each ("three where a number wanders" was done for the Guide only).


### The soak plays the whole app (step 9, 2026-10-13)
`integration_test/soak_test.dart` was the player and the coordinator alone. It now opens the whole app on the fake panel as `PanelApp` does (database, guide, player, casting with the relay and FFmpeg, the download queue) and plays a channel full screen, with the zap or fault every 90 s it always had. **Every 20 minutes a round of the rest** (decision 15):
1. **A cast:** the channel goes to the fake TV (`tools/fake_receiver`, which fetches what it is sent) for two minutes, then Stop casting. No FFmpeg may be left.
2. **A download and a walk,** with nothing playing: a movie is downloaded from the panel while the Guide and Movies are opened and scrolled with the wheel and Search is typed into. The file is checked against its size, then removed with its library row (the test's own folder: a night of them would fill the disk).
3. **The channel again,** and its picture must be drawn.

- **What it is held to:** every fault recovered within a minute; every round did all of its parts (each miss is a "trouble" line, printed at once and listed in the summary); nothing threw (looked for every minute); memory grew at most 50 MB from minutes 21–25 to the last five, as before.
- **Where the rounds fall:** the first at minute 8, before the baseline, so the relay, the downloads isolate and the picture cache have all run once before memory is first measured; none in the last 8 minutes, so both ends of the comparison are plain playback. An hour has rounds at 8, 28 and 48.
- **One connection,** as most providers give: the source is added with the user's own limit of 1 (the fake panel allows 2). That is why the download waits for playback to stop, as rule 7 has it.
- **A TV of its own each round, at the same address:** what a fake TV keeps of a session (every message, every fetch) goes with it, and is not counted as the app's memory.
- **Driven through the app's own objects, not the keyboard:** the router, the coordinators, the wheel and the text field. The walks cover the keyboard; here a missed key press would cost an hour.
- **The window as it is:** no fixed test-window size (see step 8: a fixed size stops the picture being drawn).
- **A run shorter than 26 minutes doesn't judge memory** (it would measure the warm-up) and says so; `IPTV_SOAK_ROUND_MINUTES=3` brings the rounds closer for such a trial. `tools/soak/run.sh [minutes]` is unchanged.

**What its first rehearsal found (8 minutes on the real display): after a cast, the player would not open.** Both rounds ended with the channel playing and no picture.
- **The cause:** on a screen that doesn't show what plays (Home, the Guide, Movies, Series, Favorites, Library, Search, Settings), nothing watches the playback state's provider, and **Riverpod pauses a provider nobody watches.** The provider forwarded the coordinator's stream as it was (`yield state; yield* changes`), so while paused it kept one state already on its way and queued every later one behind it. During a cast those are "casting" states. The next player to open was handed that past first, took it for a cast starting, closed itself and opened the casting view; the channel went on playing with sound and no picture.
- **Who could meet it:** anyone who cast from a player opened anywhere but Live TV, changed nothing or changed channel on the TV, stopped casting, and then played something. Since Phase 7. No test had played anything after a cast, and the walks can't see a missing picture.
- **The fix, in two places:**
  - `currentThenChanges` (`lib/core/streams/current_state_stream.dart`): a state provider's stream hands over the state as it is **at that moment**, nothing while its listener is paused, and the current state once when the listener is back. Used by the four providers of that shape: playback, the cast, the cast's timeline, the library scan. Changes made in one go arrive as the last of them; what is drawn from state wants exactly that.
  - The player hands over to the casting view only when the coordinator itself says it is casting, not on the provider's word alone.
- **Tested:** the stream's rules; and through the app, a player opened from Settings, cast, zapped on the TV, stopped and opened again (`casting_screens_test.dart`: it fails without the fix).

**The hour on the real display (2026-10-13, after the fix): playback and the rounds clean, memory 10 MB over.**
- **Clean:** 67 reconnects, 0 failures, 9 s the longest without a picture, never more than one connection; 3 rounds, each a cast that played on the fake TV, a download that finished whole, a walk of the Guide, Movies and Search, and the channel back with its picture; no FFmpeg left, nothing thrown.
- **Memory: 60 MB from minutes 21–25 to the last five (budget 50).** It was flat between rounds and stepped up at each: 10-minute medians 837, 955, 939, 963, 966, 991 MB (a debug build with the test harness, RSS 769 → 991, peak 1,050). The first round, before the baseline, added about 70 MB; the second and third about 25 and 20.
- **What grows, from heap snapshots** (`IPTV_SOAK_HEAP=1`: every isolate's heap after a full collection, and the main isolate's classes by size, before the first round and after each; a 30-minute run under Xvfb with rounds every 3 minutes): **the Dart heap levels off** — 272 MB before the first round, 314 after it, then 319, 324, 321, 326, 324, 329, 329, 328 after the ninth (flat over the last three). What it gained over rounds 1–3 was mostly code compiled on first use (Instructions, Code, ICData, ObjectPool: the debug build's JIT, absent from the release app) and the screens' own objects. **What keeps growing is native memory outside it** (the player, the picture's textures, the allocator): RSS after each round 1,594, 1,701, 1,658, 1,749, 1,765, 1,773, 1,774, 1,801, 1,696 MB under Xvfb — up and down, lower after the ninth round than after the fourth. That 30-minute run itself passed: 10 rounds with every part done, 18 reconnects, 0 failures, and 50 MB less at its end than at minute 21. From the third round to the ninth the classes that grew most were small and slow: lists and strings, compiled code, and the guide's programmes with their times and Riverpod's provider records (`EpgProgramme` +385 KB, `DateTime` +489 KB, `ProviderMeta` +404 KB over six rounds) — the Guide is opened twenty minutes later each round, so new programmes are read; worth a look if a night's soak ever shows a slope.
