- **So the 60 MB is not a leak in the app's objects, as far as an hour and a half of soaking can say.** The Dart heap levels off, and native memory that steps up with a burst of work and comes down again is an allocator's high-water mark; a leak would keep its pace. The hour on the display held only two rounds after its baseline. The budget is the night's (docs/06: "8 h soak memory growth"), and the night was not run, by the user's choice; it is what would settle it. Nothing was changed for it.


### The exit (2026-10-14)
**The hour of the new mix, on the real display:** playback and every round clean, memory 60 MB against a budget of 50 ("The soak plays the whole app", above). The heap snapshots show the app's Dart objects levelling off and the rest rising and falling; the night that the budget is written for was not run.

**docs/08's exit criteria for Phase 9, one by one:**
1. **Every budget in docs/06 met in profile mode and recorded: recorded, and met but for a frame or two in three places** (step 8 above): the Guide's Home and sideways moves, the poster grid's fastest flings and its End and Home, and one frame while ↓ is held in the channel list. Everything a person does at a person's pace is within budget; cold start, zapping, idle memory, the player's processor use, sync, the guide's import, search and the library scan are within theirs with room.
2. **An 8-hour soak with faults: not run, by the user's choice** (2026-10-13: "not the 8-hour soak"). In its place: the hour above (no crash, every fault recovered, every round complete, **memory 10 MB over the budget**), and a 30-minute run with ten rounds that passed with memory lower at its end. A slow leak is therefore not ruled out; `tools/soak/run.sh 480` is the night's run, whenever it is wanted.
3. **Every screen passes the keyboard-only audit at 100, 115 and 130 % and with reduce motion: met for every screen and state the audit draws** (55 goldens and 26 scenes in twelve looks). **Not drawn by it:** loading states and most error states, which each screen's widget tests cover at one size; and four walks that don't open the app through `PanelApp` were not run at 130 %.
4. **Copy diagnostics contains no credentials, checked with a real source configured: met** (step 4; a copy of this laptop's database and log, searched for the real host, username and password).
5. **A reminder set from Search or the Guide shows at its programme's start: met in the app's own tests and walks** (`reminder_walk`, the fake notification service). **Not yet seen on the user's desktop:** the one try had both reminders come due with the window in front, so both were in-app messages, as designed; the second try was left for another day (`integration_test/manual/real_reminder.dart`).

**Never run on real Windows** (no PC yet): the notification, hardware decoding and its Off, the diagnostics' graphics cards. CI builds and tests there.

**So the decisions above are accepted as built, and the phase's exit is met except where this section says otherwise:** the night's soak (the user's choice) with the hour's memory 10 MB over, three small frame-budget misses, the audit's undrawn states, and one look at a real notification. Whether those close Phase 9 is the user's call; nothing in them blocks TV-0 or Phase 10.


## ADR-017 · 2026-10-13 · Accepted (the user's request) — The player's controls, and the app in every window size
**Decision:** the full-screen player's OSD is rebuilt around large, named controls, and the app is checked in seven window sizes instead of two. The user asked for both on 2026-10-13: "adding more controlls to the play screen make it clear easy and best UI/UX possible to not make the ppl think to use, and the size of the buttons also clear to the eye even for old ppl, netflix is a good standard to follow, and also make sure the app design is responsive to the screen sizes to look perfect in each size". **This departs from the canvas's `Full-screen player` artboard** (44 px controls, no transport, no Back): the app is the reference for the player until the artboard is redrawn to match.

### What was wrong
- **The controls were seven 40 px grey icons in a corner,** with no words: Audio, Subtitles, Aspect, Mute, Stream info, Cast, and an "Exit full screen" that in fact left the player.
- **Most of the player had no control on screen at all:** the channel before and after, the channel list, the last channel, the volume (only Mute existed, and no key or slider changed the level), the favorite, a file's speed, an episode's next. Each had a key, or nothing.
- **A file's Play, −10 and +10 were small text buttons,** and a click on the picture did nothing.
- **Mute was the player screen's own flag:** leave the player muted, come back, and the button said "Mute" over a silent engine.
- **Every click on a control waited 300 ms:** the double-click detector for full screen sat above the whole OSD, so each click first waited to see whether a second one followed.
- **The OSD was the same size in every window:** on a 1440p monitor the controls were a tenth of an inch.

### What the player has now
- **Back, top left, on every face** (Esc). Netflix's place for it; "Exit full screen" no longer means "leave".
- **A channel, from the left:** Channels (the list, ←), Previous and Next channel (↑ ↓), Last channel (Backspace), then the volume. **At the right:** the favorite's star, Audio, Subtitles, Picture size, Stream info, Cast, Full screen.
- **A movie or an episode, from the left:** Play / Pause (a size up, white, round), Back 10 seconds, Forward 10 seconds, Next episode when there is one, then the volume. **At the right:** Audio, Subtitles, Speed (0.5× to 2×), Picture size, Stream info, Cast, Full screen. The seek bar is thicker (6 px, an 18 px thumb), with the elapsed and remaining time in 15 px figures beside it.
- **Play in the middle of a paused picture,** 104 px: what a click on the picture does, drawn where the eye is. A click on the picture plays and pauses a file; a double click is still full screen.
- **The volume:** Mute and a slider in one tile; **+ and −** in every face, **↑ and ↓** while a file plays (a channel's ↑ ↓ zap), and the mouse's wheel over the picture. It is one level for the full-screen player and the preview (there is one engine), kept across restarts (`player.sound` in the settings table), and told to the engine before the first thing plays. Up while muted is Unmute; Unmute from a level dragged to nothing is 50 %.
- **Controls are 56 px tiles with 28 px icons in white** (Play / Pause 64 px), on a translucent tile so they read over any picture; the shade behind the OSD's edges is docs/05's 85 % (it was 60 %), kept strong under the programme's title as the canvas draws it.
- **A control that has a state says it in words** where the row has room (a 1280 px window and up): "Audio: English", "Subtitles: Off". Narrower, or at 130 % text in a small window, every control is its icon and its tooltip names it with its key. Speed's face is its state ("1.5×"); Picture size's tooltip says the size, and its menu ticks it.
- **The OSD is drawn larger in a larger window:** laid out for 1280 × 800 and scaled with the picture, up to twice (`AppPlayerTokens.scaleFor`): 1.35× at 1920 × 1080, 1.8× at 2560 × 1440. A window smaller than 1280 × 800 keeps the drawn sizes. The channel panel, the zap banner, the number overlay, the stream info, the spinner and the failure card scale with it; menus don't.
- **The OSD stays 6 s while the keyboard is on one of its controls** (3 s otherwise, as before), and when it hides the keyboard goes back to the player: Enter can no longer press a control nobody can see.
- **In full screen the failure card and the spinner are larger too:** an 18 px title, 15 px words, buttons a size up, a 48 px spinner. The preview pane keeps the small ones.
- **Speed and Picture size last as long as the player is open** (the next episode keeps them) and go back to 1× and Fit when it closes, so the preview is never stretched or fast.

### Why these choices
- **Netflix's arrangement, not its bare icons.** Its bar is: transport and volume at the left, what you hear and read and the window at the right, Back at the top left. That is kept. Its icons carry no words; for a viewer who doesn't know them, the two controls whose state is a word (a language, Off) say it.
- **Rejected: a word under every icon.** Twelve controls with captions don't fit the smallest window (1024 px), and a caption can't hold "Subtitles: English".
- **Rejected: fewer controls, the rest in a "More" menu.** A menu is exactly the thinking the request is against, and every control here fits the smallest window as an icon.
- **Rejected: scaling the controls with the text size only.** Text size is the reader's choice for the whole app; the player's problem is distance and window size, which is why it scales with the window.
- **The volume slider and the seek bar are the mouse's, not Tab stops:** the keyboard has keys for both from anywhere in the player, and a slider that takes the arrows would take them from zapping and seeking.
- **A new engine call, `PlayerEngine.setSpeed`** (mpv's `speed`; it keeps the pitch by itself). Nothing else in the engine changed.

### The app in every window size
- **The audit draws every scene in five more windows** at the canvas's text size: 1280 × 720, 1366 × 768, 1600 × 900, 2560 × 1440 and 3440 × 1440 (an ultrawide one, where a pane that only stretches gives itself away), beside 1024 × 640 and 1920 × 1080 at three text sizes. Overflow and cut-off text fail there as they do at 130 %.
- **`AUDIT_SHOTS=<folder> flutter test …` draws each scene in each look into that folder,** so a screen can be looked at in every window. **The checks passed in all twelve looks and the pictures still showed faults,** because a row that stretches and a name cut with "…" break no rule the audit knows. What looking found, all fixed:
  - **Live TV in the smallest window cut every channel's name to a few letters** ("Aren…", "Velo…"): the list was what was left between a 248 px categories pane and a 380 px preview, 260 px. The list now keeps 340 px, and the categories (to 200 px) and the preview (to 300 px) give way to it.
  - **Live TV in a large window only stretched its list** (2,000 px rows at 2560 px, beside a 540 px preview). The preview now grows with the window, as far as its picture leaves 340 px under it for what is on; the list stops at 900 px; what is left on an ultrawide screen stays empty at the right. Under a picture wider than a line should be, the preview's words and its buttons keep to 720 px.
  - **Home's rows held a fixed number of cards** (4, 5 and 8), so a poster was 93 px wide at 1024 px and 400 px wide at 3440 px. A row now holds as many cards as fit at a card's narrowest (260, 210 and 140 px): the canvas's 4, 5 and 8 at 1440 px, 3, 4 and 5 in the smallest window, 8, 10 and 15 at 2560 px.
  - **Pages of rows and forms stretched across the window:** Settings (a row's words at the left edge, its control at the right, 2,300 px apart at 2560 px), Favorites' channels, the Library's Downloads and Folders, and Pick categories. Each now has a measure, 1336 px (`PageMeasure`; the canvas's widest page): at the left for a screen in the shell, where its title and tabs are; in the middle for the onboarding pages, which stand alone. A tab that is a grid (Favorites' movies, the Library's) still fills the window.
  - **Live TV's "1 category hidden · Manage" broke in the middle of a word** in the narrower pane at 130 %: Manage goes under the note where they don't fit side by side.
- **Unchanged on purpose:** the Guide (a wider window shows more hours), the poster grids (more columns), a details page, search and the casting view (each already has a measure of its own).
- **Rejected: centring the shell's pages.** A page whose tabs differ would jump sideways between a tab of rows and a tab that is a grid, in a 1920 px window already.
- **Every golden at the canvas's 1440 px is unchanged.** Re-recorded and looked at: Home at 1280 × 800 and its first run (7 posters across instead of 8 narrower ones), and the four 1920 × 1080 pictures whose layout this changes (Home, Live TV, Favorites, the cast picker over Live TV).

### Built
- `lib/design/components/osd_controls.dart`: `OsdButton`, `OsdVolume`, `OsdControlRow` (decides when the words show; draws the right-hand group a little smaller rather than cut should two long track names not fit), `OsdCenterPlay`, `OsdScale`. `AppSlider(overVideo:, activeColor:)`. `AppPlayerTokens` (`tokens.player`), `AppTypography.timecode`, `AppColors.osdShade`. Six icons the canvas doesn't draw (`arrow-left`, `channel-list`, `replay-10`, `forward-10`, `skip-next`, `last-channel`).
- `lib/features/playback/presentation/`: `player_sound.dart` (`playerSoundControllerProvider`), `player_overlays.dart` (`OsdShade`, `OsdBack`, `OsdTop(onBack:)`, `OsdBottom(leading:, trailing:)`), `vod_osd.dart`, `player_screen.dart`. `PlayerSound` and its place in `UiPreferences` (`lib/core/settings/`).
- **The picture's click detector is under the OSD and under the failure card now, not around them** (`PlayerSurface(onPictureTap:, onPictureDoubleTap:)`; the preview pane uses it too), so a control answers at once; the shades take no clicks, so one on them is a click on the picture.
- `lib/design/components/page_measure.dart`, `AppLayoutTokens` (`tokens.layout`: the page measure, Home's cards, Live TV's panes), `live_tv_screen.dart`'s `_panes`.
- Tests: `layout_tokens_test.dart`, `page_measure_test.dart`, `live_tv_layout_test.dart` (Live TV's panes in four windows); `player_sound_test.dart`; the OSD's controls in `player_screen_test.dart` and `vod_player_screen_test.dart` (its rig moved to `support/vod_player_rig.dart`); goldens `player_osd`, `player_movie_paused`, `player_episode_1920x1080`; the Component Gallery's "Player controls".

### Known limits
- **The canvas's player artboard is out of date** (above).
- **Menus opened from the OSD are the app's usual size,** not scaled with the window.
- **The words on Audio and Subtitles are cut with "…" past 140 px** (a track titled at length); the menu shows the whole name.

## ADR-018 · 2026-10-14 · Accepted (the user's request; the name and mark are the session's choice, to be overridden at will) — The name, the mark, and how the app describes itself
**Decision:** the app is **Bijou** ("Your own little cinema."), its mark an emerald-cut jewel with a play button in its table, and it describes itself as **a player for what the user brings** — never as an IPTV app. The user asked on 2026-10-14: "generate a name and a logo and put it into the app, and consider that the iptv concept is not something will get approved I think, so we need to promote this product as a player for something can be approved". docs/05 "Brand" has the rules; `lib/core/app_identity.dart` the words.

### Why these choices
- **Stores remove apps that look like they sell or point at pirated television,** and "IPTV" in a name or a listing is what their reviews look for; "Xtream Codes" is the panel software of the services police shut down in 2019. A general video player that plays the user's own files, the playlists they add and the services they sign in to, and ships no content, is what VLC, Kodi, Infuse and many approved players are. So: no "IPTV" in the name, the About line, the README, the desktop entry or a listing; the source types renamed **Server login / Playlist link / Playlist file** (the M3U and server words kept in their descriptions, for the user who knows them); example addresses at `example.com` (they were `line.example-provider.tv` and a `get.php` URL, an IPTV panel's shapes).
- **Bijou:** a small jewel and the old name of a small cinema; five letters; says nothing of the source of the pictures; no current video player has it (searched; "Vesper" was dropped for a Flutter video player package of that name, "Spool" and "Wren" for apps in other media). Also considered: Matinee (no conflict found; a word with an accent to lose), Vesper, Spool, Wren.
- **The mark** is the jewel the name says, seen from above, holding a play button: it is a player at 16 px and a jewel at 64. Rejected: a faceted play triangle (Google Play's mark) and a play button in a white box (YouTube's), which the small sizes became before their corners were cut deeper and their lower facets shaded.
- **What a system knows the app by keeps its first name** (the application id, the Windows product name, the package, the database, the executable, the temp folders, the settings file's mark), because each names where something is saved: a Linux data folder, the Windows data folder and the file `flutter_secure_storage` keeps the passwords in (it reads the product name itself), PID files the next launch sweeps. Renaming them would lose the user's sources, passwords and settings, or leave processes unswept. Phase 10's packaging decides whether an installer moves them; until then, only what a person reads changed: window titles, the nav rail and onboarding mark, About, notifications (their app name; GNOME's icon and name come from the desktop entry, whose file name is the application id), the files the app suggests (`bijou-settings-…json`, `bijou-diagnostics-…txt`), the Windows file description (Task Manager), the TV's sender name.
- **Downloads:** Videos + `Bijou` from now on — except where Videos + `IPTV Player` already is: the downloads made before stay together, nothing is moved (hard rule 11).

### Left for the user
- Say if you want another name, or another mark: one constant (`appName`), one drawing (`tools/brand/make_icons.py`, `JewelPainter`), the window titles in `linux/runner/my_application.cc` and `windows/runner/main.cpp`, and `FileDescription` in `windows/runner/Runner.rc`.
- To see the icon in the dock now: `flutter build linux --release`, then `tools/install_desktop_entry.sh`.
- The canvas still says "IPTV Player" in its rail and onboarding header: the app is the reference for the name and mark until it is redrawn.

## ADR-019 · 2026-10-14 · Accepted (a bug the user found on the Bedroom TV) — A relayed file on its own clock: the TV's bar shows the film's length and time
**Decision:** the relay rewrites the boxes of its continuous fragmented MP4 so the TV reads the file's length and the file's time (docs/04 "The TV's own bar"): the length into `mvhd`/`tkhd`/`mdhd` and a new `mehd`, every `tfdt` moved by where the stream begins in the file, `mfra` left out. The app treats the TV's time as the file's, floored at the stream's start, and falls back to adding the start when a TV turns out to count from 0. The user, 2026-10-14: "lets say the movie is one hour and when u pause it on tv to see the progress bar, u see the full length is 40 seconds and once we reach to the end of it it will extend to a new number so its nver true, it feels like a buffer load not the actual progress bar".

### Why this way
- **Reproduced before fixed:** headless Chrome (the receiver's media stack) playing the relay's stream showed the duration growing with what it had read: 2 s, 12 s, ... — the TV's symptom. The LOAD already carried the film's length; the receiver shows its media element's.
- **FFmpeg can't be told:** with `empty_moov` the header precedes any length, and its MP4 muxer starts every fragment at 0 (tried `-output_ts_offset`, `-copyts`, `-use_editlist 0`, `frag_discont`, on 8.1). So the relay edits the few boxes that carry times, between FFmpeg and the TV, which costs nothing per picture.
- **The film's time, not just its length:** with only the length, a resume at 30:00 of an hour would read 0:05 of 30:00 on the TV — another bar that "is never true". Chrome takes a stream whose first fragment is at 10:01 as 10:01 of the whole (measured), and a LOAD's `currentTime` there lands on the first picture.
- **The length only from the stream:** a catalogue's runtime may be minutes off, and a TV told too short a length would end the film early. With no measured length, the header stays as it was (the TV's length then grows, as before).
- **A safety net because no real TV has run it yet:** if a TV counted the stream from 0 after all, adding nothing would make the app's timeline and saved place wrong after every seek. The first report past a second, when the stream began 10 s or more in, tells the two apart (the film's time is never below the stream's start), and a "counts the stream from 0" line goes to the log. The matrix's file rows fail on that line, or on "says the file is … long", so its run on the Bedroom TV answers both.

### Not done
- ~~Not yet run on a real TV~~ — run on the Bedroom TV on 2026-10-14 (below).
- A seek with the TV's own remote in a relayed stream still goes back to the stream's start: the stream has no index to seek in. The app's seek (laptop, phone) is the way, as before.

### On the Bedroom TV (2026-10-14, the seventh session)
- **The user's report, with the fix in:** a 1:40:00 film moved to the TV from the laptop's player showed on the TV's bar a length equal to where it was, about 47 minutes, growing with it to the second. The app's log: "Facts from the player", then "Bedroom TV says the file is 2748 s long, not 6000 s".
- **Why:** the relay writes a length only from `StreamFacts.duration`, and the facts a cast takes from the laptop's player (Cast while watching) had none: `streamFactsFromPlayer` read the codecs and tracks, never the length mpv had read. With no length the header stays as FFmpeg writes it, and the TV shows what it has read as the length; on the film's clock now, so from where it resumed. A cast started from a film's page is probed and always had the length; every test of ADR-019 went that way.
- **The fix:** `PlaybackHandover.duration` (the player's reading of the stream, never the catalogue's runtime) → `streamFactsFromPlayer(duration:)`. The coordinator's test of Cast while watching a film fails without it (the length `null`).
- **The TV honours the header:** a scratch probe cast the same 10-minute film, resumed at 5:00, four ways: the relay's form (fragmented MP4, chunked, no length), with a `Content-Length` and Range requests, with a `Content-Length` and no Range, and a VOD HLS playlist. All four reported 10:00 long and 5:00 in. No other form of stream is needed.
- **Matrix rows 17–23, and a new row 24** (Cast while the laptop plays a film at 3:00, the user's way), on the Bedroom TV: **all eight passed** (17:27–17:33 local, `build/real_cast_run/matrix.md`). The MP4s the TV reads itself sought in 0.1 s; through the relay, seeks in 0.5–0.7 s and onto the MP4 made beside it in 0.1 s; no row logged "says the file is" or "counts the stream from 0", so the TV showed each film's length and counted on its clock; subtitles inside and beside the files switched in 0.2–0.4 s; the resume at 3:00 resumed at 3:00; row 24 took "Facts from the player", as the user's cast did, and moved the film at 3:02 on one provider connection, with the right length. Each played 3.4–4.4 s after Cast, the receiver's launch included.
- **The matrix runner took the TV's address for this computer's** (`Socket.address`, the client's bug of 2026-10-13, left in the runner): it now uses `pickLocalAddress`, as the client does.
- ~~**The Bedroom TV is a classic Chromecast** (Cast firmware "mediashell", from its `eureka_info`): no Android, so not a TV for TV-0.~~ **Wrong** (the eighth session): "mediashell" is also the name of the Cast receiver on Android TV (`com.google.android.apps.mediashell`). The Bedroom TV's SSDP `SERVER` is `Linux/5.15.170-android14…`, the Living Room's Google TV's to the character, and Android TV's remote service listens on its ports 6466/6467: **it is a Google TV**, and TV-0 can run on it once USB debugging is on.

### The user's film, on the Bedroom TV (2026-10-14, the eighth session)
- **The length is right:** UNABOMBER played on the laptop from 48:42 (a scratch integration test: the real provider, mpv, the cast coordinator, the relay, as the app wires them; with the user's yes, the provider idle), then Cast moved it: "Facts from the player", and the TV's first report said **1:40:06**, the length mpv had read (the catalogue says 1:40:00). Twice. Nothing logged "says the file is".
- **But the TV closed the receiver** 0.5 s after it began to play, nobody near it: ADR-024.

## ADR-020 · 2026-10-14 · Accepted (the user's request: "the steps to get the apk ready … then start doing that"; both TVs offline) — TV-0 begun without a TV: the Android toolchain, the Google TV project, and what the emulator showed
**Decision:** TV-0 starts with what needs no TV. The laptop builds the Google TV app; the repository has its Android project (`android/`), whose app is **`io.github.yasiralobaidi.bijou`**, named Bijou, in Google TV's launcher with its banner; the app starts on a Google TV emulator and runs its shared code there; CI builds the APK on every push. The engine comparison of docs/07 (media_kit against Media3, on the device) waits for a TV.

### The toolchain (docs/setup.md)
- **Installed in the user's home, no sudo:** Temurin JDK 21.0.12.1 (`~/develop/jdk`, a link to `~/develop/jdk-21.0.12.1+1`) and the Android SDK in `~/Android/Sdk`, where Flutter looks first: command-line tools 23 (now the "Android CLI", 1.0.16500706; `sdkmanager` still works), platform-tools 37.0.1 (`adb` 1.0.41), platform 36, build-tools 36.0.0, NDK 28.2.13676358 (Flutter's, for sqlite3's native build), emulator 37.2.12, and the Google TV system image `android-36;google-tv;x86_64`. Gradle added platform 35 itself (a plugin compiles against it). `flutter config --android-sdk ~/Android/Sdk --jdk-dir ~/develop/jdk`; `flutter doctor` is clean. Downloads checked against their published SHA-256/SHA-1. The SDK's licences were accepted as part of the install the user asked for.
- **JDK 21, not 17:** Flutter 3.47.4's template is AGP 9.1.0 on Gradle 9.3.1 with Kotlin 2.4.0, which need 17 or newer; 21 is the JDK Android Studio bundles, so it is the one those versions are most used with.
- **The emulator:** `bijou_google_tv` (Television 1080p, 2 GB like the Chromecast with Google TV). Google TV images in x86_64 exist only for Android 16 (API 36), and Flutter no longer builds for 32-bit x86, so the emulator runs Android 16 where the Living Room TV runs 14. Run headless (`-no-window`), never on the user's display.
- **Memory:** the template gives Gradle 8 GB. On 2026-10-14 a build (Gradle ~4.4 GB and two Kotlin daemons) beside the emulator ran the 15 GB laptop out of memory: systemd-oomd killed the user's GNOME session and the kernel their editor. `android/gradle.properties` now caps Gradle at 3 GB and Kotlin's daemon at 1 GB (the build needs no more), and the rule is: build, stop Gradle (`./gradlew --stop`), only then start the emulator.

### The project
- **`flutter create --platforms=android` in a scratch copy**, `android/` brought over alone, so nothing else in the repository changed but `.metadata` (android in its list) and `analysis_options.yaml` (which Flutter itself told to exclude `android/**`).
- **The application id `io.github.yasiralobaidi.bijou`** (namespace and Kotlin package too): ADR-018 kept the desktop's `…iptvplayer` only because data is saved under it; on Android nothing is saved yet, and an id is forever once installed, so the TV app takes the name now.
- **The manifest:** `LEANBACK_LAUNCHER` beside `LAUNCHER`; `android:banner` (320 × 180, `drawable-xhdpi`, the jewel and the name white on the accent, drawn by `tools/brand/make_icons.py`, which also draws the launcher icons for every density); `android.software.leanback` and `android.hardware.touchscreen` not required (docs/07); `INTERNET`; `usesCleartextTraffic` (many services and playlists are plain http). The launch window is the dark look's background, so a TV never flashes white before the first frame. Core library desugaring, which `flutter_local_notifications` needs to build. Signed with the debug key until TV-5's packaging (sideloaded, docs/07).
- **Start-up by form factor** (`FormFactor`, ADR-008): on the TV, no window to set up (`window_manager` has no Android side), and none of what a TV doesn't have (docs/07): FFmpeg's sweeps, the cast's notices, the download folder, the queue, the library's scans. Reminders, syncs and guides run as on the desktop. Android starts in touch mode, which draws no focus, so on the TV focus is always drawn (`FocusHighlightStrategy.alwaysTraditional`).
- **A player bug the emulator found, for Windows too:** the engine set mpv's `sub-files` to `""` to clear the last file's subtitle files. The system libmpv 0.34.1 on Linux reads that as an empty list; the newer libmpv media_kit ships for Android (and for Windows) reads it as one file named "" and fails every play ("Can not open external file ."), so nothing played and the watchdog reconnected forever. The option is now left out when there are none, and `change-list sub-files clr` empties the list, which both read the same way. Windows playback has never been run (ADR-003), so this is the likely first thing it would have hit there.

### What the emulator showed (Google TV, Android 16, a debug build)
- **Works:** install; the launcher's banner row; a cold start in 3.1–3.6 s (`am start -W`, debug, emulated); Welcome with focus drawn and the D-pad moving it; Connect with Google TV's own keyboard (Gboard); a Server login to the fake panel at `10.0.2.2:8899`, its test in 161 ms; the sync (240 channels, 120 movies, 24 series) and the guide's import (39,168 programmes in 1.75 s) in their background isolates, drift and sqlite3 included; Home; Live TV; a channel and a film open in the player, MediaCodec's H.264 decoder starts, the sound decodes (mp2, 0.6–0.7 s cached, nothing dropped), the film's time runs, seeks and Start over work.
- **No picture on the emulator:** mpv cannot create its GL context on the emulator's EGL (`eglCreateContext: EGL_BAD_ATTRIBUTE`), with the software renderer and with the laptop's GPU alike; nothing is drawn, nothing else fails. It says nothing about a real TV, which is what TV-0's comparison on the device is for.
- **For TV-1 (the shell, the focus audit, the remote's keys):** a 1080p TV is 960 × 540 logical pixels (density 2), smaller than the desktop's smallest window, and the rail overflows by 44 px; the D-pad can't leave a text field (Tab can; a remote has none); Pick categories opens with nothing focused; in the player ←/→ seek, so the remote can't reach the controls; the remote's Home leaves the app (the desktop's "Home starts over"); desktop words ("this computer", "your system keychain", Ctrl K) and the cast button are on the TV.

### Not done (TV-0's rest, needs a TV)
- **The engine comparison** of docs/07 on a real Google TV: media_kit on the device (does it draw? start and zap times, 4K HEVC HDR, AC-3/E-AC-3 passthrough, frame-rate matching, MPEG-TS, memory on 2 GB) against a Media3 (ExoPlayer) plugin, built for the comparison. Then the ADR that picks one.
- **Running on the device:** Developer options and network debugging on the TV, `adb connect <address>:5555`, `adb install`. And reading its ABI there (`getprop ro.product.cpu.abilist`): the Chromecast with Google TV is said to run a 32-bit system on its 64-bit chip; the APK carries armeabi-v7a, arm64-v8a and x86_64 either way.

## ADR-021 · 2026-10-14 · Accepted (the user's request: "add the option of starting from beginning, in case the user needed that") — Start over, as a button, in the player and the cast
**Decision:** a film's or an episode's player has a **Start over** button (a circular arrow around a play triangle) after Back 10 s and Forward 10 s, always there, not only after a resume; the casting view has the same after its seek buttons; Home does it in both. The film's page (Resume / Start over) and the library's "Play from the start" were already there.

### Why
- **The player only said "Resumed from 24:10 · Home starts over"**, for 5 seconds, as text: nothing to click with a mouse, nothing a remote can reach, and a remote's Home leaves the app. A button in the controls is there whenever the controls are.
- **Always, not only after a resume:** a film watched a while ago, a scene to see from the start again, an episode opened from Continue watching; and a button that comes and goes moves the others under the focus.
- **After the seeks, not beside Play:** the controls are reached with →/Tab from Play, and a stray press there would lose the place, which nothing gives back.
- **The cast too:** a cast starts where the laptop's player is, and the casting view had no way to the start but dragging its bar.
- **The icon:** the set's replay arrow (as Back 10 s) with a play triangle in it, so it reads as "play again" and not as "back 10 s" or "previous episode" (⏮ beside Next episode would).

## ADR-022 · 2026-10-14 · Accepted (the user's request: "design a better and more creative logo", then of the concepts drawn: "use it and add it to the project properly") — The mark: a trillion, the jewel that is the play button
**Decision:** Bijou's mark is a **trillion** — the triangular cut of a jewel — pointing right, so the jewel *is* the play button: nine crown facets around a half-size table, lit from the top left, white over the accent's deep shade, on the accent's tile. It replaces ADR-018's emerald cut with a play button in its table. Smaller, the facets merge: one band along each edge from a 28 px tile, the triangle alone under it (`JewelDetail`). docs/05 "Brand" has the rules; the concepts are on the canvas https://claude.ai/artifact/7dUdNr7Ry8W5Lw4GYZp7as.

### Why
- **One idea, not two stacked:** the emerald cut held a play button like a sticker on a box; the trillion is a real cut whose outline is the play symbol, so "a jewel" (the name) and "a player" (the app) are the same shape at every size.
- **It degrades on its own terms:** the emerald cut needed a second drawing under 64 px (a bigger jewel, deeper corners, three shaded facets) so it would not read as YouTube's white box. The trillion keeps its outline at every size and only loses facets: at 16 px it is a play triangle on a tile, which is right for a player.
- **ADR-018 rejected "a faceted play triangle" as Google Play's mark.** The user chose this one knowing the concepts side by side, and it keeps clear of Play: one color, sharp corners, a cut stone's table and crown facets, always on its rounded tile; Play's mark is a rounded triangle in four colored pieces on nothing. Store review (Phase 10, the Google TV listing) should still be read with this in mind.
- **Also drawn:** Solitaire (a ring whose stone is a small screen: says "jewel" at once, but a blur under 24 px) and Glint (a TV with a sparkle on its screen: clear, but generic).

### How it is kept one drawing
- `JewelPainter.corners`, `tableScale`, `facetShades`, `bandShades` and `JewelDetail.forTile` in `lib/design/components/app_mark.dart` and `CORNERS`, `TABLE_SCALE`, `FACET_SHADE`, `BAND_SHADE` and `detail()` in `tools/brand/make_icons.py` are the same numbers; `test/design/components/app_mark_test.dart` compares the painter's faces and shades with `design/brand/bijou_icon.svg`, which the script writes, so changing one side alone fails a test.
- The script wrote every icon again: the SVG master and its Linux scalable copy, the Linux hicolor PNGs, the Windows `.ico`, Android's launcher icons and Google TV's banner. `brand_mark_sizes.png` (and its amber twin: the mark takes the accent) is the golden of every size.

## ADR-023 · 2026-10-14 · Accepted (found by the casting matrix on the Bedroom TV, the user's "do all the tasks related to the bedroom tv") — A live continuous stream that ends is loaded again even when the TV doesn't say so
**Decision:** when the relay's live continuous stream to the TV ends (`CastRelayEnded(tvLeft: false)`: FFmpeg died or the provider's stream ended for good), the coordinator waits `CastTimings.liveEnd` (10 s) for the TV's IDLE/FINISHED, which reloads it (ADR-004), and reloads it itself if none comes. The reload counts against the same budget (3 within 2 minutes), so a stream that keeps ending still fails.

### Why
- **Seen on a real TV:** the matrix's row 11 (the relay's FFmpeg killed during a continuous fMP4) failed on the Bedroom TV: the stream ended, the TV stayed BUFFERING, and nothing was loaded for 40 s. The fake TV always said FINISHED, which is what ADR-004 measured on the Living Room TV in Phase 0.
- **The TV does both:** after the fix, row 11 alone and after row 10 passed, the TV saying FINISHED 5.4–5.5 s after the end both times. Its statuses weren't logged in the failing run, so whether it said nothing or said something the app didn't take as ours isn't known; the wait covers both, since it looks only at whether a LOAD followed.
- **10 s:** the TV plays out about 4 s of buffer before FINISHED (ADR-004); 10 s leaves room for that and for a FINISHED that comes late, and is short against a frozen picture.
- **Not on the TV leaving the stream** (`tvLeft`): that is the TV's own choice (a new LOAD, a stop) and has its own handling.

### Checked by
`cast_coordinator_test` "live streams that end": a continuous stream that ends with the TV saying nothing is loaded again; the TV saying FINISHED in time loads it once; the TV leaving is not an end. On the Bedroom TV: row 11 twice (above).

## ADR-024 · 2026-10-14 · Accepted (found checking the user's film on the Bedroom TV; the user: "Nobody touched the TV") — A Google TV that closes the receiver as a cast wakes it: launched again
**Decision:** the Cast client reads `isStandBy` and `isActiveInput` from RECEIVER_STATUS (HDMI-CEC), and a join that launched the receiver on a device that was asleep (in standby, or showing another input) says so (`CastJoined.woke`). When that device closes the receiver with nothing else running (`CastEnd.closedOnDevice`) within `CastTimings.wokeClose` (20 s) of such a launch, the coordinator keeps the session: it launches the receiver again after `relaunchAfter` (2 s), trying up to `relaunchTries` (5) more times `relaunchEvery` (1 s) apart while the TV answers LAUNCH_ERROR, and loads what was cast again where it was (a film from its place, the relay renewed there; a channel live). No toast; it is logged. Once per launch that woke the TV: a second close, a close after the window, or a close of a receiver that was joined running ends the session as before ("Casting stopped on …"); refused every time, the cast fails with Try again.

### Why
- **Seen:** the user's film (ADR-019, "The user's film"): the right length, then the Bedroom TV closed the receiver 0.5 s into the film, with nobody at the TV. The matrix's row 24, on the fake panel's film, did the same 3.8 s in; and the runner called it passed, since its `hold` didn't look (it does now).
- **Measured** with a scratch watcher, a second sender that only listens to the TV's statuses: before each cast the TV was in standby (`isStandBy` true, `isActiveInput` false); the LAUNCH turned it on; **the receiver closed 7.2–7.4 s after the TV woke, four times of four**, whatever it was doing (loading, playing, showing no media); the TV went back to standby about 30 s after. **Awake, it doesn't:** a receiver launched on the TV already on stayed 20 s (no media, a sender connected) and played the film 30 s (row 25). The seventh session's matrix, 24 rows with the TV on, never saw a close.
- **A launch right after is refused:** launches 0.5 s and 1.2 s after the close got `LAUNCH_STATUS USER_ALLOWED` and then `LAUNCH_ERROR SYSTEM_ERROR`; one 2.3 s after was taken, the receiver up 4.2 s after the close. The first fix launched at once and failed on the TV ("launchRefused"); hence the 2 s, and the tries.
- **Why the TV closes it** isn't known. Likely: the TV coming on renegotiates the HDMI link, the Google TV's display mode changes, and the receiver goes with it. The app can't prevent it, only start again.
- **Only after a wake, and only within 20 s:** a close of a receiver someone is watching is theirs (Back on the remote); fighting it would be wrong. A close within seconds of the launch that woke the TV is the TV's.
- **No toast:** the TV comes on, blinks, and plays; a notice would only be noise.

### Checked by
`cast_status_json_test` (standby and input read, tolerantly); `cast_v2_receivers_test` (a launch on a TV in standby says it woke it, one already on doesn't; the fake TV closing the receiver as it wakes ends the session as closed on the device); `cast_coordinator_test` "a TV the cast woke" (launched again once, the film where it was on the same relay; refused twice, then taken; refused every time → failed; a second close ends it; an awake TV's close ends it; a close after the window ends it; a channel). The fake TV models the Bedroom TV (`standBy`, `closeAfterWake`, `refuseAfterWakeClose`); the matrix's new row 25 casts to the TV in standby and passes on the fake TV (closed, refused once, launched again). On the Bedroom TV: row 25 with the TV awake (no wake, played through); row 25 with the TV in standby 8 s (woke, **did not close**: played 33 s, the window opened and lapsed unused). **The launch again has not run on the real TV:** the one cast before it that closed (the user's film, 01:26) predates the 2 s wait and failed on the refusal; the cast meant to check it, after 3 minutes of standby, didn't happen (the session's tools stalled for an hour past the TV's window). **So 4 wakes of 5 closed the receiver:** the four after 3 minutes to 4 hours in standby; not the one 8 s after the TV went dark, which likely hadn't let go of its HDMI link yet.

**Addendum (2026-10-15, the ninth session): row 25 again, and the TV behind the Bedroom TV.** The Bedroom TV is a **Chromecast with Google TV 4K** ("sabrina", Android 14, 2 GB, a 32-bit system: `armeabi-v7a` only) in **HDMI 3 of the 58" Hisense Roku TV** (the user: "Yes, same TV"; the Roku names that input "Chromecast"). So "the TV in standby" is the Chromecast asleep, and "the TV coming on" is the Roku TV coming on through HDMI-CEC. Tonight the Roku TV was switched off through its network control (ECP `PowerOff`, "DisplayOff") and the Chromecast put to sleep (`KEYCODE_SLEEP`, `isStandBy` true); 4 minutes later row 25 cast the test film: the Chromecast woke, played 40 s, **and the Roku TV never came on** (a 0.5 s poll of its ECP read `DisplayOff` and its own menu throughout); nothing closed the receiver ("launched again 0 times"). That fits this ADR's guess: the close follows the TV powering up and renegotiating HDMI, which didn't happen. **The launch again is still unseen on the real TV.** To see it, the TV has to be switched off with its own remote (which may tell the Chromecast by CEC, as ECP didn't) and the cast must turn it on.

## ADR-025 · 2026-10-15 · Accepted (the user: "continue the work", USB and wireless debugging on, then "make sure the tv version is for tv, so no need for keyboard shortcuts or streaming options … keep the PC features for pc too", and "if there is something will enhance the performance do it") — TV-0 on the Bedroom TV: media_kit stays, drawing straight into the surface; and Bijou on a TV is the TV's
**Decision:** On Android the engine stays **media_kit (libmpv)**, with mpv's **direct output** (`vo=mediacodec_embed`, `hwdec=mediacodec`: MediaCodec decodes into the picture's surface) in place of mpv's `gpu` output, which copied every decoded frame back (`mediacodec-copy`). Media3 isn't built: on this TV it could win only on what nobody here needs yet (passthrough, HDR, native 4K; below). And on a TV the app is the TV's: no Cast, no keycaps, no library or downloads, no keyboard page, no window controls; the remote's keys work; playback stops when the app leaves the screen; and long playback with no key asks "Still watching?". The desktop is unchanged.

### The device
Chromecast with Google TV 4K, Android 14 (`UTTC.250917.004`), Amlogic, 4 × Cortex-A55, Mali-G31, 2 GB (about 840 MB free with Bijou running), a **32-bit system** (`ro.product.cpu.abilist` = `armeabi-v7a,armeabi`). Its HDMI output (to the Roku TV) is **1080p 59.94 Hz**; the TV's EDID offers 23.976/24/29.97/30/59.94/60 Hz at 720p, 1080p and 2160p, and **no 50 Hz mode at all**, and AC-3, E-AC-3 and IEC 61937 for audio. Paired over Wi-Fi (Settings → System → Developer options → Wireless debugging → Pair device with pairing code; `adb pair <ip>:<port> <code>`, then `adb connect <ip>:<port>`): no cable, and the laptop stays paired. Impeller runs on Vulkan there.

### The comparison (release builds, the user's provider and the fake panel)
| | `gpu` (`mediacodec-copy`), media_kit's default | **direct (`mediacodec_embed`)** |
|---|---|---|
| beIN 720p50 H.264, dropped frames | **904 in 28 s** (about 32 of 50 a second) | **≈ 0** (7 → 6) |
| busiest thread | CodecLooper **88 %** (the copy) | Flutter's raster 19 % |
| device CPU, user | 130–138 % of 400 | 97 % |
| channel start, the user's provider | 1.1–2.2 s (median 1.9) | 1.2–1.7 s, and 8.1–8.6 s three times (below) |
| channel start, the fake panel on the LAN | — | 0.7–0.8 s |
| 1080p50 HEVC (beIN "4K", a match) | — | 3 dropped in 20 s |
| 2160p25 H.264 13.8 Mb/s / HEVC 14.9 Mb/s + E-AC-3 5.1 | — | hardware, 1 / 2 dropped; Bijou 156 MB |
| 1080i50 H.264 | — | the Amlogic decoder deinterlaces it itself (25 fps out, 0 dropped) |
| Flutter's OSD and panels over the picture | yes | yes (the picture is still a texture) |

- **The 8 s starts are the network, not the output:** the old decoder was released at :31.75 and the next created at :39.68; nothing ran in between but the stream's opening. The fake panel on the LAN never took more than 0.8 s, and the gpu build's six zaps happened not to hit one. Likely the provider holding a new connection while it still counts the old one (it allows one).
- **Passthrough doesn't work in media_kit's libmpv on Android:** media_kit forces `ao=opensles`, which can't carry encoded audio; with `ao=audiotrack` and `audio-spdif=ac3,eac3`, mpv opened its pass-through decoder (`spdif_ac3`) and then nothing: no audio output, so the clock never started, and the watchdog reloaded the channel 9 s later. Left off (AC-3/E-AC-3 are decoded to PCM, which plays). This is Media3's one sure win.
- **Frame-rate matching can't help this TV's channels:** the TV takes no 50 Hz mode, so 50 fps sports play at 59.94 Hz whichever player draws them. 24p films could switch (the window's `preferredDisplayModeId`, an Android call either engine can make).
- **What a texture can't do:** the picture goes through Flutter's 1080p layer, so a 4K stream is shown at 1080p (the HDMI output is 1080p now anyway) and HDR is shown as SDR. Media3 with a `SurfaceView` could output both. Not needed by anything the user watches now; revisit with Media3 if HDR, native 4K or passthrough is wanted (`spike/tv_engine_spike/`, as ADR-020 planned).
- **The emulator's "no picture"** (ADR-020) was the emulator's EGL: the real TV draws with both outputs.

### Bijou on a TV is the TV's (docs/07)
Everything is gated on `FormFactor` (`formFactorProvider`, which `bootstrap()` sets from the platform; unset it is the desktop, since a widget test's platform is Android). On the TV:
- **No casting** (`castsToTvsProvider`): no Cast button in the top bar, the player, a channel's menu or a title's page; no C key; no casting bar or view; no Settings → Casting.
- **No keycaps** (`KeyHints`, above everything the router draws): tooltips, menus and the search field draw none, nor do the Guide's sheet, the reminders dialog or the search overlay's legend. Hints that named keys say what the remote does: "Hold OK on a channel, then choose Add to favorites".
- **No library or downloads** (the rail has no Library; Settings has no Downloads & library), **no Keyboard shortcuts page**, **no playlist files** (Welcome's "Open a file instead" and Connect's "Playlist file" are gone: a TV has no files), and "this TV" where the desktop says "this computer" (Welcome; Connect: "kept encrypted on this TV").
- **The player:** no volume slider or mute (the remote's volume works the TV) and no full-screen button (no window). The remote's keys: Back as Esc (closes stream info or the channel panel first, then leaves), Channel Up/Down zap, Info is stream info, play/pause, fast forward and rewind in a film, Stop leaves. The Guide key opens the Guide (as G does).
- **OK the Android TV way** (`TvSelectKey`): a press activates on release, and holding it half a second opens the focused item's menu, the remote's only way to a menu (and so to favorites).
- **Focus at launch:** the first screen's first control (Home's rows come with their data, so the shell tries for 2 s); Pick categories starts on Finish.
- **Playback stops when the app leaves the screen** (`TvAwayObserver`, `PlaybackCoordinator.stopWhileAway` / `resumeAfterAway`): Home, another app, the device asleep; back on screen it plays again (a film where it was, paused if it was). **Measured on the TV:** the old build kept its connection to the panel 28 s after Home and counting; the new one closed it within 4 s and opened it again on return. (A first figure of 2 MB/s "after Home" counted the whole device, the launcher's trailers included; the connection count is the measure.)
- **"Still watching?"** (`StillWatching`): 4 hours of playback with no key from the remote asks, Keep watching focused; no answer in 60 s stops playback and goes Home. **Why:** the Roku TV went dark (ECP) and the Chromecast never knew (awake, active input, Bijou's window keeping the screen on as a player must): Bijou went on reading the stream at about 1.9 MB/s to a dark TV. Only a question can tell a watcher from an empty room.
- **Lighter on 2 GB:** the decoded-image cache is 64 MB on a TV (150 on the desktop); a title page with no backdrop shows its poster decoded 24 px wide and scaled, in place of a full-screen blur (docs/07: no blurs on the TV).

### Diagnostics on a TV
On Android the app's log also goes to logcat (redacted, as every output): a release build's private folder is out of adb's reach. Each play logs its first frame (`First frame in 1243 ms (mediacodec, mediacodec_embed)`), on the desktop too. A build with `--dart-define=BIJOU_MPV_LOG=ao,ad,cplayer` adds mpv's verbose lines from those modules.

### Checked by
`mpv_options_test` (the direct output always asks for MediaCodec); `playback_away_test` (a channel and a film stop and play again; a paused film comes back paused; a failure stays; something started meanwhile is left; the observer's lifecycle order); `tv_select_key_test`; `still_watching_test` (asks after the while, stops with no answer, a key starts the count again, Keep watching goes on); `tv_shell_test` (the TV without Cast, keycaps, Library and the desktop's Settings sections; the desktop with all of them); `shortcut_table_test` (the Guide key). On the Bedroom TV: everything above, by remote keys over adb. **Not checked on the TV:** holding OK (adb can't hold a key: `--duration` sends both events at once; a real remote sends repeats), and the launch again of ADR-024.

## ADR-026 · 2026-10-15 · Accepted (the user: "fix my bug list (sound, text fields and the keyboard, the player's controls by remote, a film's pause, Favorites' tabs, the rail's toggle, Home's See all, the round TV icon) and the rest of the TV experience, with performance, testing each on the TV") — Bijou by remote: its own keyboard, the shell's edges, the player's OK
**Decision:** On a TV (ADR-025's `FormFactor`; the desktop unchanged unless said):
- **Text is typed on Bijou's own keyboard** (`RemoteKeyboard`, `showRemoteKeyboard`, lib/design/components/remote_keyboard.dart). A field asks Android for no keyboard (`TextInputType.none`: a plugged-in keyboard still types into it), the arrows move between fields as between anything (`RemoteTextEntry`; one with nothing that way passes the key on, so Left at a screen's edge still reaches the rail), and **OK opens the keyboard**: a panel at the bottom with the field's name and text (dots for a password, Show), ten keys a row (digits, then QWERTY with `@ . - _`), an address row for a URL (`http:// https:// : / .com .net`), ABC, `#+=`, Space, Delete, Clear and Done. Each key edits the field at once and calls its `onChanged` (Search shows results as the words come); **Done** closes it and submits; **Back** closes it and keeps the text. `TvMode` (lib/design/focus/tv_mode.dart, beside `KeyHints`) tells the design system it is on a TV. **Search** opens with the keyboard up; Done leaves the results to ↓/↑, OK opens the highlighted one, ↑ at the top brings the keyboard back.
- **Why not Google TV's keyboard:** on the Bedroom TV it took no key from the remote in a Flutter field — not the D-pad, injected either as a keyboard's or as a D-pad's arrow, and not even Back, which closed the whole Connect form behind it (Bijou's window flags are ordinary; the keys simply went past it). The user hit it in the search bar and Movies' filter. An app's own keyboard is also the TV pattern (YouTube, Netflix) and can carry an address row.
- **The shell's edges:** in the content, **Left and (on a TV) Up first move within the screen**, and only at its edge go to the rail or the top bar; **Down from the top bar** returns to the screen (a TV). Before, Left anywhere a screen didn't bind it went straight to the rail (Favorites' tabs: the user), and nothing led into the top bar at all (Search and the source switcher were out of a remote's reach). A text field on the desktop keeps its caret keys. **Something always has the focus** on a TV: when it is lost (a page or a dialog closed) the screen's remembered control, or its first, takes it — after the new page's own autofocus (a microtask queued during its build), which it must not beat.
- **The rail has no expand toggle** on a TV (960 logical px wide, under the 1280 at which it expands: the toggle did nothing). **Home's rows end in a See all card** (→ at a row's end), the header's link being out of a remote's reach.
- **The player** (docs/07's "OK/Info shows the OSD"): in Live TV, OK on the picture shows the controls with the focus on Channels (a typed number goes with OK); in a film, OK pauses and plays on, ↑/↓ show the controls on Play, ←/→ seek. While a control has the focus the arrows and OK are its own, and Back gives the picture the focus again and hides the OSD. The picture is skipped by the arrows (`skipTraversal`): as big as the screen, it was the next stop past the last control.
- **The sound:** the player plays at full volume, unmuted, always (`PlayerSoundController` ignores the stored level and changes nothing). A stray M during the ninth session's measurements had muted Bijou, kept across launches (ADR-017), with no control left on a TV to undo it; the remote's volume works the TV.
- **No downloads anywhere** (`downloadsHereProvider`, as `castsToTvsProvider` for Cast): a film's page drew Download, and the grids', episodes' and Search's menus offered it. Settings → Data has no Export/Import (a TV has no files) and says "this TV"; Playback has no Hardware decoding (the direct output is always MediaCodec's); Search's empty state doesn't promise "the videos on this computer".
- **A round icon:** an adaptive icon (the accent's gradient behind, the trillion in front, inside the safe zone), which Google TV masks round, `roundIcon`, and round PNGs for Android 7; `tools/brand/make_icons.py` writes them (the existing icons came out byte for byte the same). The 320 × 180 banner stays.

### Addendum, the same day: the user's second round on the TV
The user, after using it: "make sure with the new keyboard I can see what I am writing and see the suggestions … the highlight of [the keys] are being behind some buttons … the sizes of the components all over the app are not responsive and optimized to the screen size … the cursor of the new keyboard is not blinking", and Movies stuck after a filter.
- **The keyboard is a panel down the screen's left side** (460 wide), the screen beside it undimmed: what the words find shows as they are typed. **Search moves its results to the right of the panel** while it is open. The text sits in a box like the field's, its end in view when long, **after a caret that blinks** (on half a second, off half; steady with reduced motion). **A focused key fills white with dark letters** (Google TV's own keyboards' highlight) instead of the design system's ring, which neighbours 6 px away painted over (`FocusableSurface(ring: false)`, only there).
- **A TV is laid out on a 1280-wide canvas** (`TvCanvas`), scaled to fill the screen, with text 1.2× on top of the user's size. Android gives a 1080p TV 960 × 540, narrower than any window the layouts were drawn for, so every screen took its narrowest form with desktop-sized parts (Movies: four posters a row and one row in view; Home: one Continue watching card half the screen wide). At 1280 the layouts are the canvas's own: Movies six a row and two rows, Home two Continue cards, five channel tiles, seven posters. The rail stays collapsed there.
- **Movies stuck:** a `FocusPaneController` remembered a poster the filter then dropped; its node kept a context but had left the grid, so ↓ from the chips "focused" it and nothing moved. A remembered item now counts only while it is in the pane and on screen. **Up from a field at a screen's top** stayed there too (the field's EditableText answers `DirectionalFocusIntent` itself and keeps the key): the TV's field arrows are an intent of their own now, so the shell's edge rule hears them.

**Measured on the Bedroom TV** (the fake panel on the LAN): Home at rest 91 MB PSS, ~0.5 % of a core; Live TV playing full screen (1080p) 121 MB PSS, Bijou ~0.8–1.4 cores of 4 — Flutter's raster thread ~45 % of one, Android's main thread ~25 % (each video frame handed to Flutter as a texture), MediaCodec's loop ~17 %; SurfaceFlinger ~31 %, the codec service ~28 %. That is ADR-025's texture path; only a `SurfaceView` (Media3, or a platform view) would leave the compositing to the hardware. Not changed: it plays without drops, and the cost is the engine question ADR-025 left open.

**Not done, written down:** the first channel played right after Live TV opens sometimes shows a black preview (`First frame … (no, null)`: mpv started before the preview's surface existed; the picture came back full screen); Movies and Series land on their filter, not the grid; Back on Connect drops a typed form; overscan margins; holding OK on a real remote; whether Live TV now has sound (an mpv mute never shows in Android's audio log: the user's ears).

**Checked by:** `remote_keyboard_test` (typing by remote, ABC/symbols/Delete/Clear, the address row, Done and Back, a password, a field on a TV asks for no keyboard and the arrows leave it, the desktop unchanged); `player_screen_test` "on a TV" (OK shows the controls, the arrows don't zap, OK presses; → walks every control and never the picture; Back to the picture; a number with OK); `vod_player_screen_test` (OK pauses, ←/→ seek, ↓ to Play, the arrows move the focus, Back; no Download on a TV); `player_sound_test` (full volume on a TV whatever was stored); `tv_shell_test` (Up to the top bar and Down back; Left along Settings' buttons to the rail; no toggle; the focus kept; Data without Export); `home_screen_test` (See all card); `search_overlay_test` (the keyboard with Search, Done, ↓, ↑ back to it); `data_text_test`. On the Bedroom TV by remote keys over adb: the whole sign-in to the fake panel typed on Bijou's keyboard, the top bar, the source switcher, Live TV's controls, a film's OK/seek/controls/Back, Search, Back from the player to Live TV to Home.

## ADR-027 · 2026-10-15 · Accepted (the user: "fix the git error and fix the commits problems u made, and the icon of the app is not rounded as agreed on the bedroom tv homescreen, and the keyboard I need u to add arabic language, … when I opened a live channel for few mins and then went to continue watching the bad guys movie it was frozen and showing a screen for the live tv channel for 30 seconds, and showing reconnecting attempts, and then the movie continued from last place I stopped, investigate and fix this bug") — A resume plays from its place; Arabic on the keyboard and in search; the round banner
**What the Bedroom TV's log showed** (logcat, 00:02–00:03 the TV's time; its 256 KiB were all the log there was, the video decoder filling it in minutes): the channel stopped at 00:02:35.7; the film opened at 00:02:38.5, its decoder made a 1280 × 720 frame at once and the engine called that its **first frame (721 ms)** — but no sound followed, nothing moved, and **8 s later the stall check reconnected it** ("Reconnecting (attempt 1 of 6): PlaybackProblem(network, null)", 00:02:47). The next open's first frame came at once too; 2.4 s later mpv flushed the decoder and started the sound (the resume's place reached), then the picture's surface was made again for 1280 × 720 (it was the channel's 1920 × 1080: media_kit's Android controller sets `vo`/`vid` again and seeks) and nothing played: the second reconnect at 00:02:57. The third open, its surface already the film's size, played from the place at 00:03:01. All that time the screen showed **the channel's last picture**: the state said Playing, then Reconnecting, both of which show the picture, and the surface had nothing newer. The provider's connection was free (the channel closed 3 s before) and answered every open.

**Reproduced on this laptop** (`player_engine_vod_test`, the fake panel's new `seek_delay_ms`: a range past a file's first byte is answered that much later): with the resume's range held 4 s, the engine said "first frame" after 0.28 s (MP4) and 0.09 s (MKV).

**Decision:**
- **A resume's first frame is the one at its place:** the engine's first-frame test also waits, for a file opened part-way in, for mpv's `seeking` to be no (playback restarted where it was asked). Until then the coordinator's open time runs (a file's 30 s), not the 8 s stall check, and the picture stays hidden behind "Preparing…". Now: the first frame after 4.3 s (MP4) and 8.5 s (MKV: two ranges, its index then the place) on that panel; 0.8 s with no delay, as before.
- **A seek still waiting after 0.5 s is buffering** (`PlayerBuffering`, from mpv's `seeking` after the first frame; `paused-for-cache` as before): 15 s and the spinner over the picture, not a stall after 8. A seek into what is buffered lands within a few frames and shows nothing. On the slow panel: buffering after 0.5 s, playing 4.0 s after the seek.
- **Reconnecting shows the item's own last picture, or none** (`PlaybackReconnecting.hasPicture`): a film that never showed a picture shows black with the Reconnecting pill, never the channel before it.
- **The log says why:** "Reconnecting …" and "Playback failed …" carry the problem's detail ("The stream stalled", "No picture within the open timeout").
- **The TV's log file is where adb reads it:** on Android the app's log goes to its folder on shared storage (`/sdcard/Android/data/io.github.yasiralobaidi.bijou/files/logs`, other apps can't read it), besides logcat. This session had only logcat's last three minutes.
- **Left as it is:** media_kit's Android controller still makes the surface again when a stream's size differs from the last one's, setting `vo`/`vid` and seeking; it now counts as buffering. It is likely also the black first-channel preview (ADR-026's "Not done"); a patch of `android_video_controller/real.dart` would want the TV to prove it.

**Arabic, on the keyboard and in search.** The user's catalogue (the laptop's copy) has 4,022 Arabic film titles, 4,042 Arabic series and 336 Arabic channels, written several ways: "ارطغرل" and "أرطغرل", "شديد الخطوره" (ة as ه), "العميل ٧٧" (Arabic-Indic digits).
- **The keyboard has Arabic:** the bottom row's first key, **عربي** (in English) or **English** (in Arabic), each language's key in its own words. Arabic is laid out as a phone lays it out (ض ص ث ق ف غ ع ه خ ح ج · ش س ي ب ل ا ت ن م ك ط · ذ ء ؤ ر ى ة و ز ظ د), then a row of what a phone keeps under a long press, which a remote can't make: أ إ آ ئ, لا, Arabic's comma and question mark, `. - _`. No ABC in Arabic; `#+=` as in English and back with أبت. The words in the box read right to left, the caret at their left, a long text's end (its left) in view. **The language is kept across launches** (`UiPreferences.keyboardLanguage`, the provider `remoteKeyboardChoiceProvider`, given to the keyboard by `RemoteKeyboardLanguage` above the router). Why a phone's layout and not the alphabet's order: the user types Arabic on a phone, and the English page is QWERTY for the same reason.
- **Search reads Arabic's spellings as one** (`foldArabic`, `searchSpellings`, `searchTerm` in search_words.dart): each alef as any of ا أ إ آ (but the article's in "ال"), a word's closing ه also as ة and ي also as ى, digits as 0–9 and as ٠–٩; at most 32 spellings a word, OR'ed as prefix terms (the words are AND'ed explicitly: FTS5 takes no implicit AND beside parentheses). The bold in the results folds the same way, harakat and tatweel dropped. The index is unchanged: no migration.

**The icon on Google TV's home screen:** its app row shows the app's **banner**, not the launcher icon, so ADR-026's round adaptive icon never showed there. The banner (320 × 180) is now **the round icon and the name, white, on the dark background** (lifted a little at the top so the card stands off Google TV's dark home); every other icon came out the same.

**Git:** the user had pushed the session's first three commits (b3e5725, fe065f5, b10a883) while it ran; the session then reworded b3e5725 and rebased the rest, believing nothing was pushed, so the user's push was refused and `git pull` stopped in a conflicted merge. The merge was aborted and the local work rebased onto origin/main (the reworded copies dropped as already applied): a plain push works again. b3e5725's message, now on origin, says "Checked on the TV: … the new player has no clientVolume mute where the old one had": that was the Google TV launcher's mute, not Bijou's; the sound itself was never checkable by adb. Also found: bdab868 (2026-09-15) still ends with a Co-Authored-By line on origin; removing it would rewrite every commit since, so it waits for the user.

**Checked by:** `player_engine_vod_test` (the real player on the fake panel: a resume held back 4 s gives no first frame before its place, for an MP4 and an MKV; a seek held back is buffering, then plays), `playback_vod_test` "a resume after a channel" (a file gets 30 s; a reconnect before its picture has none; after it, the picture; a slow seek is buffering for 15 s), `player_surface_test` (the channel's picture never shows under the film), the fake provider's `seek_delay_ms` test; `search_words_test` (folding, spellings, the 32 cap, the term), `db_search_repository_test` (each spelling finds the others in the index), `search_text_test` (the bold); `remote_keyboard_test` (Arabic's layout and hamza row, لا, Delete by letter, symbols and back, English again; right to left with the caret at the left and a long text's end in view; the scope gets the choice and the next keyboard opens in it), `remote_keyboard_choice_test`, `db_ui_preferences_test`, `app_paths_test`.

## ADR-028 · 2026-10-15 · Accepted (the user: "make sure the icon of the app on the bedroom tv to be fixed and rounded since its not now") — Google TV's apps row shows the round icon once the version code changes
**What the Bedroom TV showed** (a screenshot, the user's yes for the TV): Google TV's home (launcherx 1.0.994451607) no longer has a row of 16:9 banners. **"Your apps" is a row of circles**, each the app's adaptive icon, and Bijou's circle held **the square tile from its first install** (2026-10-15 08:01 the TV's time, before b10a883's adaptive icon): the blue rounded square with light corners. ADR-027's reading ("the app row shows the banner") was wrong for this launcher. The APK installed at 00:40 already had the round icon and the round banner (read back with `aapt2 dump`), but **its version code was 1, as every build's had been** (pubspec's `0.1.0+1`), and the launcher keeps an app's icon until the version code changes: `adb install -r` changes `lastUpdateTime` only.

**Decision:**
- **The Android build's version code is the commit count** (`git rev-list --count HEAD`, `android/app/build.gradle.kts`): every update is a new version, so Google TV draws its icon again. A larger `--build-number` still wins; with no git history (CI's shallow checkout) it is pubspec's, 1. The version name stays pubspec's.
- **Installed on the Bedroom TV (version code 205): Bijou's circle in "Your apps" is the round icon at once**, the accent's gradient and the jewel, like the apps beside it. Nothing else changed: the adaptive icon is b10a883's, the banner ADR-027's (kept for launchers that show banners).
- **An older APK no longer installs over a newer one** (`INSTALL_FAILED_VERSION_DOWNGRADE`): `dist/bijou-0.1.0.apk` and CI's APK have version code 1. `adb install -r -d` puts back only a debug build; a release needs an uninstall first, which loses Bijou's data on the TV. So the TV only ever gets a build from a newer commit.

## ADR-029 · 2026-10-15 · Accepted (the user: "do a sweep and check for the design and the sizes of the buttons and the highlight blue lines when I use the keyboard or when I highlight a movie or a series or even the tabs … it's not fit and it doesn't look good … even on the live TV show selections the highlights and the blue lines are not fixed yet", then "the guide will be the least used one so make it the last one … is there a better icons to use") — Focus that fits, white on a TV; the Guide last in the rail
**What the Bedroom TV showed** (a sweep by remote over adb, the user's yes; the user's provider browsed, nothing played from it; Live TV and the player on the fake panel, added as a second source): every focus was the desktop's 2 px accent ring with its glow, drawn **outside** the control, and it fitted almost nothing:
- **Posters and cards** (Home's rows, Movies, Series): the ring went round the picture **and its caption**, the title flush against the ring's left edge; the canvas rings the picture alone.
- **Lists** (Live TV's categories and channels, the source switcher's menu): the list clipped the ring outside its rows, so a focused row showed **only its corners or one edge**.
- **Tabs** (Favorites'; a series' seasons the same): 4 px each side of the words, the ring touching the top of the letters with the underline's space below.
- **Edges of the screen**: a focused card was scrolled to the very edge of its box, so the screen cut its ring off (Home's lower rows).
- **Buttons of two heights side by side**: a button's height grows with the text (a TV's is 1.2×), an icon button's didn't: ☆ beside Play on a film's page, ⋯ beside Edit in Sources, ☆ beside Watch full screen in Live TV.
- **The canvas's `Google TV Home`** draws the TV's focus as a **white** 4 px ring (at 1920) round the card, the card grown to 1.06; Bijou's keyboard keys already fill white when focused.
- Also seen, not focus: "Drag to reorder" and drag handles on the TV (Favorites, Sources, Categories), which a remote can't use.

**Decision:**
- **The ring has a place** (`RingPlacement`, `AppFocusRing`): round a card's **picture**, not its caption (`PosterCard`, `LandscapeCard`); round a **tab's label** with 10 px each side, the chosen underline below and outside it (`CountTab`, now also a series' seasons); **inside the edge of a row that fills its list** (channel and category rows, menu items, Settings' sections and lists, the cast picker's devices, Pick categories' tiles, episodes, downloads, reminders); outside everything else.
- **Room for it:** Home's rows and the poster grids leave round their cards what the focused one needs (`AppFocusTokens.tileReach`: its growth and its ring), and **a focused control scrolls into view with its ring, its growth and `revealMargin` round it** (`FocusableSurface` shows that rect on screen after the traversal's own scroll).
- **An icon button grows with the text as a button does** (its icon doesn't): ☆ and ⋯ are as tall as the buttons beside them at every text size. At 100 % nothing moves.
- **A TV's focus is white** (`AppFocusTokens.tv`, given by the theme on a TV): a 3 px `textPrimary` ring (the canvas's 4 px at 1920, on the 1280 canvas), **2 px off the control** so a white poster never runs into it (Material's focus indicator does the same), no glow; tiles grow to the canvas's **1.06**; a focused control keeps **24 px** from the edge of what scrolls it. The accent stays the TV's *selection* (the chosen tab, chip, rail item), white its *focus*. Text fields and the Guide's cells draw the same ring. The desktop keeps the canvas's accent ring and glow.
- **No drag affordances on a TV:** "Drag to reorder" and the handles are gone there (Move up and down are in the menus).
- **The rail: Home, Live TV, Movies, Series, Favorites, (Library), Guide** — the Guide last, the least used (the user). Ctrl+1 … Ctrl+7 follow the order (Movies is Ctrl+3, the Guide Ctrl+7; G still opens the Guide).
- **Two icons redrawn** in the set's style (24 px grid, 1.8 px round strokes; `tools/extract_icons.dart` writes them over the canvas's): **Series**, a stack of screens with a play mark (was a stack of layers, which says nothing about episodes), and **the Guide**, a schedule grid of programme blocks (was a bare table). Home, Live TV (a set with its aerial), Movies (a film strip) and Favorites (a star) are the conventional signs and stay.

**Addendum, 2026-10-16 (the thirteenth session; the user: "continue the work", the Bedroom TV free, then, watching the walk: "the white highlights … on the movies filters didn't look fit exactly, there was gaps … check them all", "when u were moving between the cards of the movies it was like jumping", "the details page … the highlight white border on the play button is not fit", and Search: "the search still has the blue border", "I can't control if I wanna select history search or type a new one", "on the keyboard I can't go to the right … to the search results"):**
- **The ring is the control's edge on a TV.** 2 px off the control (the first form above) it looked loose; just outside, the control's edge and the ring's met at a half pixel on the TV's 1.5× screen (the 1280 canvas on 1920 pixels) and left a dark seam. On a TV every ring is drawn **inside** the control's edge, over its border (`AppFocusTokens.inside`; `reach` is then 0, a tile's room is its growth). Nothing can clip an inside ring either: the old build's ring round Play on a series' page was cut by the page's column. The desktop keeps its accent ring and glow outside. *Superseded:* "2 px off the control … Material's focus indicator does the same".
- **One ring on a field:** a TV's focused text field drew its blue border inside the white ring; its border stays at rest now (red for an error).
- **Moves glide, once.** Flutter's focus callback jumped the view to a card's edge and the card's reveal then glided on for its ring: a jump and a glide per move. Every pane and group moves with `revealingFocus` (`appTraversalPolicy()`; a root group in the app's builder): a `FocusableSurface` scrolls itself into view once, 200 ms ease-out (`motion.base`); anything else is scrolled to as Flutter does, animated.
- **Where the focus lands on a TV:** Right from the rail into Movies or Series lands on the first poster, not the filter (`FocusLanding`); Left into the rail lands on the screen's own item (the rail's selected item is its landing), not on the item last focused there (the TV: Settings, from Home's posters); the keyboard's Left still returns where it left the rail. A focused card brings its row's heading into view (`RevealArea`): back on Home's first row, its title was half under the top bar.
- **Menus of choices open on the chosen item** (the source switcher opened on the other source, where an OK at once switched).
- **Fitting things found in the goldens:** a grid's room for the ring took a column at 1920 (the columns are counted as before; the room narrows the cards); a count or button beside a row of tabs sat 4 px below the tabs' labels (`CountTab.underlineRoom`); the Series icon's two lines blurred at 20 px and the Guide's read as bricks (redrawn: one line over a screen with a play mark; a channel column and programme blocks); a poster's stand-in lettering broke a long word in two at a TV's type (drawn smaller when its widest word won't fit).
- **Search by remote** (built by an agent, checked by the session): on a TV, Search draws Bijou's keyboard panel itself, beside its results (it was a dialog over them, so the focus could not pass and a click on a recent search hit the dialog's barrier). Right at the keys' edge goes to the results on the cursor's row; Up/Down walk them; OK opens a result or **runs a recent search** (its words in the query and the keyboard's box; the focus stays on the results, on the first one; back to the keys when it finds nothing now); held, the row's menu; Left, Esc or Back return to the key left from. Back on the keys closes the keyboard and keeps the words; with it closed, Back closes Search. OK on the field opens the cursor's row; Left on it reopens the keyboard. One focus on screen: no row is ringed while the keys have it; the typed box has a field's resting border, not the accent. The desktop is unchanged (its keyboard stays the dialog where any field uses it). *Supersedes* ADR-026's "Search opens with the keyboard up; Done leaves the results to ↓/↑" for a TV.
- **Also:** Back on Connect keeps what was typed as the pending draft (the remote's Back popped the form); a failing golden fails instead of hanging the run (`pumpApp` unmounts the app before the fixtures' databases close).
- **Seen on the Bedroom TV (build 213):** Home's rows (the lower row's ring and caption in view), Movies' grid and Up through chips → filter → top bar in one press each (the "stuck on the filter" report is gone), a film's page (☆ as tall as Play), Favorites' tabs (focus white, selection blue, no drag hint), Settings → Sources (⋯ as tall as Edit), the switcher's menu (rows ringed inside), Live TV's categories and channels (rings inside, whole), the live player's controls (all nine reached; the walk restarts only when the OSD has hidden), Search's Arabic keyboard (Android's Arabic font, right to left; the language kept). The ring's gap and the jump were what the user saw on that build. **On build 217** (everything above, the user's provider browsed, nothing played): the chips', the filter field's (one ring), the sort's, a poster's and Play's rings are the controls' edges, no gap or seam; a screen recording of three Downs in Movies' grid shows each move as one glide over 5–6 frames (e.g. 38, 81, 33, 9, 4 px a frame at 30 fps), no jump; Search: no blue border, Right from p to the recent searches (white ring), OK on one ran it (11 results, the focus on the first), Left back to p, Back and Back closed it. At launch the focus was on the rail's Home (earlier builds: Home's first card); Up from Movies' sort reached the switcher, not the search beside it (the top bar's remembered item).

## ADR-030 · 2026-10-16 · Accepted (the user: "I cannot seek forward or backword only by the 10 secodns buttons, I need to controll that by the remote too. to move, by putting the focus on it and then seek it forward or backword") — A TV's seek bar is a stop; a held arrow seeks faster
**What the user had:** on a TV a film's seek bar was not a stop (the desktop's rule: ←/→ seek from anywhere, the bar is the mouse's).
