With the remote on the controls, the arrows move between them, so the remote reached a place only through −10 s and +10 s, one press each. ←/→ on the picture did seek, 10 s a press, but nothing on the screen said so, and a hold went 10 s a repeat (about 3 minutes a second).

**Decision:**
- **On a TV the seek bar is a stop, above Play** (↑ from any control of the row; ↓ goes back to the control it came from; Back gives the picture the focus, as from any control). The OSD still opens on Play (ADR-026), as Google TV's own players do.
- **With the remote on it the bar grows**, track 6 → 10 px and thumb 18 → 28 px (`AppPlayerTokens.seekTrackHeightFocused`, `seekThumbRadiusFocused`), over the TV's quick focus motion, **and shows its time** in the bubble. A ring round a 6 px bar would not read from across a room; the white thumb is the TV's focus colour already.
- **←/→ on the bar seek as on the picture:** the bar moves at once and one seek runs once the key rests 300 ms. The player hears them (the bar's own `Focus` has no keys), so the slider's own keys, a tenth of the length a press, never apply.
- **Held, the steps grow** (`VodPlayerController.scrubStep`, on the bar and on the picture): 10 s a press and for the first 20 repeats, 30 s to the 60th, then a minute. Android repeats a held key 20 times a second, so that is 10 s steps for about a second, 30 s for two more, then 20 minutes a second: the middle of a two-hour film is about 5 seconds' hold away, and a press is still 10 s. The desktop keeps its keys (10 s, Shift 60 s; the mouse drags).
- **OK after seeking goes there now and plays** (`VodPlayerController.seekNow`): a paused film plays on from the new place. With no seek waiting, OK pauses or plays on, as before (on the bar and on the picture).
- **Play's ring shows on a TV:** the white ring drawn inside the white Play/Pause vanished, so after Down from the bar nothing showed where the remote was. A surface filled with the text colour takes the accent ring (`onLight` on `FocusableSurface` and `AppFocusRing`), as an accent surface takes the white; the desktop's ring was the accent already.
- **Also, the two things seen on build 217** (an agent, from a brief; its diff and tests checked before the commit): **Up into the top bar lands on the bar's item above the control** (`FocusPaneController.focusAbove`: of the items across its width the nearest, else the one whose edge is nearest its middle), not the bar's remembered item: Up from Movies' sort reached the source switcher, left of the search right above it. Down still returns to the control. **At launch, until the user's first press, the screen's first control takes the focus as soon as it has one:** the launch's check gave up after 2 s and stopped at anything focused, the rail's Home included (held there while Home showed skeletons, or after "Add a source" went as the sources were read), and the provider's Home rows came later. A press ends it, so the focus never moves from where the user put it; a screen's own autofocus (a title's Play) lands first.
- **Up from any of the row's controls is the bar** (the user, after build 222: "when I'm on the pause and play button when I click the upper arrow … it will take me to … the progress bar not to the return button"). Up picked what lay straight above: past an hour the time beside the bar ("1:05:00") pushes the bar's start right of Play, and the top's Back was the only control above it (the test film, 10 minutes, had "7:24" and the bar reached over Play). The row is one focus node (`VodOsdBottom.rowFocusNode`); Up from any control in it goes to the bar, and Down from the bar back to that control (Play if it has gone).
- **A card's watch progress shows again on a TV** (the user: Continue watching "used to have a bar … now it's not there"). The bar ran along the picture's bottom edge and the TV's ring, drawn inside the edge since ADR-029's addendum, covered it; the first card has the focus at launch, so it was never seen (the TV focus golden had it hidden too). On a TV the bar floats 8 px inside the picture (`AppFocusTokens.cardBarInset`), 4 px thick, an even 5 px clear of the ring on three sides, its track the text colour at 30 % over a soft shadow (`AppElevation.overArtwork`) so it reads on a dark poster and a bright one; the desktop keeps the canvas's edge bar. Seen on the Bedroom TV (builds 225, 226): focused and at rest, on a light and a dark picture.
- **On the Bedroom TV** (the user: "the bedroom tv is free to use"; a film from the fake panel, nothing played from the provider; keys by adb, which cannot hold one): build 219, Up from Pause focused the bar, grown with its time; →×3 moved it 30 s and ← 10 s, each one seek; Down left it at rest, and Pause showed no focus (fixed above). Build 220: Pause's blue edge; OK on the bar paused (Play in the middle, the bar still focused); Left and OK in one burst went 10 s back and kept playing. Build 222: Up from Movies' sort (Recently added) went to the search, Down back to Recently added; launched on xvip.pro, the focus was on Home's first card at 3 s and 12 s (build 219: the rail's Home). Not seen: a held arrow (adb sends one repeat at most); the user's remote.

## ADR-031 · 2026-10-17 · Accepted in part (the user, on the fourteenth session's recommendations: "do evrything that wont effect the experiance, and keep it smooth, and the guid its not simething the users will really care, so diableing it for now that will save us space memory and everything am I right? or if its easy to add a toggle and enable or disable the guid, and make the defualt disabled and hide it from the list do what u think better", then "if there is anything that doesnt need a tv then do it now", then "stop on a clear point with proper hand off") — A lighter TV; the guide off by default; history upkeep
**What the TV showed (progress.md, Measurements, 2026-10-16):** ~200 MB free with ~400 MB swapped; a film playing costs about half a core, most of it Flutter redrawing the whole screen for every video frame (the picture is a texture); Bijou keeps 77 MB of data and 23 MB of cache.

**Done (on `main`, desktop-tested; not yet on the TV):**
- **A TV's player buffers** (`mpvOptionsFor(lowMemory:)`, set on Android): at most 32 MiB read ahead whatever the preset, 16 MiB kept behind, a film's too (was 64 + 64 MiB for a film, up to 128 + 64 on Stable). The preset's seconds still apply below the cap (8 s on Balanced is under 32 MiB up to ~33 Mb/s); a seek further back than what is kept asks the server again. docs/03.
- **A TV's picture folder is 150 MB** (`artworkDiskBytesOnTv`; the desktop keeps 500): the launch's sweep trims it in its isolate. docs/07.
- **A film's hidden OSD is not rebuilt as it plays** (its time moved 4 times a second): it stays built while hidden (a TV focuses its controls before they fade in) but listens to nothing once faded out; a show catches it up at once.
- **CI's one failure on 8472d88:** `guide_settings_keyboard_test` pressed ↓↓ in Keep's menu, which opens on the chosen 7 days since ADR-029's addendum; ↑↑ is 3 days.

**Decided, built on WIP branches, not yet reviewed (the session stopped at the user's word):**
- **The guide is off by default, on the desktop and the TV, with a switch** (Settings → Guide's first row; branch `wip/guide-switch`). The user: users don't care much about it, and it costs space and memory. Off: the Guide leaves the rail/top bar, Ctrl+7, the shortcuts list and the channel menu's matching; /guide goes Home; no XMLTV import at all; the imported guide is cleared (at the switch and at launch), keeping the user's matches, offsets and reminders. Now/next on channels, the preview and the player still come from the provider's short EPG (a request for the channel looked at, cached 5 min). On: as before, plus on a TV 2 hours behind and 3 days ahead (docs/07's promise) and the scheduled import held while anything plays.
- **History upkeep** (branch `wip/history-upkeep`): at launch, after the guide's scheduling, channels not watched for 90 days, finished films and episodes after 120 days (each series' newest kept, for "next episode"), anything in progress after 12 months; `sync_runs` and `epg_imports` to the newest 30 a source (rows still referenced kept); then `VACUUM` (and a WAL checkpoint) when the file has a lot of free space, only with nothing syncing, importing, downloading or playing — the step that gives back the space the guide frees. Settings → Data: "Watch history → Clear…" (channels, Continue watching and watched marks, or everything; favorites, sources and settings stay) and "Database" with its size and Compact. Never touched: favorites, groups, sources, settings, hidden/renamed items, guide matches, reminders, downloads, the library.

**Waiting for the TV (docs/plans/tv-video-layer-and-frame-rate.md, researched read-only):** the full-screen picture on its own SurfaceView under a transparent Flutter layer (the CPU win, the hardware scaler, HDR10/HLG), trialled first with no mpv (T0), then films only (T1); and an optional "Match frame rate" for films (`preferredDisplayModeId`, off by default). Found on the way: mpv's direct output (`vo=mediacodec_embed`) ignores scaling, so Picture size very likely does nothing on the TV today — check it there.

**Addendum, 2026-10-17 (the sixteenth session): history upkeep finished and on `main`** (396b88b; desktop-tested, not yet on the TV). Decided while finishing it:
- **A compaction needs half the file free while a guide is in place** (a quarter without one): each import stages the new guide beside the old and frees about a guide's worth of pages, which the next import takes again; at a quarter, nearly every launch would have rewritten the file for nothing.
- **A history choice with nothing in it** says "Nothing to clear", is dimmed and can't be chosen; the Database row is measured again after Clear guide and Clear history.
- **VACUUM's copy stays in memory** (`package:sqlite3` is built with `SQLITE_TEMP_STORE=2`): a temp file needs an Android temp directory not testable here, and compactions are rare. On the desktop (the fake panel's large catalogue): 541 → 357 MB in 1.4–2.3 s with a guide (+231 MB RSS at its peak), 560 → 241 MB in 0.8–1.4 s with it cleared (+27 MB); the UI isolate's worst gap 16–20 ms. The TV's numbers are still to measure.
- **Only playing, syncing, a guide import and a download block a compaction**; a library scan, guide matching and the channel-name fill wait behind it. On a TV there is no download queue.

**Addendum, 2026-10-17 (the seventeenth session): the guide switch finished and on `main`** (the `wip/guide-switch` branch reviewed whole, rebased, merged). As built, beyond the decision above:
- **Installs from before the switch read as Off:** their stored choice has no `enabled`, and only `true` turns the guide on (the user's "make the default disabled"). The launch then clears their guide; the compaction after it gives the space back.
- **Read before the first frame** (`launchGuideSettingsProvider`, set by `bootstrap()`): the rail never gains or loses its Guide a moment after launch.
- **Off, every way in is closed or redirected:** G types a letter, Ctrl+7 does nothing, a remote's Guide key opens Live TV; the shortcut lists drop the Guide's group, its keys and the programme-only rows; an upcoming programme in Search and a reminder's Enter open the channel in Live TV; the empty reminders list says to switch the guide on; the preview drops "No guide information" and its Match button. "The channel menu's matching" above was that button: the channel menu itself has no guide item.
- **A TV, switched on:** 2 hours behind and Keep's 3 days ahead; a scheduled import holds while anything plays (paused too) and goes on once two looks 30 s apart find nothing playing, so the moment between two channels doesn't count as an end. Refresh guide never waits.
- **Goldens:** 39 differ only in the rail's Guide icon (gone); `settings_guide` gains the switch row, `settings_guide_off` is new, the two shortcut lists lose the Guide's keys. Integration walks that use the Guide switch it on as a user who chose it would have (`integration_test/support/guide_on.dart`).

## ADR-032 · 2026-10-17 · Accepted (found on the Bedroom TV, build 231; the user: "I am home and the bedroom tv is free to use, continue the work", a 30-minute window) — A TV's player: a clean process after Back, no idle output; what the TV showed
**Two faults, both seen on the TV and fixed:**
- **A crash at the first play after leaving with Back.** Back on Home finishes the activity (Flutter's `SystemNavigator.pop`) and its engine, but Android keeps the process; the next launch starts a second engine in it while libmpv keeps the first engine's player, whose wakeup callback (media_kit's) points into the freed engine. The second engine's first play crashed in mpv's `wakeup_client` (`blx` to the dead callback; SIGSEGV on the main thread, `libmpv.so+0x2a4c64`, `mpv_request_log_messages+0x5a8` the nearest export). Reproduced twice: a cold start, Back, launch, OK on a film. **Fix:** `MainActivity.onDestroy` ends the process when the activity is finishing (not a configuration change), so a launch always starts clean. Cost: a cold start (Home in ~1.1 s). Not reproducible on the desktop (the process ends with the window). Kotlin: no desktop test; checked on the TV (the process gone after Back, a new one at launch, the film played, no crash).
- **A core spun after every film.** media_kit sets `force-window=yes`, which keeps mpv's video output while nothing plays; Android's direct output (`vo=mediacodec_embed`) then ran its thread at ~100 % of a core on Bijou's Home after a film closed (about 1 % while the film played), until Bijou went to the background (`wid` 0 → `vo=null`). Each Picture size change made a new output thread (ADR-027's surface remade), with the same end. **Fix:** `force-window=no` with the direct output (`mpvOptionsFor(direct:)`, set at every open): mpv drops the output when the video ends and makes it again for the next one (the `vo` option and `wid` are kept). On the TV: after a close no output thread is left and mpv's core is idle; a second play in the same process has its picture (first frame 0.97 s).

**Seen on the TV (fake panel's 1080p25 H.264 film, OSD hidden):** Bijou ~47 % of one core while playing (raster 17 %, mpv 11 %, main 5.8 % — ~10 % before the hidden OSD stopped rebuilding — MediaCodec 5.8 %); PSS 133 MB (151 MB before the lighter buffers); a 10 s seek back resumed within a second from memory (the I overlay: ~2.1 Mb/s, cache 8.0 s ahead, 16.4 s ahead and behind after the seek). **Picture size:** 16:9 and 4:3 work on the TV (`video-aspect-override` changes the size media_kit's `Video` fits; 4:3 pillarboxed the 16:9 film), so ADR-031's "very likely does nothing" was wrong for those two; Fill (crop) and Stretch use mpv's own scaler (`panscan`, `keepaspect`), which the direct output skips, and a 16:9 film can't show them: unchecked, likely nothing on another shape. **The display:** the user set Google TV to 4K (3840×2160 at 59.94 Hz, HDR10/HLG/Dolby Vision offered); apps still draw at 1920×1080 (`ro.surface_flinger.max_graphics_*`), so Flutter's cost is unchanged and only a video layer of its own can show 4K or HDR. Mode switching is `SWITCHING_TYPE_WITHIN_GROUPS` with each mode its own group: the system never changes the rate on its own, so Match frame rate needs `preferredDisplayModeId` (as planned); 3840×2160 23.976 and 24 exist. **No soundbar or receiver** (the user): Media3's passthrough isn't worth it now (ADR-025 stays on mpv).

**Also seen, not fixed yet:** the launch right after the install showed Home 19.9 s after `main()` while the xvip.pro sync (12,600 channels, 20,137 films) ran; the next launches were quick. Watch for it on later launches with a sync due.

**Addendum, 2026-10-17 (the seventeenth session, the Bedroom TV for 1–2 hours, the fake panel only):**
- **Picture size's Fill and Stretch now work on the TV:** on the fake panel's 4:3 channel (576i) both did nothing (the bars stayed); the direct output draws whole frames and skips mpv's scaler, so on it Fill and Stretch are the picture widget's fit (`directOutputFit`: cover, fill); the ratios stay mpv's `video-aspect-override`. Checked on build 236: Fill fills the width and crops top and bottom, Stretch fills the screen.
- **Search's recent searches: Clear by remote** (the user: "the clear button on the search i couldn't reach it with the remote … the focus never goes there"): ↑ from the first recent search (or from the field, the keyboard closed) is Clear, ringed alone; ↓ the list, ↑ the keyboard, ← and Back the keys; after Clear, the keys. Checked on build 239 (the ring on Clear, not pressed: the user's searches kept).
- **The guide off on the TV (build 238):** the launch cleared both stored guides (27 and 39,168 programmes, ~9 s) and compacted the database in 6.2 s, **74.2 → 38.4 MB**; PSS 125 MB at the compaction's peak, 112 MB on Home after. A later launch: Home 862 ms after `main()`.
- **T0, the picture's own layer, tried** (branch `trial/video-layer-t0`): transparency works on Vulkan and Flutter idles at 0 frames a second, but the composer put the test pattern and the window on the GPU (SurfaceFlinger 34 %). A conditional go: T1 with real MediaCodec output decides (docs/plans/tv-video-layer-and-frame-rate.md).

## ADR-033 · 2026-10-17 · Accepted, built (see its addendum) (the user: "add a button for the next episode while watching one, so the user can skip the wait time and go to the next [episode] or just skip it if he wants to, and make sure on the movies it wont show up") — Next episode during the credits
**What exists (Phase 5's decision 4):** an episode with a next one has a Next episode button in the OSD's row, and at 20 s left a card inside the OSD (pinning it up) that counts from 10 ("Play now · N", Cancel), then plays the next episode; after a Cancel the end card offers Play next episode / Back to series. A film has neither.

**Decided (best experience; the user may override):**
1. **An offer in the credits:** from **60 s before the end**, for an episode longer than 4 minutes that has a next one, the card shows with no count: **"Next episode"** (focused; plays it at once — the user's "skip the wait") and **"Hide"**. From 20 s left the count runs as today unless hidden.
2. **Hide is "skip it":** the button, Esc or a remote's Back dismisses the card for this episode; nothing then plays by itself and the end card offers Play next episode / Back to series. Seeking back out of the last minute takes the card away; reaching it again brings it back unless hidden.
3. **The card stands on its own:** offering or counting, it no longer pins the OSD; it floats bottom right where it is today, the OSD showing and hiding as usual (no jump when it does). The end card may still pin it.
4. **Focus:** the card's first button takes the focus when it appears; on a TV OK plays, Back hides, ←/→ between the two; after Hide the picture has the focus again.
5. **Never on a film**, nor the row's button: pinned by tests on the desktop and the TV.

**State:** an agent was briefed and started in its own worktree; the session was stopped (the user's word) before it changed anything. Build it from this ADR.

**Addendum, 2026-10-17 (the seventeenth session): built, and changed by what the user saw on the Bedroom TV.**
- **The count shows from the start** (the user, watching build 242's offer with no number: "But it should show the count down if u won't click anything then u will go to the next"): an episode over 4 minutes shows **"Next episode · N"** and **Hide** from 60 s left, N the seconds until the next episode starts by itself, counted from the episode's own time (a pause holds it, a seek moves it), its bar draining; at 0 (10 s left, when the old count ended) the next episode plays. One count, no second phase. A shorter episode keeps the count from 20 s, labelled the same ("Cancel" is "Hide" everywhere). This replaces point 1's offer without a count.
- **Focus, desktop and TV apart** (a reasoned change to point 4): on the desktop the card never takes the keyboard by itself (←/→ seek and Space pauses with the card up, so a Space meant to pause can't start the next episode; Tab reaches the card). On a TV it takes the focus when it appears unless the remote is on one of the OSD's controls (scrubbing through the credits keeps seeking), then when the OSD hides; the end card takes it on both.
- **Found on the TV and fixed with it:** the OSD hid behind its own open menu (Picture size, Speed, Audio, Subtitles): keys in a menu never reach the player, so its timer ran out; after a choice the OSD stayed hidden with the focus on its invisible button and the next Back did nothing visible. The OSD now stays while a menu is open, its timer starting again after.
- **Found by the walks and fixed with it:** the series page under the player took the focus with its Play button's autofocus when the episode just watched changed what it would play, and the player's keys went to it (Esc left the player). While the player is the page on top, a focus taken outside it comes back. The page's background autofocus is still worth fixing at its source.
- **Checked on the Bedroom TV:** build 242, the card at 60 s left over a fake-panel episode, "Next episode" ringed, the card staying when the OSD faded, → to Hide; **build 244 (the count)**, "Next episode · 34" at 43 s left, "· 19" fifteen seconds later with the OSD gone, and the next episode starting by itself with no key pressed.
- **A bug the count's tests found before the TV did:** asking for the next episode from inside the coordinator's timeline event re-entered its event stream ("Cannot fire new event"); the count's end now asks just after the event, once.

## ADR-034 · 2026-10-17 · Accepted (the user: "since we have many versions I need u to keep a stable version on a branch and another branch for dev once we test and make sure everything is stable and good, then u merge it to the main branch and when u test on the tv install a beta version and keep a stable version") — Stable and dev branches; Bijou and Bijou Beta on the TV
**Branches:** `main` is the stable branch: only work that has been tested goes into it (the full suite under `TZ=UTC`, the touched walks, and on the TV whatever touches the TV). Work happens on **`dev`** (made from `main` at c1134ac, the seventeenth session's tested end), or on a branch from `dev` that merges back into it. When `dev` is tested and good, the main session merges it into `main` (a fast-forward when it can) and tags it `stable-<build>`; a trial that may never land (`trial/video-layer-t0`) stays on its own branch. The user pushes both branches and the tags; nothing is rewritten once pushed.

**Two apps on the TV:** `tools/tv_apk.sh beta` builds **"Bijou Beta"** (`io.github.yasiralobaidi.bijou.beta`, version "0.1.0-beta", from `dev`) and `tools/tv_apk.sh stable` **"Bijou"** (`io.github.yasiralobaidi.bijou`, from `main`), each to `build/tv/bijou-<channel>-<build>.apk`. They install side by side: each has its own data, sources and sign-in, so the beta needs a source of its own once (the fake panel, or the user's provider signed in again by the user). Tests on the TV use the beta; the stable app is updated only after `dev` is merged into `main`. `--dart-define=BIJOU_CHANNEL=beta` sets the id suffix, the label (a manifest placeholder) and the version suffix in `android/app/build.gradle.kts`; nothing in Dart changes. Checked with `aapt dump badging` on both builds (245).


## ADR-035 · 2026-10-18 · Accepted, on `main` since ADR-037 (the user, after setting the Chromecast and the Roku TV as asked: "if there are points to work on to start enhancing the picture and get the player to show a better quality then lets work on it, I am not home now so no tv … do it now without them and after few hours we can start testing", then "stop at a clear point with proper hand off") — The picture: its own layer on the TV (T1), Match frame rate, the desktop's High quality, the picture tests
The three items of docs/plans/tv-video-layer-and-frame-rate.md's answer to "is the picture the best it can be?", in the user's order, and the films to test them with. Nothing here is on `main`; only the picture tests are on `dev`.

**1. The picture's own layer, T1 (built on `trial/video-layer-t1`; the TV's go and the default since the addendum below).** As the plan's steps 2–7, behind `--dart-define=BIJOU_VIDEO_LAYER=true|clear` (`tools/tv_apk.sh beta layer|layer-clear`), two choices of its own:
- **The SurfaceView is the app's** (`VideoLayer.kt` in MainActivity, channel `bijou/video_layer`), not the plugin's: media_kit_video's second patch is Dart only (`AndroidVideoController.setLayer(wid)`: mpv draws into the app's surface and leaves the texture alone; null goes back to it, rebuilding the texture's surface at the next picture's size). Less vendored Java, and the app owns the lifecycle (Home's `surfaceDestroyed` moves mpv off it).
- **The layer is hidden whenever no film plays on it** (GONE; with `SURFACE_LIFECYCLE_FOLLOWS_ATTACHMENT` its surface, and so mpv's `wid`, survive), so Home and the menus are T0's transparent-Flutter case with no second full-screen layer.
- A film (`!live`) on the direct output goes on the layer, within a second of asking for the surface or on the texture; channels stay on the texture (T2 is their own). The picture is placed where its widget is, as Picture size says (`videoLayerRect`: Fit and the ratios letterbox, Fill covers past the screen, Stretch fills): the composer scales, mpv's direct output has no scaler (ADR-025). The player paints nothing over a playing picture on the layer (`PlayerEngine.pictureBehindApp`), and the video colour before its first frame, so no old frame shows. Stream info's **Colour** row names HDR10/HLG/SDR from mpv's `video-params/gamma` and `primaries`.
- **Built:** `bijou-beta-layer-250.apk` (1c3815a) compiles; the TV decides (the plan's T1 go / no-go: the layer DEVICE, not CLIENT).

**2. Match frame rate (TV only; WIP on `wip/match-frame-rate`, made from cc510be: rebase onto `dev`).**
- **On by default, and it follows the TV's own Match content frame rate** (`DisplayManager.getMatchContentFrameRateUserPreference()`, API 31): Always → switch; Seamless only → only a seamless switch (never on this TV: each mode its own group, ADR-032); Never → never. The user set the TV to Always; an app switch that ignored it would overrule them, and one off by default would make them say it twice. Settings → Playback (TV) has the switch to turn it off for Bijou alone; its line says the screen goes dark for a second or two when a film starts and ends.
- Only a file of 15 minutes or more at 23.9–24.1 fps (23.976 and 24 are different modes), to a mode at the current resolution within 0.01 Hz; nothing for 25/50 (no such mode here) or 29.97/30/59.94/60 (already even). **At the first frame, as part of the open:** "Preparing…" stays, the file is paused, the mode asked for (`preferredDisplayModeId`), the display's change awaited (5 s) plus ~1.5 s to settle, then it plays; neither the open's timeout nor the stall check runs meanwhile, and the switch's pause is not the user's. A stop or another play cancels it. **Back to the TV's own mode** 2 s after the player closes or a channel plays (a next episode at the same rate keeps it), and at once when the app leaves the screen.
- **Done in the WIP:** `frame_rate_match.dart` (`pickDisplayMode`, `FrameRateMatcher`), `lib/core/platform/display_modes.dart`, `PlaybackSettings.matchFrameRate` (JSON `matchFps`), the coordinator's switch, 32 tests (pass, one guard restored after the stop). **Not yet:** the Kotlin channel (`DisplayModes.kt`: modes, request, the preference, a DisplayListener), its Dart implementation and bootstrap override, the TV-only Settings row, stream info's display line, the logs' wording.

**3. The desktop's High quality picture (WIP on `wip/desktop-picture-quality`, from cc510be; written, untested).**
- **Found:** media_kit draws mpv into a texture at the video's own size and Flutter scales it (bilinear), so mpv's scalers do nothing today. **High** makes mpv draw at the picture's on-screen size (`VideoController.setSize`, from `applyBoxFit` of the widget's box in physical pixels; debounced; back to the video's size for Standard) with mpv 0.34's `gpu-hq` set option by option (`scale=spline36`, `cscale=spline36`, `dscale=mitchell`, `dither-depth=auto`, `correct-downscaling`, `linear-downscaling`, `sigmoid-upscaling`, `deband`), each restored to the running mpv's own value for Standard; never on Android. Settings → Playback (desktop) **Picture quality: Standard (default) / High** (JSON `quality`).
- **To check:** whether Picture size → Fill and Stretch do anything on the desktop today (likely not, for the same reason the TV's didn't before 9e950dc); the CPU and dropped frames with High (`IPTV_PICTURE_QUALITY=high` on `playback_cpu_test`, the real display) against docs/06's 15 %.

**4. The picture tests (on `dev`, b1d649a).** `tools/media_samples/generate.sh --picture` makes four 16-minute films (1080p 23.976 and 24 fps; 4K HDR10 and 1080p HLG at 23.976, HEVC Main10, BT.2020), a white bar crossing each at a steady speed so 3:2 judder shows; the fake provider's `--picture-tests` serves them as "Movies | Picture tests" after every generated movie (ids from 190000), leaving the catalogue unchanged without the flag. `tools/tv/picture_probe.sh` reads what the TV does with the picture (read-only; its dumpsys parsing unchecked until the TV).


**Addendum (2026-10-18, the nineteenth session: all three built, the TV's go, on `dev`).** The user: "the bedroom is available for u now to work on it and test, and I wont be there to help about the tv". Bijou Beta (builds 258, 262) on the Bedroom TV with the fake panel's picture tests only; the stable Bijou untouched.
- **§1 T1: go, and the default on `dev`** (8c973f6, merged): every TV build plays a film on the layer; `BIJOU_VIDEO_LAYER=off` (`tv_apk.sh beta texture`) builds the texture. Measured (`tools/tv/picture_probe.sh`, % of one core of four, the same 1080p 23.976 film at 23.976 Hz, controls hidden):

  | | texture | own layer |
  |---|---|---|
  | Bijou | 50.9 | 33.2 |
  | SurfaceFlinger | 15.2 | 10.5 |
  | the decoder (c2) | 21.9 | 20.0 |
  | the composer | 4.3 | 3.4 |

  The layer is DEVICE (the composer's own plane); Flutter's surface draws 0 frames a second with the controls hidden (DEVICE at 23.976 Hz; CLIENT at 59.94 for the HLG film before Match frame rate worked, so `layer-clear` wasn't needed). **The 4K HDR10 film goes out at its own 3840×2160** (the layer's crop 3840×2160; the texture is 1920×1080), BT2020_PQ with its static metadata, the HDMI output HDR10; the HLG film as HLG. Picture size places the layer (4:3: 480–3360 of 3840, still DEVICE); Home and back resumes the film on the layer at its rate; a channel after a film is on the texture (no layer, 50 fps from Flutter). **Not yet seen on the layer:** subtitles (`SubtitleView` over it), the next-episode card, a film's end.
- **§2 Match frame rate: works.** 24 fps → 24 Hz 0.6 s after asking (1.8 s after OK), 23.976 → 23.976 Hz in 0.5 s, the 4K HDR10 film at 23.976 too; back to 59.94 0.8 s after the player closed, and at once while away (Home); stream info's Display row "3840×2160 · 24 Hz · matched". **Found and fixed (4dce4f2):** the first film played at 59.94, "the film's length isn't known": the player's length came just after the first frame and a provider's film list has none; the first frame now waits up to a second for it. A/V sync after the switch is the user's ears.
- **§3 High quality: measured** (`playback_cpu_test`, profile, the real display, 1080p50 H.264 in a 1280×720 window): Standard 2.36 % of all cores, High 2.38 %, no dropped frames either way, High drawn at 1280×720. Fill and Stretch on the desktop did nothing before; they work now (the picture widget's fit).
- **The Chromecast's settings, checked at the user's word ("Check the settings if they are wrong, if u can change it to the correct options then do it"):** resolution Automatic (3840×2160 59.94), Match content frame rate Always (`match_content_frame_rate=2`): right. **Match content dynamic range was off** (`hdr_conversion_mode=2`, System preferred: the chip's "follow sink", Google TV's own home and every SDR channel sent as Dolby Vision): turned on in Settings → Display & Sound by remote keys (now 1, "follow source": SDR at home, HDR10 and HLG as themselves). The chip sends HDR10 at 4K 24 Hz as 4:4:4 8-bit; Google TV 14 has no colour-format setting, so nothing to change. Advanced: Allow game mode on (only for a game that asks), Dolby Vision Mode Standard.
- **Also on the TV:** ADR-036's keyboard (← from q to p, ↑ from the top row round to Done, ↓ back); stream info's labels touched their values at a TV's 1.2× (fixed, e3e3d53); the probe's own faults (pipefail, the process sort, the rate; 30fe9ff, 6b5feca).

## ADR-036 · 2026-10-18 · Accepted, built (the user: "about the keyboard we made, I need the specs if it same as the system keyboard, like when I go to the last key to the left and then I click one more left it will take me to the right side as a circle and so on") — The remote keyboard's arrows go round
**Decided (the best experience, decided here rather than asked):** `RemoteKeyboard`'s arrows move to the next key that way, as before, and **at the keyboard's edge go round**, as Google TV's own keyboard does:
- ← on a row's first key goes to that row's last key, → on its last key to its first (every row: digits, letters, the address row, Arabic's rows, the language/ABC/#+=/Space row, Delete/Clear/Done).
- ↑ from the top row goes to the bottom row, ↓ from the bottom row to the top row, on the key nearest across (Delete, three keys wide, is over 2; Done over 8–9). With a password's Show key, ↑ reaches it first.
- **A held arrow stops at the edge** (Android repeats a held key); the next press goes round. A hold to "the end of the row" never spins past it.
- **Search keeps → at the right edge for its results** (`RemoteKeyboard.exits`): it is the only way to them, as on YouTube's TV keyboard. ←, ↑ and ↓ go round there too. The dialog's keyboard (sources, onboarding, a grid's filter) goes round on all four edges.
- Going round clears Flutter's memory of the last vertical move (it retraced ↑ when ↓ came next, jumping back to before the edge).
- **Found while testing it:** OK on عربي/English by remote left the focus on another key (Arabic has a row more, and the unkeyed rows shifted: the focus landed on أ, so a second OK typed it). The rows are keyed now; the language key keeps the focus.
- Tests: `remote_keyboard_test.dart` (the round on every edge, the hold, an edge left to the screen, the language key; each seen failing first); Search's 28 (6 fail without `exits`).


## ADR-037 · 2026-10-18 · Accepted (the user: "continue the work, and also make sure of the new features are stable and working well as they should be to merge them to the main and make a new stable build with the new features", "bedroom tv is free to use") — `dev` into `main`: the review, its fixes, the TV's checks, the stable build
**The review.** A read-only agent reviewed `main..dev` (lib, android, the patched media_kit_video, `tv_apk.sh`; 2,900 lines). No crash, and no path that leaves the display at a film's rate (Back, Home, a channel, an error, a killed process, a second film mid-switch: each checked end to end). Six findings, each read in the code before it was fixed; the fixes' tests were seen failing first:
- **The layer kept the TV awake while a film was paused** (`keepScreenOn` from show to hide; media_kit's `Video` lets go while paused). Now `keepAwake` follows mpv's `pause`. On the TV: `KEEP_SCREEN_ON` on while playing, off paused, on again.
- **A film's bars were the window's #0A0C10, not black** (nothing paints outside the layer's rect). A black backdrop now shows under the layer with it. On the TV, Picture size 4:3: the bars (0,0,0), the picture's 1440 px still the layer.
- **The first film after launch could show a frame or two stretched** (the layer's box came only once the film played). The window's size places it until then. On the TV: `rect 0,0 1920x1080` 0.1 s before the first frame.
- **Match frame rate, three narrow ones:** a film whose length came late played its sound under "Preparing…" for up to a second (now held from its first frame until the length comes; a film whose length is known is as before); a film left during that wait could be switched for by the next film's length (now checked first); a file ending during the switch waited for the 8 s stall check (now lost at once, and reconnected).

**Found on the TV (Beta 268–274, the fake panel only), fixed:**
- **Subtitles: the menu ticked Off while mpv showed the file's own pick, and still Off after one was picked; Off then left the last line on screen for good.** Also on the desktop, since Phase 3: media_kit's `track` stays "auto" (it never reads mpv's `aid`/`sid`) and the engine set `sid` on mpv directly; mpv says nothing more once its subtitles are off. The S and A keys could only reach the first track. The engine now follows mpv's own `aid`/`sid` and picks subtitles through media_kit (which clears the line). An integration test on real mpv (two subtitle files: the pick ticked, German replaces English, Off clears it).
- **↓ in the rail threw on a title's page opened straight into a destination not shown yet** (a film played to its end from Home goes to its page under Movies; also Home's or Search's jump to a title): go_router builds that destination's screen beneath the page without laying it out, and Flutter's directional search measures every control in the shell's scope ("RenderBox was not laid out"; the toast "Something went wrong", the focus stayed). Already on `main`. **Decided:** a destination's screen is out of the arrows' search (`descendantsAreTraversable`) until it has been on top once; still focusable. Taking every covered screen out (the first try, Beta 274) let the TV's focus keeper find a title's ‹ Back before Play's autofocus (OK on a series' poster landed on ‹ Series): caught on the TV, a test for it added, the fix narrowed.
- **Stream info: A/V sync** (decided, for the user's question "do sound and picture drift apart after the switch"): mpv's `avsync`, signed, to the millisecond; no row without it. On the TV after the switch to 24 Hz: **0 ms for a minute, no frame dropped after the switch's 5–6**; after the switch to 23.976 with the 4K HDR10 film, 0 ms. mpv's own sync can't see a delay the TV adds itself after a mode change: that stays the user's ears.

**Checked on the Bedroom TV (the TV on, 4K 59.94):** subtitles on the layer (Flutter's `SubtitleView` over it, the same style as the texture's: English, German, Off); the next-episode card over the layer ("Next episode · 30" … "· 11", then E2 by itself, on the layer); a film's end on the layer (its page, Watched, the layer hidden, the display back to 59.94 two seconds later); 24 fps → 24 Hz and 23.976 → 23.976 Hz in 0.6 s; the 4K HDR10 film out as HDR10 (`HDR10-GAMMA_ST2084`) at 23.976 and back to SDR after; a 25 fps episode left at 59.94; a file under 15 minutes left as it is. **Not checked here:** whether the Roku shows its HDR badge (the Chromecast's HDMI says HDR10), and the sound by ear.

**Then:** the full suite under `TZ=UTC` on `dev` (5,295 passed, 21 skipped), the walks under Xvfb, `dev` fast-forwarded into `main`, tagged `stable-<build>`, `tools/tv_apk.sh stable` installed as Bijou over build 244 (its data and sign-in kept). Left on Bijou: Left from a title's Play goes up to ‹ Back before the rail (the geometry; on `main` before too).

**Addendum, the same evening: the stable Bijou 275 with the user's provider** (the user: "It's idle now u can test by using the real provider"; one stream at a time, xvip.pro, about 15 minutes; no error in the app's log throughout):
- **Stranglehold** (MKV, 1280×528 H.264 23.976, AAC 2.0, ~1.2 Mb/s), resumed: first frame in 3.9 s on the layer, the display to 23.976 Hz in 0.6 s, **A/V sync 0 ms over a minute**, 3 frames dropped at the switch and none after; the bars black; no subtitle tracks.
- **The user's old case (ADR-027): a channel for a minute, then the film.** beIN SP 1 (1080p60 H.264, hardware, on the texture, the display left at 59.94), then Stranglehold from Continue watching: "Preparing…" on black with the spinner (no channel picture), then the film at its place (30:03) and on; no "Reconnecting"; 23.976 Hz in 0.5 s. **Passes.**
- **Kim Possible S1 E2** (MKV, 1280×720 H.264, ~2.4 Mb/s): the file gives mpv **no frame rate** (`container-fps` and `estimated-vf-fps` empty on the direct output), so the display stayed at 59.94 ("the film's frame rate isn't known"), as designed. The next-episode card counted 46 … 4 and **E3 started by itself** on the layer.
- **F1 4K** (MKV, 3840×1600 H.264 23.976, **HDR10 PQ BT.2020**, ~8.4 Mb/s): 23.976 Hz in 0.6 s, the Chromecast's HDMI out HDR10 (`HDR10-GAMMA_ST2084`), A/V sync 0 ms, 1 frame dropped; back to 59.94 SDR after. Its page's badge says HD (the provider's listing), not 4K.
- **Seen, not fixed (the list):** the first-frame log line reads the decoder too early for a channel ("(no, null)"; stream info says mediacodec); Home's rows keep the focus at the same place when they re-sort after a play, so Back lands on another card than the one played; a file with no frame rate isn't matched (measuring it from the frames would be an improvement); a title's quality badge follows the provider's listing.

## ADR-038 · 2026-10-18 · Accepted, on `dev` (seen on the Bedroom TV: subtitles small from a sofa; decided, not asked, as the user's standing rule says) — Subtitle size: Small / Medium / Large, a TV's Medium larger
**Found:** media_kit_video's `SubtitleView` draws 32 px for a 1920×1080 box, scaled by √(box area / 1920×1080). A TV lays the app out on a 1280×720 canvas (1.5× on its 1080p window), so its subtitles are about 32 px of 1,080 lines: 3 % of the height.

**Decided:**
- **Settings → Playback → Subtitle size**, on a TV and the desktop: **Small · Medium · Large**, Medium by default, after Preferred subtitles. JSON `subtitleSize` (`small`, `medium`, `large`); anything else, or nothing (settings stored before it), reads as Medium.
- **Medium on the desktop is media_kit's own** (the same configuration object: nothing changes for the PC by default). **Medium on a TV is 1.4×** (about 45 px of a 1080-line screen, 4.2 % of its height, where TV apps draw them). **Small is 0.8× and Large 1.3× of the platform's Medium** (a TV's 1.12× and 1.82×). The numbers are `AppPlayerTokens.subtitleTvMedium`, `subtitleSmall`, `subtitleLarge`, and `subtitleScale(size, tv:)` (hard rule 9).
- **It shows at once, on what is playing** (the only Playback row that does): the picture is built again with the new size; nothing is opened again.
- **Only the font's size changes.** Where subtitles sit, their colours, the box behind them, and their growing with the picture's box stay media_kit's. Settings → Appearance's text size doesn't touch them (media_kit sets its own text scaler), as before.

**How (hard rule 6):** `PlayerSurface` watches the setting and the form factor and passes `subtitleScale` to `PlayerEngine.videoView`. The media_kit engine turns it into a `SubtitleViewConfiguration` (`subtitle_style.dart`: media_kit's style with the font times the scale; one kept per scale, since a configuration has no equality and `Video` rebuilds its subtitles for a new one) for the TV's layer (`_LayerPicture`'s `SubtitleView`) and every `Video` (`subtitleViewConfiguration`: the desktop's texture, the TV's texture for channels). `Video` takes a changed configuration a frame later (its `didUpdateWidget`). The fake engine's view records the scale (`FakeVideoView`); the unavailable engine ignores it. The patched media_kit_video is unchanged.

**Tests (each seen failing first):** the JSON (round trip, Medium for bad values); the row (desktop and TV, by click, Tab and Enter, and by the remote's arrows and OK); the mapping, platform × size → the font (25.6 / 32 / 41.6 px on the desktop, 35.84 / 44.8 / 58.24 on a TV, against media_kit's 32) with everything else media_kit's; one configuration per scale; the wiring through the fake engine (a change reaches what plays without a new open; a TV's Medium; the stored size from the first picture). Settings → Playback's golden re-recorded (the new row). **Not checked:** on the TV, and the real engine's view in a test (it needs libmpv): only the mapping and the wiring.

## ADR-039 · 2026-10-18 · Accepted (found on the Bedroom TV with the user's provider, ADR-037's addendum; decided by the session, the user's standing rule) — Back on Home, the focus is on what was played
**Seen:** Continue watching and Recently watched channels are newest first. Stranglehold played from the third Continue card, then Back: the row had re-sorted (Stranglehold first) and the focus was on the third place, now Cars Toon. The same with a channel played from the fourth tile.

**Why:** a row's cards were keyed by their place (`ValueKey(index)`), each holding its own focus node, so the node the page gave the focus back to was the third place's, whatever it showed now.

**Decision (TV and desktop alike: `HomeRow` is shared):**
- **A card is its item** (`HomeRow.ids`: a channel's or a title's source and key; a series' Continue card is the series', whichever episode it continues; a library file its hash; an id repeated is told apart by its place). The cards are keyed by it and the rail finds a moved card by its key (`HorizontalRail.findItemIndexCallback`), so the card's state, its focus node, goes with the item: back from playing, the focus is on what was played, wherever it is now (normally first). Every Home row.
- **The rail scrolls with that card** (`HomeRowController.follow`): the card the keyboard is on, or comes back to, keeps its place on screen as the row re-sorts, set before the rail's layout. A card is built only near the view: the seventh card played, moved first under a rail still scrolled to the seventh place, was dropped with the focus. ADR-029's reveal then shows it whole, its heading with it.
- **Gone from the row** (watched into its credits: out of Continue watching): the row's first card takes the focus, not the card focused before it (Flutter's choice, maybe in another row). The row gone with it (the last of Continue watching): Home's first card.
- **Unchanged:** every other way into Home (a destination's jump, launch, the rail) and ADR-029's landings, reveal and rings. Home's providers rest while the player covers it (Riverpod pauses a screen not shown), so the rows change once Home shows again, with the focus already back on the card.

**Checked by:** `home_screen_test` "back on Home, …" (8 tests, the real app on the fake player: the third Continue card on a TV and on the desktop; the seventh, far along the row; a channel from the fourth tile; a film watched — saved as it plays, and as it is left — out of the row, and the last of the row): all eight fail on `dev`, the seventh also without `follow`, the four watched without the hand-off to the first card. `media_test`: a keyed card keeps its state and focus when the rail re-sorts (fails without the callback). Not yet seen on the TV.
- **Something new first in its row** (played elsewhere while Home was away; found by `vod_walk`: the focus on the movie's card, Ctrl+4 to Series and an episode, Ctrl+1 back: the series first, the focus followed the movie, now second): **the focus goes to the first card**, the newest, not the card it had. Only the held card coming first itself (played from further along) follows it. When the page gives the focus back after the rows change, the move waits for a card of the row to take it, for 2 s at most (`HomeRowController.toFirst`).


## ADR-040 · 2026-10-18 · Accepted, on `dev` (the user: "When I brows between pages I have to click to move to another page. Will it be easier to make it without clicking like when I move it will take me to the page and that's it?"; the rest found on the Bedroom TV with the user's provider, ADR-037's addendum) — The TV's side menu opens on a rest; Left on a title's page; the launch waits for Home
**Decided (the user's standing rule: UX decided, not asked):**
- **The side menu opens a page when the focus rests on it** (a TV only; Google TV's own menu does so). `NavRail.openAfter` 300 ms after the focus lands on an item, its destination opens as OK opens it, the focus staying on the item; Right goes into the page; OK as before. Each move restarts the wait, so quick presses and a held arrow (repeats every ~50 ms) open only where the focus stops. A rest on the destination shown changes nothing (OK on it goes to its root; a rest must not: the focus passes Movies on its way up from a film's page). On a TV the rail's focus lands on the shown screen's item after a jump (after Back to Home it stayed on the item left, and a rest there opened it again). **Live TV takes no connection when passed or rested on:** it plays only when a channel row has the focus (the preview's 350 ms), never on open. The desktop is unchanged.
- **Left from a title's Play goes to the menu** (a TV, a title's and the library's pages): Left moves within the page only to a control beside the focused one (their heights overlapping), else to the rail; before, Flutter's search took the nearest control to the left, ‹ Back far above the row, and a second Left reached the menu. Up still reaches ‹ Back. Not app-wide: Live TV's lower channel rows reach the categories by the old rule.
- **The launch's work waits for Home's rows** (`LaunchMark.settled`, at most 15 s for a launch elsewhere), then 2 s as before: a sync due at launch began 2 s after the first frame, before Home had read its rows, and those reads waited behind its 5,000-row batches on the one database connection (Home 19.9 s after main() after an update, build 231). To measure on the TV with a sync due.
- **What decodes a channel is read once mpv names it:** on a TV's texture the output, and so the decoder, come up just after the first frame; the log said "(no, null)" and Diagnostics' decoder list would have said software for MediaCodec. The note waits up to 1.5 s (none named: software), the log line waits for the output, beside the first frame.

**Addendum, the same night: on the Bedroom TV (Bijou Beta 285, 286; the fake panel's large catalogue, 50,000 channels, 30,004 films, 3,000 series).**
- **The launch with a sync due: Home on screen 1,266 ms after main()**, the sync begun 2 s after it (it took 79 s). Before (build 231, the user's smaller catalogue): 19.9 s.
- **The side menu:** a rest on Live TV opened it with the focus kept on the menu and **no stream** (the preview's "Pick a channel"; no player line in the log); quick presses (five Ups from Settings) opened only Home, where they stopped; Settings opened on a rest; remote presses work as the tests' keys do.
- **Found there and fixed (9b5429d): OK on a Home poster opened its film's page, then the screen went back to Home.** The page's controls weren't there yet, the TV's focus keeper put the focus on the menu's Home (its last item), and the rest opened it over the page. **A rest now opens an item only when the focus came there by a key** (pressed or held; `HardwareKeyboard.logicalKeysPressed` at the focus change); the app's own focus moves open nothing. Seen fixed on 286: the page stays (1 s, 3.5 s), Play focused.
- **Left from a film's Play reached the menu in one press** (ADR-040). **Back on Home: the film played from the third card came back first, with the focus** (ADR-039). **Subtitles at Medium on the TV are ~1.3–1.4× the old width** (497 px for 26 characters, against 412 px for 28; ADR-038).
- **Seen, not fixed (the list):** during a big sync a screen's lists wait behind the sync's batches (Live TV's 50,000 channels: ~10 s of placeholders); the user's stable Home showed the same channel name twice in Recently watched channels (the user: "No duplicate on the recent tv channels on Home Screen").

## ADR-041 · 2026-10-19 · Accepted in part, on `dev` and two branches (the user: "anything will work slowly then add a nice loading screen for it so the user wont get board, and about the subtitle make sure the default is off", "faisal.jpeg I want it to be the cover image of the app on the PC and the tv from outside only not inside the app, also make sure a version of it fits perfectly the tv app cercular design", "how to install the stable version of it to the PC linux and windows", "will mac be an option or not? and the mobile ios/andriod too"; the TV not available this session; decided by the session where the user's "decide UX" rule applies) — Subtitles off unless asked for; screens read beside a sync; the cover outside the app; loading stages; one tile per channel name on Home; installing
**Subtitles start off** (4f5ea75). Found: with Preferred subtitles empty, mpv still showed a file's default-flagged track; on the Linux desktop's mpv 0.34 the first subtitle file beside a video showed whatever was asked (measured: `--slang=` empty picked `film.en.srt`; 0.34 gives such a file no language); media_kit sets a newer mpv's `subs-fallback=yes` (any track when none matches) and the TV's mpv (v0.36.0-549) also has `subs-match-os-language`; and a track picked in one file stayed mpv's `sid`/`aid` for the next (reproduced on real mpv: German's number carried into the next file).
- **Decided:** subtitles start only in a language Settings → Playback → Preferred subtitles names; none named (the default) → off, whatever the file marks or has beside it. Each open sets `aid=auto`, `sid=no` (none asked) or `auto`, `subs-fallback=no`, `subs-match-os-language=no` (0.34 lacks both; harmless). Once the file plays the engine checks mpv's pick (`subtitleChoice`: the first language asked that a track has; mpv's own pick kept when it is in that language, as its default/forced flags chose), reading a beside-file's language from its name (`subtitleBeside`) and matching codes as the cast planner does ("en" = "eng"). Casting starts the same way (a file beside the video no longer shows with none asked). The user may still pick any track while watching.
- CI's red run on `main` (09fa004) failed this test with mpv's pick read as "no"; never reproduced here (with or without video); the test now waits for the settled pick, which the engine makes itself.

**Screens read beside a sync** (0af2049): drift's read pool, 2 readers on their own isolates (WAL), writes and transactions on the database's isolate (which the sync connects to); `foreign_keys` set on every connection. Measured during the large catalogue's sync (50,000 channels), a page of channels every 100 ms: one connection — median 10 ms, 95th 277 ms, longest 333 ms; the read pool — median 9 ms, 95th 12 ms, longest 12 ms; the sync 7.4 s → 6.8 s. Not yet measured on the TV (the ~10 s of placeholders, ADR-040).

**Recently watched channels: each name once** (the Home fix, cherry-picked from an agent's branch). History can't hold one channel twice (unique key, one upsert); the provider lists one channel as several streams (FHD/HD, one per category) and a tile shows the cleaned name without the quality, so two streams were two identical tiles. The row is one query: history joined to visible channels, the newest watch per `LOWER(shown name)`, the limit after. A name the user gives one tells it apart.

**The cover outside the app** (294164d): the photo is kept in `design/brand/faisal.jpeg`; `tools/brand/make_icons.py` draws every outside icon from it: Linux's hicolor PNGs (round; the jewel's scalable SVG removed, it would win; `install_desktop_entry.sh` clears old icons first), Windows' `.ico` (round), Android's round icon and adaptive icon (the head inside the 66 dp safe circle of the 108 dp layer, the photo carried to its edges, feathered where it runs out) and the 320 × 180 banner (the photo edge to edge, hair to chin). **Bijou Beta's carry an amber BETA pill** (manifest placeholders `appIcon`, `appRoundIcon`, `appBanner`), so the two circles differ on the TV. **Inside, the jewel stays:** the app's mark, and Android 12's launch splash (`values-v31`, `values-night-v31`: the jewel on the accent's circle; Android would show the launcher icon there). Not built into an APK yet, not seen on the TV.

**Loading stages** (on `wip/loading-stage`, reviewed, not merged): `LoadingStage` for waits long by nature — the jewel with a breathing glow and an orbiting arc (still with reduce motion), the title, a live line ("12,340 of 20,000 channels"), a bar, a tip every 7 s (desktop tips may name keys; TV tips only the remote). Live TV, Movies/Series, Home and Search show it during a sync that fills them; onboarding's sync shows the tip. Short waits keep their skeletons (docs/05 principle 5). The audit's new "a first sync" scenes found the top bar's sync ring turning with reduce motion: an `AppSpinner` now. **The player's opening screen** (on `wip/player-opening`, unfinished): a film's artwork dimmed with its title and "Preparing…", a channel's logo and name after 0.6 s, "Still connecting…" at 4 s, "Your provider is slow to answer. Still trying…" at 12 s.

**Installing** (docs/install.md): Linux — `tools/install_stable_linux.sh` builds `main` (or a tag) in its own worktree (`~/.cache/bijou/stable-src`) and installs to `~/.local/share/bijou/app` with the entry and icons; Windows — CI's `iptv-player-windows-x64` artifact (no FFmpeg: no casting) or a build on the PC; pushing — `git push origin --all && git push origin --tags`. Mac, Android phones and iOS: answered there (Mac: possible, needs a Mac and signing; Android phones: closest, needs touch layouts; iOS: most work, casting's FFmpeg process isn't allowed there).

## ADR-042 · 2026-10-19 · Accepted (the user: "sometimes when I open a movie or something like that, the screen goes dark for like one or two seconds, and then it will work proerply and it does the same when I leave the movie so is there a way to fix that too?"; decided by the session, the user's "decide UX" rule) — Match frame rate is Off by default
**The dark moment is the TV changing its rate.** ADR-035 §2 made a film at 23.976 or 24 fps (most films) switch the Chromecast's output from 59.94 Hz to the film's rate as it opens, and back to 59.94 two seconds after the player closes. Each switch is an HDMI mode change: the Roku TV re-locks to the new signal and shows nothing for a second or two. "Sometimes" fits: films at 25, 29.97 or 50 fps (and every channel) never switch. Nothing in the app can shorten the TV's re-lock, and this TV has no seamless switch (every mode is its own group, ADR-032).

**Decided: Off by default** (Settings → Playback → Match frame rate, TV only, kept for whoever prefers it). The project's order is stable → fast → beautiful: a dark screen twice per film reads as a fault to the person watching, while the 3:2 cadence of a 24 fps film at 59.94 Hz is a slight unevenness in slow pans that most viewers don't see. It also takes the switch's open question (sound and picture together after it) out of the default path. The row's line still says what On costs.
- **Stored under a new key, `matchRate`** (`on`/`off`, missing = Off). The old `matchFps` was written "on" for everyone who saved any playback setting while On was the default, so it can't tell a choice from the default; it is no longer read, and the next save drops it. Someone who had chosen On chooses it again.
- **Not changed:** with On, everything is as ADR-035 §2 (it follows the TV's own Match content frame rate). **HDR** is the TV's own switch (Settings → Display & Sound → Match content dynamic range, on since ADR-035's addendum): an HDR10/HLG film or channel still darkens the screen once when it starts and once when it ends, as in every app on this TV; turning that setting off would send everything as Dolby Vision again (how it was before).

**Seen on the Bedroom TV (Beta 297, the fake panel's picture tests):** the 1080p 23.976 fps film opened and closed with the display at 3840×2160 59.94 Hz throughout (`dumpsys display`'s active mode unchanged, the HDMI output SDR): no switch, so no dark moment either way. Bijou's own black between screens: none in a screen recording through Back (screencap can't see the picture's own layer, which shows as dark grey).

## ADR-043 · 2026-10-19 · Accepted (the user: "make ur focus is the stability of the system … make sure all the features make sure to be cleared and tested proeprly and moved to the main"; the review of `main..dev` by a read-only agent, each finding read in the code before it was fixed) — What the review of `main..dev` found, and the TV's onboarding
**Fixed, each test shown failing first:**
1. **The log's checkpoint ran on a reader** (since the read pool, 0af2049): drift 2.35 sends a select without `RETURNING` to the read pool, so `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` (the launch's log trim, the compaction) ran there; beside a write it gave up at once, or held the log's lock while a sync batch, a channel's history or a guide import failed with "database is locked". It runs in `exclusively()` now (the writer, queued behind the writes), and **every connection waits up to 5 s for a lock** (`busy_timeout`), which also covers a second copy of the app on the same file.
2. **Subtitles in a language with a region** (`es-419`, `pt-BR`, `en-US`: the TV's mpv names tracks so and matches `slang` by language) were turned off by the settle step, which compared whole codes. `sameLanguage` compares the primary subtag (casting's choices too); its table knows more codes.
3. **In place of mpv's pick, a whole track before a forced one** (track-list's `forced`).
4. **A sync took Home's focus**: since e289e4b a new first item in a row sent the focus to the first card, right after a play but also while the user browsed Recently added during a launch's sync. A row that has had the focus for 2 s is settled: its card keeps the focus and follows to its new place.
**Found on the TV during a 50,000-channel first sync (Beta 297):** onboarding's sync rows ran past the footer and nothing there takes the focus to scroll them, so "Saving series" had no Series row in view and the new tip never showed. The list follows the stage running (the tip in view when there's room, the running row's top never scrolled away) and the overall bar stays above it.
**Not changed (on the list):** a subtitle picked by the user before a resumed file's first frame is overridden by the settle step (low: needs real mpv timing to test); the desktop's stable and dev builds share one data folder: a lock is now waited for, but a future schema bump on `dev` would leave the installed stable without its database until `main` has it (decide before the next schema change: a separate folder for dev runs); one OK on the TV's Pick categories was dropped once ~30 s after the sync ended (pressed again, it worked; not reproduced in a second onboarding).
